verilog-a-sim — release log
===========================

Versioning, from 0.9.0 onward:

  * A feature, or anything that changes behaviour, bumps the patch:
    0.9.0 -> 0.9.1 -> 0.9.2 -> ...
  * A pure fix, or docs/process with no behaviour change, appends a build digit:
    0.9.8 -> 0.9.8+1 -> 0.9.8+2. Spelled `+N` and not `0.9.8.1` because Cargo versions
    are semver and a fourth numeric component is rejected outright (verified: `cargo
    metadata` refuses 0.9.7.1 and accepts 0.9.7+1).
  * Each bump gets a dated entry below with a sentence or two saying what changed.
  * 1.0.0 was reached on 2026-09-17, when the last "Road to 1.0" item (the list at the
    bottom, kept as the record of what it took) closed. From 1.0 on the same shape applies
    one level up: a feature bumps the minor, a fix the patch.
  * 2.0.0 was reached on 2026-09-27, when the last "Road to 2.0" item closed (the list at the
    bottom, above Road to 1.0's). The same shape continues: feature -> minor, fix -> patch.
  * The version lives in the workspace Cargo.toml (`[workspace.package] version`);
    every crate inherits it with `version.workspace = true`. Bump both together —
    the number in this file and the one in Cargo.toml must always agree.

Newest first.


2.39.0 - 2026-10-15
--------------------
**Integer typing: `7 / 2` is 3.** The front end keeps what is declared `integer` and applies
Verilog-A's two integer conversions (LRM 4.2.1), written into the IR as `int(...)` and
`round(...)`, so no IR type or interface change was needed.
  * A division of two integers truncates toward zero: `7 / 2` is 3, `-7 / 2` is -3. A real
    operand keeps it real (`7 / 2.0`, `x / 2`, `7 / 2k` are 3.5). Also in parameter defaults,
    where the division happens before the store: `parameter real s = 7 / 2` is 3.
  * A value stored into an integer rounds half away from zero: `i = 3.7` stores 4,
    `arr[1] = -2.5` stores -3, `parameter integer r = 2.5` is 3, an `integer` function's
    return and its `integer` arguments on entry (`half(9.6)` sees 10).
  * Integer is: a literal written as an integer (`2`, the parser's record from 2.36.0), an
    `integer` variable, array element, parameter or function (module, block and function
    scope, innermost declaration first), a genvar, `$rtoi`/`$random`/`$clog2`/`integer(...)`,
    a comparison, logical, bitwise or shift result, and `+ - * / %`, unary minus and `?:` over
    integers.
  * `W0302` (2.36.0), which warned about integer division, is retired: it would now be wrong.
    Its catalogue entry stays (codes are permanent) and says so; the corpus file for it is
    removed (the corpus is 47 files).

**This changes results** for models that divide two integers or store reals into integers.
Measured on everything shipped: instrumenting the build to report each conversion it inserted,
across the 84 `.va` files only two sites were touched, neither changing a value —
`gray_scott.va`'s `int(N / 2)` (already explicit since 2.36.0) and `rc_logger.va`'s
`level = floor(...)` (already whole). 25 decks run against 2.38.0 gave byte-identical output.

Limits: `**` is treated as real; `+ - *` do not wrap at 32 bits on overflow; a deck value for an
`integer` parameter (`n=2.5` on the instance line) is not rounded, because the IR's parameters
carry no type.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 1021 passed, 0 failed (one test rewritten from the warning to
the values, one added: `an_integer_function_argument_rounds_on_entry`); `cargo xtask validate`
28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28; clippy and fmt clean.


2.38.0 - 2026-10-15
--------------------
**Every bitwise and shift operator works on Verilog's 32-bit `integer`.** 2.37.0 did it for
`>>`; this does `<<`, `&`, `|`, `^`, `^~`, `~` and `>>>`. Operands are truncated toward zero and
wrapped into 32 bits two's complement, as storing into an `integer` does, and the signed result
comes back. They worked on 64 bits.
  * `1 << 31` is -2147483648 (was 2147483648), `1 << 32` is 0 (was 2^32), `~0` is -1 (same),
    `4294967295 & 255` is 255, `2147483648 | 0` is -2147483648, `(1 << 30) ^ ~0` is
    -1073741825.
  * A `<<` count of 32 or more, or a negative one (unsigned in Verilog), gives 0, as `>>` has
    since 2.37.0; the 64-bit shift took the count modulo 64.
  * `>>>` wraps its operand the same way and fills with the sign for a count outside 0-31
    (`-8 >>> 40` is -1, `8 >>> -1` is 0). Its parser rewrite is now
    `floor((a << 0) / 2 ** (b < 0 || b > 31 ? 32 : int(b)))`, `a << 0` being the 32-bit wrap.
  * One `to_i32` conversion in each evaluator (`va_codegen::ad` at run time,
    `va_frontend::elaborate` for parameter defaults), each pointing at its twin.

**This changes results** only where an operand or a result leaves the 32-bit range, or a count
leaves 0-31. The one use in the shipped models, `rc_logger.va`'s `mcd | 1` on a file
descriptor, stays inside it.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 1020 passed, 0 failed (1019 + 1,
`bitwise_and_shift_operators_are_32_bit`, checking run time and parameter defaults);
`cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.37.0 - 2026-10-15
--------------------
**`>>` shifts Verilog's 32-bit `integer`.** A logical shift of the 32-bit two's-complement
pattern, read back as signed: `-8 >> 1` is `32'h7FFF_FFFC` = 2147483644, as in every Verilog
simulator. It was a 64-bit shift, giving 2^63 - 4 (9223372036854775804).
  * A count of 32 or more shifts every bit out (0); a negative count, which Verilog treats as an
    unsigned and so huge one, gives 0 too. The 64-bit shift took the count modulo 64, so
    `1 >> 64` gave 1.
  * The operand is truncated toward zero and wrapped into 32 bits, as storing it in an
    `integer` would.
  * Same rule in both places `>>` is evaluated: at run time (`va_codegen::ad`) and when a
    parameter default is folded (`va_frontend::elaborate`), one small helper in each.

**This changes results** for a negative left operand or a count outside 0-31; for a
non-negative operand below 2^31 and a count in range nothing changes. No `.va` file in
`models/`, `external/` or `docs/examples/` uses `>>`.

Limits (closed in 2.38.0): `<<` and `&`/`|`/`^`/`~` still work on 64 bits. They agree with Verilog while operands
and results stay in the 32-bit range; `1 << 31` gives 2147483648 here and -2147483648 in
Verilog, and `1 << 40` gives 2^40 here and 0 in Verilog.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 1019 passed, 0 failed (1018 + 1,
`logical_right_shift_is_32_bit`); `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence
28/28.


2.36.0 - 2026-10-15
--------------------
**Integer division warns (`W0302`); `<<<`, `>>>`, `idtmod`, `$rtoi` and `$itor` are implemented.**
  * `W0302`, at the line, for each division of two integer-typed operands. Verilog-A truncates
    such a quotient (7 / 2 = 3, -7 / 2 = -3, LRM 4.2.1); this simulator still divides in real
    arithmetic (3.5), because the front end keeps no integer type. The message gives both
    explicit spellings: `int(a / b)` or `$rtoi(a / b)` for the LRM's quotient (not `round`,
    which gives 4), `a / 2.0` or `$itor(a) / b` for a real one. A division inside `int(...)` or
    `$rtoi(...)` is not reported. Integer operands are literals written as integers (`2`, not
    `2.0` or `2k`; the parser now records which, from the source text), `integer` variables and
    parameters, genvars, `$rtoi`, and arithmetic on those. Conservative: a name a block or an
    analog function declares again never counts. Across the 84 shipped `.va` files it fired
    twice, both in our own `gray_scott.va` (`N / 2` with `parameter integer N`), now written
    `int(N / 2)`: identical for the default even `N`, the LRM's answer for an odd one.
  * `$rtoi(x)` truncates toward zero and `$itor(x)` is the identity on this simulator's `f64`
    values (LRM 9.12), in expressions and parameter defaults. They were `E0343` (unknown).
  * `<<<` is `<<`; `a >>> b` fills with the sign bit (`-8 >>> 1` is -4), written by the parser
    as `floor(int(a) / 2 ** int(b))` so it needs no IR operator and folds in a parameter
    default. Exact for `|a| < 2^53`. Both were parse errors.
  * `idtmod(expr, ic, modulus, offset)` (LRM 4.5.4.1): `idt(expr, ic)` folded into
    `[offset, offset + modulus)`, lowered onto the same accumulator, so it shares 2.34.0's `ic`
    handling and `W0401` (whose message now names `idtmod` too; rev 2). The output's jump at a
    wrap is a discontinuity in a node's potential, which the step controller rejected at every
    step size (a bare `floor($abstime*1e6)` underflows the same way): each predicted wrap is now
    reported as a breakpoint, at 99.9% of the predicted distance and re-predicted at every
    accepted point, so the step that crosses it starts at one. Checked on a 1 V input (3 wraps
    in 2.6 us, every interior point on the folded ramp to 1e-6), a 1 MHz +- 0.5 sine (5 wraps
    in 5 us), a constant -2 MHz (10 wraps, downward) and a sign-changing sine.
  * `idt(x, ic, assert[, abstol])`: any third argument was an internal error (`E0001`). A literal
    `0` reset (never resets) is now accepted and `abstol` ignored; a reset that can fire is
    refused (`R0411`), because holding the output while keeping the integral's history needs
    per-call state the accumulator does not have. The refusal gives a latch-and-subtract
    workaround for a reset edge.

Limits: integer typing itself is still open (the warning reports, it does not truncate); it
needs no frozen-interface change after all (`docs/proposals/openvaf-shared-defects.md` §3 is
corrected). `idtmod`'s accumulator is not wrapped, so it grows without bound; `abstol` is not
applied. `>>` stays a 64-bit logical shift, where Verilog's integers are 32-bit, so `-8 >> 1`
differs from other simulators (a pre-existing gap, now recorded).

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 1018 passed, 0 failed (1012 + 6 in
`va-cli/tests/integers_shifts_idtmod.rs`; the error corpus is 48 files);
`cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.35.0 - 2026-10-15
--------------------
**`transition(x, delay, ...)` is a transport delay: a pulse shorter than the delay comes out
delayed instead of vanishing. `W0402` says so.** Item 2 of
`docs/proposals/openvaf-shared-defects.md`, the defect OpenVAF-Reloaded PRs #44/#46 fixed in their
own `transition`.
  * Cause: a site held one pending change, and a second change arriving within the delay
    overwrote it. A 0.5 ns pulse through a 2 ns delay produced nothing; a train of 0.3 ns pulses
    every 0.6 ns produced nothing (0 of 4 pulses by 14 ns on 2.34.0, 4 of 4 now).
  * Fix: each site queues up to 8 pending `(start, value)` changes; each starts in order, from
    wherever the ramp before it had got, so an earlier ramp also keeps running during a later
    change's delay (it used to freeze). The next pending start is reported as a breakpoint, so a
    step cannot stride over a pulse waiting in the delay. A `transition` site's state grows from
    6 to 24 slots.
  * `W0402`, once per module with a delayed `transition` (a non-zero literal, or a parameter
    whose default is not zero): every change passes `delay` later, a pulse too. This is unlike
    Verilog's inertial `#d`, which swallows short pulses. The message says how to filter short
    pulses yourself if that is what was meant.
  * Zero delay is untouched: the `transition_cross` ramp deck gives the same 1350 points,
    value for value, as on 2.34.0.

Limits: more than 8 changes inside one delay — the newest replaces the latest pending one (the
old behaviour, now only past that depth). The plan proposed a refusal there; the IR has no
run-time refusal, so `W0402` states the limit instead. Ramp corners are still not breakpoints
(the stated approximation); a delayed ramp's *start* now is.

Workaround on 2.34.x and earlier: keep consecutive input changes at least `delay` apart, or move
the delay into the stimulus (a `PULSE`'s own delay) when the input comes from the deck.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 1012 passed, 0 failed (1008 + 4 in
`va-cli/tests/transition_delay.rs`); `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence
28/28.


2.34.0 - 2026-10-15
--------------------
**`idt(x, ic)` applies its initial condition (LRM 4.5.4), and warns that it does (`W0401`).**
Item 1 of `docs/proposals/openvaf-shared-defects.md`, the highest-impact defect we share with
OpenVAF-Reloaded. `ic` was parsed and ignored: an integrator with no feedback loop around it
made every operating point singular (`E0602`), and a `UIC` run integrated from 0.
  * Static analyses (`.op`, `.dc`, the operating point before a transient): the integrator's
    row is `output = ic` instead of `input = 0`. The output is `ic`, and a bias-dependent `ic`
    keeps its Jacobian coupling.
  * Transient: integrates from `ic` whether the run starts from that operating point or from
    `UIC`; the seed reports `ic` as the integrator's charge history. A varying `ic` sets only
    the start (tested: `ic` stepping from 0.2 V to 0.9 V mid-run leaves the output at 0.2 V).
  * AC and noise: unchanged, `1/(jω)` with or without `ic` (tested: a first-order loop
    `idt(w(x - y), ic)` is 0.7071 at `ω = w`).
  * Without `ic` nothing changes: the static row still drives the input to zero, which is what
    lets a feedback loop settle the integrator at the operating point.
  * `W0401`, once per module that writes `idt(x, ic)`: the output is held at `ic` in static
    analyses, so a feedback loop around the integrator is **open** there. The message says when
    to keep `ic` (a known start, a VCO's phase) and when to drop it (the operating point should
    settle the loop). Printed by `sim` and `check`.

What it unlocked: `benchmark/models/psp103/psp103_nqs.va` (`PSPNQS103VA`, nine `idt(x, ic)`
calls in its NQS splines) was singular at every operating point, with NQS off or on, so it never
ran. On `psp103_inverter_tran.net` it now runs: with NQS off it gives exactly `PSP103VA`'s
waveform (1740 points, largest difference 0); with `SWNQS=1` it runs, from the same operating
point. That NQS waveform has no reference to compare against yet.

Limits: under `UIC` the printed `t = 0` row is still the zero vector, as for every unknown `UIC`
does not set; the first step after it is right. `idtmod` remains unimplemented (`E0342`).

Workaround on 2.33.x and earlier: `V(out) <+ ic + idt(x);` with `.tran ... UIC`; no workaround
reaches the operating point.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 1008 passed, 0 failed (1003 + 5 in
`va-cli/tests/idt_initial_condition.rs`; one codegen unit test rewritten from "`ic` is
ignored" to the new stamp); `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.33.0 - 2026-10-15
--------------------
**Five silent wrong answers become errors, and four errors that named the wrong construct now
name the right one.** Batch A of `docs/proposals/openvaf-shared-defects.md` (items 3-7 and its
section 3): defects this simulator shares with OpenVAF-Reloaded. Each message says what to write
instead; the workaround is repeated here for anyone still on 2.32.x.
  * `@(foo)`, `@(x)`, `@(posedge x)`: not analog events, so the trigger was skipped and its
    statement ran at **every** evaluation. Now `E0239`.
    Workaround: write `@(cross(sig - level, +1))`, `@(above(...))`, `@(timer(...))` or
    `@(initial_step)`; or drop the `@(...)` if every evaluation is what was meant.
  * A device-line value outside the parameter's declared `from`/`exclude` range (`X1 a 0 rr
    r=-5` against `from (0:inf)`) ran with that value. Now `E073A`, before anything is solved.
    The range is checked with inclusive ends (the IR keeps no open/closed flag), so
    `from (0:inf)` still admits exactly 0.
    Workaround, and the way to catch exactly-0 too: in the model,
    `@(initial_step) if (!(r > 0)) $error("r = %g is outside (0:inf)", r);` (the run then ends
    with `E0739`).
  * A runtime array or vector index outside its declared range read (or wrote) the last element.
    Now a `$error` naming the array, the index and the range, from a check elaboration emits
    before the statement; the access still uses the last element so the solve goes on, and the
    run ends with `E0739`. Not checked inside a `?:` arm, the right side of `&&`/`||`, or a loop
    header, where a check placed before the statement would run when the access does not
    (`i <= 3 ? g[i] : 0` must stay quiet at `i = 7`).
    Workaround: `if (i >= lo && i <= hi)` around the access.
  * `absdelay`: a negative delay became a phase advance in AC, and `max_delay` was dropped. A
    delay known when the model is compiled must now lie in `[0, max_delay]` (`E0396`); one that
    varies, or comes from the deck, is clamped into it (LRM 4.5.9).
    Workaround: `absdelay(x, min(max(td, 0), tmax))`.
  * A surplus argument to `transition` (more than 5), `slew` (3) or `absdelay` (3) was accepted
    and ignored. Now `E0334`. Workaround: remove it.
  * Wrong-construct diagnostics: `string s;` said "no discipline `string`" and is now refused as
    an unimplemented string variable (`R0204`; write the text in the `$strobe` itself);
    `break;`/`continue;` said "expected `=`" and are now `E023A` (fold the exit into the loop
    condition); `h = g;` on arrays said "`h` never declared" and is now `E0397` (assign element
    by element); `paramset` said "expected `module`" and is now `E023B` (set values on the
    instance line).

Seven new codes: `E0239`, `E023A`, `E023B`, `E0396`, `E0397`, `E073A`, `R0204`, each with a
catalogue entry and an error-corpus file (the corpus is 46 files). None of the 84 `.va` files
in `models/`, `external/` and `docs/examples/` trips any of them.

**This changes results** only for models that hit one of the above, which now stop with a
message instead of running. Six elaboration tests asserting the IR shape of runtime indexing
and `absdelay` were updated for the guard blocks and the delay clamp.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 1003 passed, 0 failed (997 + 6 in
`va-cli/tests/silent_wrong_answers.rs`); `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed,
convergence 28/28.


2.32.1+1 - 2026-10-15
----------------------
**A plan for the defects we share with OpenVAF-Reloaded:
`docs/proposals/openvaf-shared-defects.md`.** Docs only, no behaviour change.
  * Fourteen items ranked by impact (silent wrong answer above loud failure, common construct
    above typo), each with whether a user can avoid it today by writing the model or deck
    differently, and what fixing it takes.
  * Top three: `idt(x, ic)` drops `ic` (operating point singular; only `UIC` plus `ic + idt(x)`
    avoids it), a delayed `transition` drops a pending change (a pulse shorter than the delay
    vanishes; no in-model fix while `absdelay` is refused in transient), and an invalid event
    trigger such as `@(foo)` runs its body every evaluation.
  * Four have exact workarounds (parameter ranges via an `$error` guard, array index guards,
    `absdelay` clamping in AC, `<<<`/`>>>` rewrites). Two are LRM-permitted choices, not defects
    (`cross` without `time_tol`, interrupted `transition` slope); one, `paramset`, is outside the
    language.
  * Also listed: integer division is real division (a documented limitation; `int(i / 2)` is the
    workaround), and four diagnostics that name the wrong construct.
  * Every behaviour the plan quotes was re-checked against 2.32.1.

Measured: unchanged from 2.32.1 (no code change).


2.32.1 - 2026-10-15
--------------------
**Six defects of our own, found while checking the OpenVAF-Reloaded PRs against this simulator.**
None was one of their bugs; each turned up in the reproducers written for theirs.
  * `@(final_step)` fired at a transient's `t = 0`. The operating point that seeds a run (no
    `UIC`) was solved with the standalone-`.op` context, where `final_step` is true, and printed
    as the first point. `NewtonConfig::transient_seed` (default `false`) solves it with
    `final_step` false; `va-cli` sets it for that one solve. `.op` and `.dc` points are their own
    last step and are unchanged.
  * `zi_*` filters started from zero histories, so the output fell from the operating point's
    `H(1)·u` to the first impulse-response sample and climbed back (2, 1, 1.5, 1.75, ... for
    `zi_nd(u, {1}, {1, -0.5}, T)`, `u = 1`). They now start in the operating point's steady state:
    past inputs `u`, past outputs `H(1)·u`. A filter with a pole at `z = 1` has no steady state
    and keeps zero histories.
  * `hypot` and `atan2` had a `0/0 = NaN` derivative at the origin, where a zero start puts the
    first Newton iterate when their arguments scale a node voltage: the solve stopped at once
    (`E0604`). The subgradient `0` is used there, as `abs` does at its kink, in the
    interpreter, the scheduled evaluator and the JIT.
  * A periodic `PULSE` source could skip an edge: the next-corner search looked only at the
    current cycle, so from just past a cycle's last corner it returned the cycle after next.
    It now searches the neighbouring cycles too.
  * `SIN`'s trailing delay, damping and phase were dropped, so a delayed sine started at once.
    They are parsed (SPICE units: seconds, 1/s, degrees) and the DC value is the waveform's value
    at `t = 0`, `offset + amplitude·sin(phase)`.
  * A refused event list now names the term that cannot be scheduled (`cross(...) or
    absdelta(...)` names `absdelta`, not `cross`), with `R0202` when that term is a step event.

**This changes results** for decks using any of the six. One test changed: the one-pole and
conjugate-pair `zi` checks in `zi_filters_follow_their_difference_equations_in_transient` fed a
`DC 1` input and expected a step response, which only the zero histories produced. Their decks
now step the input at 0.1 µs, so the run starts from 0 and the expected values are unchanged.

Checked and **not** a defect: `@(above(...))` in a `.dc` sweep fires once, at the crossing, not at
every point past the threshold. That is the LRM's rule and the v1.2.3 design.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 997 passed, 0 failed (992 + 5 new); `cargo xtask validate` 28
checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.32.0+3 - 2026-10-15
----------------------
(Committed on a branch as 2.24.2+3; renumbered on merging after 2.32.0.)
**scripts/benchmark/bench_c7552_tran_linux.sh: run the c7552 transient on your own Linux
machine.** Script and docs only. It runs c7552_tran.net with default settings and with
`--bypass` from a source checkout and writes `c7552_tran.txt`: wall time, peak memory, exit,
timepoint count and the `check_iscas.py tran` check, for comparison with ngspice/OpenVAF's 20 min
on an i7-9750H. It reports only the 3 719 checked nodes and caps each run at 3 h (`CAP=`). Why
a script rather than a figure: the cloud run was lost to a container restart and then stopped,
so there is still no completed c7552 transient. Smoke-tested here with `CAP=20` on 4 vCPUs:
both runs stopped at the cap after 20.1 s, at 1 829 MB (default) and 1 453 MB (bypass).

2.32.0+2 - 2026-10-15
----------------------
(Committed on a branch as 2.24.2+2; renumbered on merging after 2.32.0.)
**circuits/benchmark/iscas85/c7552_tran.net: a c7552 transient deck, the size of the
ngspice/OpenVAF c7552 transient.** Deck only, no code change. c7552.net with each of the 207
inputs a PULSE (100 ps edges, period 2*(i%8+1) ns, edges staggered by (i%8)*100 ps) and
`.tran 10p 10n`, checked with `check_iscas.py tran c7552.bench`. Derived from c7552.net rather
than regenerated with gen_iscas.py because gen_iscas.py needs external/benchmarkExt/, which is
not in every checkout. The cost: the staggered edges make the step control accept far more than
the 1 000 print steps (~2 500 points after 2 h of a first, interrupted run), so this deck runs
for hours. Leave out the default report: it keeps all 268 676 unknowns, ~2.15 MB per point, and
heads for the memory limit. Pass `--report` with the checked nodes from `check_iscas.py report`
instead. Timings are not in this entry; they follow when a run completes.

2.32.0+1 - 2026-10-14
----------------------
**The ISCAS'85 suite at 2.24.2: every operating point correct, c7552 in 295 s; bypass is the
setting that helps, the JIT and node collapse do not.** Docs only:
circuits/benchmark/iscas85/README.md ("The suite at 2.24.2"). Release build, Xeon 2.8 GHz,
4 vCPUs, one deck at a time on 4 threads. (Committed on a branch as 2.24.2+1 and renumbered
when merged after main's 2.25.0-2.32.0; the figures are 2.24.2's, not re-measured at 2.32.0.)

  * `.op`, default settings, all PASS against their `.bench` logic: c432 9.1 s, c499 12.1,
    c880 13.1, c1355 15.9, c1908 26.4, c2670 53.0, c3540 128.7, c5315 180.6, c6288 100.6,
    c7552 295.3 s (6.95 GB peak). Close to 2.4.0+1's figures from another 8-thread machine.
  * Transients: c17_tran 39.7 s, 23.3 s with `--bypass` (both PASS, 13 524 points);
    c432_tran 330.4 s, 230.5 s with `--bypass` (same 2 532 points, no node moved more than
    1 uV). It is a timing deck with no logic check.
  * `VA_JIT=on` is a net loss here: one serial ~0.8 s compile per distinct PSP103 parameter
    set (6-15 per deck), for no measurable evaluation gain (c880 12.0 -> 20.6 s).
  * `VA_COLLAPSE=on` helps up to c1355, then breaks down: c1908 27.1 s / 1.2 GB -> 65.2 s /
    7.2 GB, c2670 to 8.0 GB, c3540 killed for memory at 13.6 GB. The collapsed matrix factors
    with far more fill (not measured). Its 2.21.0+1 check never ran these decks. Keep it off
    above ~10 k unknowns until that is understood.
  * No OpenVAF/ngspice ISCAS timings are in the repository (they came with
    `external/benchmarkExt/`, not in this checkout), so the side-by-side comparison is not
    made here.

Measured: the runs above, with the 2.24.2 release binary. No code change; 2.24.2's test and
`validate` figures stand.


2.32.0 - 2026-10-14
--------------------
**A `cross` firing just before a `transition` ramp ends no longer kills the transient run.** The
defect OpenVAF-Reloaded PR #48 fixed in its own simulator (its blocker 3), found here by running
that PR's PLL scenario: a ramp ending at 0.50001 V through a `cross` at 0.5 V, the crossing
20 fs before the ramp's end, died of a timestep underflow (`E0605`) at a 1 ns `.tran` step.
How close was fatal scaled with the minimum step: 0.2 ps at a 10 ns step, 2 fs at 0.1 ns.
  * Cause: `transition` bounded each step to an eighth of what **remained** of its ramp, so each
    step covered 1/8 of the rest and the steps shrank geometrically toward every ramp's end,
    down to the minimum timestep. A crossing inside that tail made the step passing it fail
    its error test (the `cross` body's jump) at every size the controller still allowed.
  * Fix: an eighth of the **whole** ramp, so a ramp is sampled at even steps, as a linear ramp
    should be. The ramp's duration is a sixth state slot of each `transition` site (was five),
    set when a new ramp latches. A model's `state_len` grows by one per `transition` call.
  * Fewer timepoints as a side effect: the ramp deck at a 10 ns step goes from 2052 to 1350
    points, an `if`/`case` model of a pulse from 899 to 822; no extra cost anywhere measured.
  * New test `transition_cross.rs` runs the binary on four ramp levels (mid-ramp, 20 fs and 0.2 ps
    before the end, exactly at the threshold); it fails with `E0605` without the fix.

PR #48's other three blockers were checked and do not reproduce here: a self-rescheduling
`@(timer(next_edge))` fires on time (10 edges in 10.5 µs, at exactly 1, 2, … µs), a 0.1 µs
running-sum timer gives 200 edges with no step collapse, and `transition` in a `case` `default`
arm compiles and switches correctly.

Limits: no golden circuit uses `transition`, so `cargo xtask validate` does not cover this change;
the transient unit tests that exercise `transition` all pass. The ramp's corners are still not
breakpoints (the stated approximation, now rounded by at most an eighth of the ramp).

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 992 passed, 0 failed (991 + 1); `cargo xtask validate` 28
checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.31.0 - 2026-10-14
--------------------
**Coded diagnostics, step 3d: `va-cli`'s own errors. Every error a user can reach now has a code.**
36 new `E07xx` codes (`E0704`-`E0739`) for the command line, files, plots and the deck/model
wiring `va-cli` does itself: unreadable files and directories, a missing analysis card, an
analysis the deck asks for but the command line does not, `K`/`F`/`H` couplings, `.dc`/`.noise`
cards naming the wrong device, model parameters a deck cannot set, port-count mismatches, plots
that cannot be drawn, `$error` counts.
  * A sweep of every model directory, the error corpus, the LRM examples and a dozen failing
    command lines shows 44 distinct codes and no `E0000`. `E0000` stays as the fallback for an
    error added later without a code; `render_error`'s unit tests now check it, since no binary
    path reaches it any more.
  * Two places flattened a codegen error to text (`anyhow!("{e}")`), so under `sim` a codegen
    refusal reached the user as an uncoded sentence instead of its refusal block. They now keep
    the error's type.
  * Options with a bad value (`--reltol x`, `--threads 0`, `--integration foo`, `--define`)
    use `E0703`, the code step 1 gave invalid option values.
  * Errors only a library caller can cause (`va_cli::load` skipped, a sweep source whose row
    count changes on rebuild) are `E0001`, internal.
  * The "while:" lines that say what `va-cli` was doing (`parsing deck.net`, `DC
    operating-point solve failed`) are still in the program's own words; they are context, not
    diagnostics, and are not translated yet.

Not coded, by choice: `va-harness`'s `HarnessError`. It is printed only by `cargo xtask
validate`, a developer tool, and its `Run` variant already carries the pipeline's coded text as
a string. Coding it would take a stage of its own (`08xx`).

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 991 passed, 0 failed (989 + 2: the E0000 test moved from
the binary to `render_error`, and a second checks a coded error under context);
`cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.30.0 - 2026-10-14
--------------------
**Coded diagnostics, step 3c: numerical errors.** A failed solve is shown under an `E06xx` code:
`E0601` Newton did not converge, `E0602` singular matrix, `E0603`/`E0604` a non-finite value or
derivative, `E0605` time step underflow, `E0606` an unimplemented integration method, `E0607` a
model's `$fatal` in transient and `E0608` in a static solve.
  * The numerical crates are unchanged: `va-core` may depend on `va-abi` alone (CLAUDE.md §2),
    and their errors are already typed, with exactly the fields the catalogue needs. The new
    `va_cli::numerical_diagnostic` maps each variant to its code at the edge, the same way
    whether a `CoreError` arrives bare or inside a transient or AC/noise error. A caller's misuse
    of their APIs (`RecordOutOfRange`, `InvalidOutput`) is `E0001`.
  * A non-finite value names the unknown, as before, but now in the coded message:
    "error[E0603]: a model produced a non-finite value (NaN or inf) in the equation of `V(a)`
    (unknown #0)", where 2.29.0 printed the core's sentence plus an uncoded context line.
  * `va-cli`'s error rendering prefers a diagnostic attached as `anyhow` context over one
    found in the chain, since `va-cli` attaches one only to say something more specific.
  * Tests: `every_numerical_error_has_a_code` (every variant), and
    `a_numerical_failure_names_its_code_and_unknown` (the binary, on `ln(V)` at 0 V).

Limits: `E0606`'s `{method}` and `E0608`'s `{point}` are filled with the program's own words
(`Gear`, `the operating point`), not yet translatable. Command-line, file, plot and harness
errors are still `E0000`: step 3d.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 989 passed, 0 failed (987 + 2); `cargo xtask validate` 28
checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.29.0 - 2026-10-14
--------------------
**Omitted arguments and comma-separated events, the LRM syntax OpenVAF-Reloaded PR #35 added.**
Of the seven `docs/examples/LRM/` cases, five valid ones were rejected; all five are now
accepted, and the invalid one is rejected with a message that says why.
  * **Null arguments** (LRM A.6.4/A.6.5): an argument may be left out where the grammar allows
    it, `cross(e, dir, , , en)`, `timer(0, p, , en)`, `laplace_nd(x, , den)`. Allowed:
    `cross`'s direction and two tolerances, `above`'s two tolerances, `timer`'s period and
    tolerance, a Laplace filter's numerator, denominator and tolerance, a Z-domain filter's
    numerator, denominator and transition time. The parser keeps the position as the new
    `ExprAst::Null`; elaboration reads it as "not given". An omitted filter list means unity:
    no roots, or the polynomial `1`.
  * **Anywhere else** an omitted argument is the new `E0238`, "argument 4 of `timer` may not be
    omitted", instead of `E0207` "expected an expression, found `)`". This covers every call,
    `exp(, 1)` included. System tasks keep their own null-argument rule (`$strobe("a",,"b")`).
  * **A comma between events** means `or` (LRM 5.10.1), and compiles to identical IR. It was
    refused (`R0201`) with a message saying only an `or` list is honoured.
  * Codegen accepts an empty root list (`laplace_zp(x, , poles)`, `zi_zd(x, , den, T)`): the
    empty product. An empty coefficient list stays malformed IR.
  * `or`'s row in `docs/token-reference.md` said it had no grammar production; it has been the
    event separator since 2.16.0. Corrected, and the comma and null-argument rules added.

Kept refused, on purpose: `@(initial_step("tran") or final_step("tran"))` (`R0202`). It parses;
what it should mean (the body only at the first step of the named analysis) is a semantic
question, and running it at every analysis's first step would run it where the model excluded it.

Tests: `null_arguments.rs` builds each omitted-list filter beside the same filter with `{1}`
written and asserts identical stamps (residual, Jacobian, charge, dcharge) at a bias point,
for seven Laplace and Z-domain forms; it was checked to fail on a mismatched pair. Three
frontend tests cover the event arguments, `E0238` and the comma. New corpus file
`parse_required_argument_omitted.va` (39 files).

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 987 passed, 0 failed (983 + 4); `cargo xtask validate` 28
checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28; `check --codegen` models 68/68, benchmark/models 25/25,
external 8/9 (unchanged).


2.28.0+2 - 2026-10-14
----------------------
**The LRM examples move to `docs/examples/LRM/`**, beside the project's other examples; 2.28.0+1
put them in a new top-level `examples/`, which the project does not use. Same seven files, same
verdicts (rechecked from the new path). No code changed.


2.28.0+1 - 2026-10-14
----------------------
**New `examples/LRM/`: LRM examples, starting with the seven from OpenVAF-Reloaded PR #35.**
That PR closed parser gaps in OpenVAF for the event and filter syntax of LRM A.6.4/A.6.5; run
through this simulator, the same examples show the same gaps, recorded in
`examples/LRM/README.md`:
  * omitted (null) arguments, `cross(e, dir, , , en)`, `timer(0, p, , en)`,
    `laplace_nd(x, , den)`, are a syntax error (`E0207`) although the LRM allows them;
  * a comma between events, `@(initial_step, cross(...))`, is refused (`R0201`) although it
    means the same as `or`, which is accepted;
  * `@(initial_step("tran") or final_step("tran"))` is refused (`R0202`), deliberately;
  * `timer(0, 1e-6, 1e-9, )`, invalid, is rejected, but by the generic `E0207` rather than a
    message naming the argument that may not be omitted.
No code changed: the examples record the gaps, they do not close them.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` not rerun (no code change since 2.28.0: 983 passed, 0 failed).


2.28.0 - 2026-10-14
--------------------
**Coded diagnostics, step 3b: the netlist.** Every error `va-netlist` raises carries an
`E05xx` code (33: element syntax, values, `.subckt`/`.include`/`.model` structure, subcircuit
flattening) and the three things a deck can say that are recognised but not applied are coded
warnings: `W0501` a malformed `.nodeset` entry, `W0502` an ngspice `.control` block, `W0503`
`.options`.
  * `NetlistError::Parse` carries `diag: Diagnostic` instead of `message: String`, located at
    `deck.net:12` for a line of the deck itself and at `line 12 (in `models.inc`, instance `x1`)`
    for a line from an included file or a flattened subcircuit. `Netlist::notes` is
    `Vec<Diagnostic>`; `va-cli` prints them as `warning[W0503] at deck.net:4: ...`.
  * An unreadable deck is `E0501`, where 2.27.0 showed `E0000`: the `diagnostics` test that used
    it as its example of an uncoded error now uses an unreadable model file, still `E0000`.
  * A device's parameter-override errors keep the include/instance origin, which they lost
    if raised inside `parse_param_overrides` (the origin was appended by the caller).
  * New test `a_netlist_error_and_warning_carry_their_codes`. `va-netlist` depends on `va-diag`.

Limits: `NetlistError::UnknownModel` is never raised and has no code. The netlist parser
names unsupported elements (`unsupported element`) as errors, not refusals: refusals are for
Verilog-A constructs.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 983 passed, 0 failed (982 + the new test);
`cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.27.0 - 2026-10-14
--------------------
**Coded diagnostics, step 3a: code generation.** Every error `va-codegen` raises now carries
a code, and its catalogue text: 10 refusals `R0401`-`R0410` (a second time derivative, a filter or
noise source buried inside an expression, a loop past its iteration cap, a laplace filter with a
pole at s = 0 or an improper one, ...) and 13 model errors `E0401`-`E0413` (a contribution or
`bound_step` inside an analog function, a zero denominator, non-conjugate roots, a zi_* advance,
a variable read before it is assigned, ...).
  * `CodegenError` changes shape: `Unsupported(Diagnostic)` is a refusal, the new
    `Invalid(Diagnostic)` is a model error, `Internal(Diagnostic)` is `E0001`. Before, all three
    kinds were `Unsupported(String)`, so `va-cli` reported a zero laplace denominator as a
    refusal; now the model is said to be wrong, which it is. `CodegenError::diagnostic()` reaches
    the code; `va-cli` renders a codegen refusal from its catalogue entry (what, why, instead).
  * 31 messages that only a malformed IR can produce (a missing state slot, a filter call with
    the wrong argument layout the frontend never emits) became `E0001` internal errors.
  * A variable read before it is assigned was an "internal error: variable #0 read before
    assignment", reachable from ordinary source (`real x; ... <+ x * V(p, n);`). It is `E0413` and
    names the variable.
  * `ad::expand_roots` and `ad::zi_realization` return a `Diagnostic` instead of a `String`.
  * Two new corpus files, `cgen_variable_read_before_assignment.va` and
    `refused_laplace_pole_at_zero.va`: the corpus is 38 files, all coded.
  * `va-codegen` now depends on `va-diag` (CLAUDE.md §3 and the thesis map updated).

Limits: a codegen diagnostic has no source location, because the IR carries none.
`TerminalCount`, a caller's misuse of the API, has no code. Netlist, numerical and command-line
errors are still `E0000`: steps 3b-3d.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 982 passed, 0 failed (the two corpus files are inside the one
corpus test); `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28;
`check --codegen` over models 68/68, benchmark/models 25/25, external 8/9 (unchanged).


2.26.1 - 2026-10-14
--------------------
**A code's number within its stage is hexadecimal: `E0399`, then `E039A` to `E03FF`.** The
code stays a letter and four characters; the first two are still the decimal stage, and the last
two may now use upper-case `A`-`F`, which gives each stage and kind 256 codes instead of 100.
Every existing code is unchanged, since a decimal number is also a hex number, and sorted as text
the codes stay in numeric order. This answers 2.26.0's open question (elaboration had 4 codes
left): it now has 106, and no stage needs an overflow range or a fifth digit.
  * `va_diag::is_well_formed` accepts the hex form; lower case is still rejected in a catalogue
    file, while `va-cli --explain e039a` upper-cases its argument as before.
  * The merged argument-count codes (`E0333`-`E0335`) stay merged. Hex removes the reason they
    were merged, but one code per kind of mistake, naming the signature, is how rustc does it,
    and splitting them back would only add near-identical catalogue entries to translate.
  * `codes.rs`, `messages/README.md` and CLAUDE.md §5 describe the format.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 982 passed, 0 failed (the format test gained hex cases inside
an existing test); `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.26.0 - 2026-10-14
--------------------
**Coded diagnostics, step 2: every diagnostic the frontend raises has a code.** The
preprocessor, lexer, parser and elaboration now raise `va_diag::Diagnostic`s, not sentences:
179 new codes, each with an English entry in `messages/en/` (message, what it means, what to do).
A user who mistypes a net name now reads

    error[E0366] at r.va:8:10: unknown net `q`
      = for what this means and what to do: va-cli --explain E0366
      while: elaborating module `r`

where 2.25.0 printed `error[E0000]: elaborating module `r`` with the real problem as a cause.
  * Codes by stage: 27 preprocessor (`E01xx`), 37 parser (`E02xx`), 95 elaboration (`E03xx`),
    `E0001` for an internal error. Refusals `R0101`, `R0201`-`R0203`, `R0301`-`R0310`: a coded
    refusal's what, why and instead come from the catalogue, so they are translatable too, and
    its marker reads `refused[R0202]:`. Warnings: `W0101`-`W0104` for the four directives with no
    Verilog-A effect, `W0301` for a port without a direction.
  * `FrontendError`'s variants carry a `Diagnostic` instead of a `String`;
    `FrontendError::diagnostic()` gets at it, and `va-cli` renders it with what it was doing
    as `while:` context. `Preprocessed::warnings`, `CompiledDesign::warnings` and
    `port_direction_warnings` are `Vec<Diagnostic>`. A refusal is boxed in its variant
    (`FrontendError::refused(r)`), because the code pushed `Refusal` past clippy's size limit
    for an error type.
  * The error corpus asserts each file's code with a new `code:` header. All 36 files carry
    one and none is `E0000`; the header line moved every `at:` down by one.
  * A placeholder name must now start with a letter or `_`, so `{1, tau}` in a message is text.

Design calls, and their cost:
  * **Elaboration did not fit in 100 codes.** One code per message needed 104 in `E03xx`. The
    two dozen argument-count messages are now three codes (`E0333` exact, `E0334` range, `E0335` at
    least), as rustc does with E0061, each naming the signature: `` `cross(expr, dir, time_tol,
    expr_tol, enable)` takes 1 to 5 arguments, but this call passes 6 ``. Nine near-duplicates
    were merged too (unknown net in a probe or a connection, slice errors, ...). That leaves
    `E0396`-`E0399`: **four free elaboration codes.** The next elaboration diagnostics need a
    decision: an overflow range (`08xx` is unassigned) or five digits.
  * Wording moved from the message into the entry's description and actions, so a few
    messages are shorter. Five corpus `message:` lines changed with them: `` `exp` takes exactly
    one argument `` is now `` `exp` takes 1 argument(s) ``, and an analog function's arity error
    no longer says "analog function". Tests that checked the moved text now check the code.
  * Some placeholders hold English words the program supplies (`{via}`, `{construct}`, `{what}`,
    `{list}`): they are not translated yet. `messages/README.md` says so.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 982 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked,
0 failed, convergence 28/28; `check models` 68/68, `check benchmark/models` 25/25,
`check external` 8/9 (unchanged; its failure is now `E0101`, an undefined macro). The local
280-file corpus was not run here. Codegen, netlist and numerical errors are still `E0000`: step 3.


2.25.0 - 2026-10-14
--------------------
**Coded diagnostics, step 1: the code system, the message catalogue and `va-cli --explain`.**
Every user-facing diagnostic is to carry a code: `E` error, `W` warning, `R` refusal, `C`
communication, then four digits whose first two name the stage (00 general, 01 preprocessor,
02 parser, 03 elaboration, 04 codegen, 05 netlist, 06 numerical, 07 files and command line).
`E0000` is an error with no code yet. A code is permanent: never renumbered, never reused.

The text is not in the Rust source. Each code has `messages/<lang>/<CODE>.json` with a
one-line `message` (with `{name}` placeholders the program fills), a `description` and a list of
`actions`, so another team can write and translate it; `messages/README.md` is their guide.
  * New leaf crate `va-diag`: `Code`, `Diagnostic` (an `Error` that carries a code, its arguments
    and a location), the catalogue and rendering. The English catalogue is built into the binary,
    so a copied `va-cli` still explains itself. `VA_MESSAGES_DIR` overlays edited text without a
    rebuild.
  * The language comes from `VA_LANG`, else the locale (`LANG=fr_FR.UTF-8` -> `fr`). A code
    falls back to English when the language has no text for it, and to the program's own wording
    when no language does. A translation made from an older English `rev` is used, with a note.
  * `va-cli --explain <CODE>` prints the message, what it means and what to do. Every error is now
    shown as `error[CODE]: ...` followed by the `--explain` hint.
  * Seeded and wired: `E0701` unknown subcommand, `E0702` `check` with no models, `E0703` invalid
    option or environment value (`--solver`, `VA_PARALLEL`, `VA_BTF`, `--explain` itself). Every
    other error is `E0000`, shown with its own text and causes, exactly as before.
  * `catalogue_is_consistent` checks every file against the code list: parses with exactly the
    documented fields, name equals code, English has a `rev`, every defined code has English text
    and nothing else does, translations carry `source_rev` and keep English's placeholders.
  * CLAUDE.md gains `va-diag` in the crate table and a house rule: a new diagnostic gets a code
    and an English entry in the same PR.

Limits: the words around the catalogue text ("error", "What it means", the hint) are English in
every language for now, and plurals are not modelled. Warnings, refusals and the frontend's
errors are not converted yet: that is step 2, crate by crate, with the error corpus asserting
codes. Until then almost every error a user meets is `E0000`.

Cost: one new direct dependency, `serde_json` (pure Rust), which brings `itoa` and `zmij`; serde
was already in the tree.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 981 passed, 0 failed (7 new in `va-diag`, 4 new in
`va-cli/tests/diagnostics.rs`); `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28;
`check models` 68/68, `check benchmark/models` 25/25, `check external` 8/9 (unchanged).


2.24.2 - 2026-10-11
--------------------
**Predefined macros, and `va-cli --define`.** `__VAMS_ENABLE__` and
`__VAMS_COMPACT_MODELING__` are defined (as `1`) before any source is read
(`va_frontend::preprocess::PREDEFINED`). `va-cli sim|check --define NAME[=VALUE]` predefines
more, repeatable, `NAME` alone meaning `1` (`PreprocessOptions::defines`,
`preprocess_named_with`). Every LRM directive was already handled; these were the gap.

  * Why: compact models guard the compact-modeling extensions with
    `` `ifdef __VAMS_COMPACT_MODELING__ ``. Undefined, that branch was compiled out silently.
    `external/mos11010.va` (MOS11) now compiles its correlated-noise network: 7 nodes, where
    it had 4. Its noise analysis runs with it (a 1 kOhm-loaded MOS11, 1 kHz-1 MHz).
  * Correction to what was said before the change: MOS11 does not compile in this repo
    either way. It includes `discipline.h`/`constants.h`, which are not shipped, and uses
    `P_EPS0` from the vendor's `constants.h`. The comparison above supplied
    `P_EPS0=8.854187817e-12` (now possible with `--define`) and the standard headers in a
    scratch copy.
  * No other effect: `va-cli check --codegen` over `external/` (7/7) and `models/` (68/68)
    gives line-identical reports before and after; no other model there uses the macros.
  * A name that is not an identifier is refused before any source is read. A model may still
    `` `undef `` either predefined macro. Function-like macros cannot be predefined.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 970 passed (new: the predefined macros take their
`` `ifdef `` branch, read 1 and can be undefined; caller defines are seen, replace a predefined
one and have their names checked); `cargo xtask validate` 28/28; fmt and clippy clean.

2.24.1+1 - 2026-10-11
----------------------
**Lockfile: `private-gemm-x86` 0.1.20 -> 0.1.22 (a `faer` dependency), because 0.1.20 was
yanked.** `cargo deny`'s advisories check refuses yanked crates (`deny.toml`: `yanked = "deny"`),
so CI's `cargo deny` job failed on every branch, `main` included. It only showed once the Actions
runners came back (2026-10-11; before that every job died without a runner). `cargo update -p
private-gemm-x86`, nothing else moves. No behaviour change: `deck-diff` between the two lockfiles
gives 14 of 14 decks byte-identical (dense and sparse solves, PSP103 transients, diodes, the ring
oscillator, Fig. 5, HiCUM).

Measured: `cargo deny check` fails with 0.1.20 (the CI error, reproduced) and passes with 0.1.22;
`cargo test --workspace` 968 passed; `cargo xtask validate` 28/28.

2.24.1 - 2026-10-11
--------------------
**A transient run keeps only what it reports: memory no longer grows with the circuit.**
`va_transient::integrator::run_recording` integrates as `run_with_events` does but stores only
the unknowns asked for at each timepoint (`Waveform::columns` says which, `Waveform::value`
reads by unknown index either way). `va-cli sim --tran` passes the quantities it prints, so
`--report` now bounds memory as well as output. `run`, `run_with_events` and every library
caller (va-harness, xtask, the tests) still get the whole vector.

  * Why: every accepted timepoint stored the whole solution vector, 8 B per unknown. On the
    MNIST crossbar with PSP103 (16 830 unknowns, 20 cycles) that reached ~3.5 GB a run, and
    with four runs at once one was killed for memory (2.23.0+2). Memory, not cores, limited
    parallel inference.
  * Measured (digit 1, 32 quantities reported): rung C crossbar 418 -> 40 MB peak; PSP103
    `--bypass` crossbar 590 MB peak, flat through the run (2.24.0 had reached 2.6 GB by 500 s
    of its 713 s; its peak was not measured). Both outputs byte-identical to 2.24.0's. No
    speed cost: Fig. 5 (PSP103, 1 thread, old and new alternated) 26.8 / 24.6 s -> 25.5 /
    24.5 s, same output.
  * A reported quantity with no unknown behind it (index `GROUND`, e.g. a model's own branch
    to ground) never printed and still does not; va-cli drops it from the kept set.
    `run_recording` itself refuses an out-of-range index (`TransientError::RecordOutOfRange`)
    instead of skipping it. `deck-diff` caught the first version refusing them on 22 decks.
  * Limitation: what is not reported is not kept, so it cannot be plotted or measured after
    the run; ask for it in `--report`. The rest of a run's memory (matrix, JIT, bypass cache)
    is unchanged.
  * Also recorded (circuits/snn/transistor/README.md): no further switch parameters. Rung B's
    one parameter costs ~25 % of run time; the edge effects stay unmodelled, and PSP103 is the
    working reference until the real device model ships.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 968 passed (new: recording keeps the kept unknowns'
bits and refuses an unknown out of range; va-cli keeps a ground quantity unprinted, not
refused); `cargo xtask validate` 28/28; fmt and clippy clean; `cargo xtask deck-diff` 2.24.0
against this change: 63 decks compared identical, all those sorted before
circuits/multiphysics/piezo_storage.net (the run hit a time limit there; the remaining 72 were
still running at commit time, and their result is on the PR). The 13 slow decks deck-diff skips
without --all were not run.

2.24.0 - 2026-10-10
--------------------
**Rung B: the fitted access switch gets the triode's V_DS term, and rung C's +1.6 mV bias is
explained.** `mos_switch.va` gains `kds` (default 0 = rungs A/C, byte-identical): the gate drive
loses kds * V(p, n). `fit_switch.py` fits kds = 0.5533 with vt = 0.948284 V (and refits the
charge with that vt). R_on then follows PSP103 within 0.09 % over +-20 mV, against rung A's 1.9 %.
`--switch fitbc` / `--synapses fitbc` write the decks. New: `bias_phases.py`, which splits a
fitted switch's V_m error by cycle phase, and `edge_kick.py`, a one-word-line bench for the
word-line edge kicks.

  * Why: on the MNIST crossbar, rung C's error grows +0.77 mV a neuron-cycle while the stage
    integrates with the word lines steady, the same for rung A. Its gate drive was measured from
    the source-line terminal, so the switch was too conductive on each synapse's branch above the
    source line and not enough on the one below, and both push V_m up. Rung B removes it
    (+1.8 uV a neuron-cycle left).
  * Ten digits, against the same PSP103 references: worst V_m error 5.5 -> 1.8 mV, RMS 1.2-2.2
    -> 0.6-1.0 mV, every spike (340) still agrees. The sign flips: -0.5 to -0.8 mV mean.
  * What is left (not modelled): the first ~50 ps of each word-line edge, which V_m sees because
    the edges coincide with the clock edge to the picosecond. PSP103's gate resistance (~73 Ohm),
    its lower overlap capacitance at the swing's low end (3.46 against 3.74 fF) and the cell
    node's junction capacitance account for it on a bench, but do not separate cleanly.
  * Cost: ~25 % slower than rung C on the crossbar (381-526 s against 322-376 s). The closest
    calls move as much as with rung C (up to 6.8 mV), since the edge charge decides them. Fig. 5:
    same spikes, worst V_m 0.30 mV against rung C's 0.21, now one-signed.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 964 passed (new: the V_DS term's R_on and Jacobian against
central differences); `cargo xtask validate` 28/28; fmt and clippy clean. Fig. 5 with rungs A and
C byte-identical before and after the model change.

**Rung C holds on all ten MNIST digits: every spike agrees with PSP103.** The 16-neuron hidden-2

circuits/snn/transistor/README.md ("Ten digits"). New: `scripts/benchmark/xbar_digits.sh` runs
the decks several at once, and `circuits/snn/transistor/cmp_digits.py` compares them. No

  * Spikes: 340 PSP103 spikes; rung C and rung A agree on every spike train. Rung C's V_m is
    +0.9 to +1.7 mV high on average, worst 5.5 mV; rung A +5.8 to +9.5 mV, worst 20.2 mV.
    The +1.6 mV bias rung C left on digit 0 is the same on every digit, still open.
  * The reference for digits 2 and 5-9 is PSP103 `--bypass`. On digits 1, 3 and 4, bypass
    matched the full runs: 127/127 spikes, V_m within 1 uV (print resolution).
  * Margins are now measured at the latch edge. The 0.5 ns-earlier sample reads several mV
    low, because V_m still rises ~13 mV/ns there. A margin within a few mV of V_th does not
    decide the spike, because of the comparator's ~2 mV logistic and the flip-flop's latch
    instant.
  * Throughput, 4 vCPUs: full PSP103 gains almost nothing from parallel runs (1.4 against 1.3
    images an hour). With bypass it is 9.7 an hour. Rung C gains 2.3x (18 -> 41 an hour)
    and rung A 3.2x (57 -> 180) from 1-thread runs side by side.
  * Cost found: the transient keeps every unknown at every timepoint (`Waveform::x`), ~3.5 GB
    for a 20-cycle PSP103 run. Four at once overran the ~15 GB container, and digit 2's full
    run was killed after 9 580 s. Memory, not cores, limits parallel inference. Not fixed
    here.

Measured: the runs above at this commit's simulator (2.23.0+1, unchanged). `xbar_digits.sh`
reproduced digit 9's rung C output byte for byte at 2 threads, against the 4-thread original.

2.23.0 - 2026-10-10
--------------------
**Rung C: the fitted access switch gets the gate's charge (two more parameters). Fig. 5's V_m
error falls from 2.4 to 0.21 mV, and the 16-neuron MNIST crossbar's from 14.7 to 4.6 mV worst,
with every PSP103 spike kept.** Rung A's error was one-signed and came from the word-line edges,
so the charge was the next rung, not the V_DS term.
  * models/snn/mos_switch.va: each channel terminal carries q(V) = cov*V + cch*ns*softplus((V -
    vt)/ns), with V = V_G - V_terminal: overlap plus half the channel, switching on with rung A's
    own vt and ns. `cov` and `cch` default to 0, which is rung A, byte-identical
    (fig5_fit.net's output is unchanged).
  * fit_switch.py: a quasi-static gate ramp at V_DS = 0, where each terminal current is exactly
    dQ/dt. cov = 3.742 fF, cch = 8.472 fF per terminal. PSP103 moves 18.23 fC through each
    terminal over the full swing, and the fit is within 0.29 fC everywhere. Added to
    switch_fit.json; the rung A values there are unchanged.
  * gen_fig5_access.py --switch fitc writes fig5_fitc.net. gen_mnist_tx.py / gen_xbar_deck.py
    --synapses fitc write each path as rram + charged mos_switch, keeping the cell node, because
    the drain-side charge needs somewhere to land.
  * Fig. 5: worst |V_m - PSP103| 0.21 mV, both signs (rung A: 2.4 mV, always high). The edge pulse
    in the source line is -68 / +145 uA against PSP103's -74 / +149 uA. Spikes 6, 10, 14.
  * Crossbar, 16 hidden-2 neurons, 20 cycles, digit 0, same PSP103 reference: mean / RMS / worst
    +1.6 / 1.9 / 4.6 mV (rung A +8.0 / 9.2 / 14.7). All 17 spikes at the same steps. 2 494
    unknowns (PSP103 35 262), 168 s (PSP103 2 693 s; rung A folded 52 s). The 4.7 mV margin rung
    A left at h2_5 step 16 is back to 14.7 mV (PSP103 16.1).
  * **The cost, stated plainly:** about 3x rung A's run time, because the cell nodes return. The
    cell-node kick overshoots (an HRS node falls to -0.23 V, PSP103's to +0.40 V), because there
    is no junction capacitance; it settles before the window. A +1.6 mV mean bias remains on the
    crossbar, still mostly high, with its cause not yet separated (junction capacitance, the V_DS
    term, the charge partition). The coverage is still one image, 16 of 64 neurons, 20 of 29
    cycles.
  * Test: the_fitted_switchs_gate_charge_moves_psp103s_charge_and_differentiates_correctly
    (the 18.23 fC swing, drain and source alike, no charge in rung A, charge Jacobian against
    central differences in all three terminals).

2.21.0+1 - 2026-10-08

**Node collapse (2.20.0, `VA_COLLAPSE=on`) checked over every deck in the tree: no deck's answer
moves beyond its solver tolerances.** No code change; this records the check 2.20.0 promised.

text, and every run exits 0 both ways. All 29 were rerun with both outputs kept, and every value
reported in both was compared:
  * Agree to the last printed digit: 20 decks. PSP102/103/104, BSIM6, BSIM-BULK, BSIM-CMG,
    BSIM-IMG and HiSIM2 NANDs; the PSP103 inverters; c17; both microrings; speaker_ac and
    speaker_large; ring_gyro_noise; bench_access_bias, fig5_access and uic_access.

    Every other value agrees within the time-print rounding (below), except up to 1.5 mV on
    PSP103 internal nodes at waveform corners.
  * bsimsoi NAND: V(out) moves by up to 31 uV in the transition (VA 0.56-0.70 V). Rebuilt with
    Newton at abstol 1e-18 / reltol 1e-12 (not committed), off and on agree at every printed
    digit over all 101 points, so this is Newton's tolerance, not collapse.
  * speaker_thermal: 301 163 vs 299 285 timepoints, because the step control sees fewer rows.
    Its pressures agree to <= 0.1 %, within the 1e-3 transient tolerance.
  * What "within the time-print rounding" means: va-cli prints t to 7 digits, so two printed-equal
    times can be one unit of the last digit apart. Near 11 s that is 10 us, enough to move the
    deck's own 40 V, 200 Hz source by 0.47 V. These comparisons subtract slope x that bound.
  * `VA_COLLAPSE=on cargo xtask validate`: 28/28.
  * Found on the way, NOT caused by collapse and not fixed here: at the default Newton
    tolerances, the bsimsoi NAND's transfer curve is up to 0.4 mV from the tight-tolerance answer,
    collapse on or off. The circuit carries ~1 pA there, and abstol is 1e-12 A, which is a
    current tolerance as large as the circuit's whole current.

2.21.0 - 2026-10-08
--------------------
**A behavioural access switch fitted to the SG13G2 transistor (rung A), for training: on Fig. 5 it
puts V_m within 2.4 mV of the PSP103 deck, with the same spikes, in 3.2 s instead of 20 s.**
Training cannot run at transistor level (snn-transistor-performance.md §4.6). So the transistor has
to come back into the behavioural model, with as few parameters as the circuit allows.
  * Step 1, measured over fig5_access.net: with the word line high, the access transistor sits in
    deep triode at a fixed V_SB (the source line is held at V_ref = 1.65 V), with |V_DS| <= 20 mV.
    Its edge kicks on the cell node (up to 1.15 V) settle in 12.5 ns, before the neuron
    integrates.
  * models/snn/mos_switch.va: g = beta * ns * ln(1 + exp((V_GS - vt) / ns)), I = g * V_DS. It has
    vswitch.va's port order, so a deck swaps one for the other. It is linear in V_DS, so it can be
    folded into synapse_2t2r exactly (not done yet).
  * circuits/snn/transistor/fit_switch.py fits it to PSP103 and writes switch_fit.json:
    ns = 31.8 mV (subthreshold slope); beta = 3.795e-3 A/V^2, pinned so that R_on at the high
    word line is PSP103's 376.97 Ohm exactly; vt = 0.951 V, least squares over strong inversion
    (largest current error 10 %).
  * gen_fig5_access.py --switch fit writes fig5_fit.net.
  * cmp_vm.py compares two runs where the flip-flop latches V_m, and checks the spike steps.
Fig. 5, worst |V_m - PSP103|: 118.9 mV with the old 10 Ohm switch, 2.4 mV with rung A. Spikes
6, 10, 14 in all three runs.
  * **The cost, stated plainly:** in this deck rung A is no better than vswitch at a constant
    376.97 Ohm, which agrees with it to 1 uV. The word lines are ideal rails, so the gate
    dependence never comes into play.
  * The remaining 2.4 mV is the gate's charge, not V_DS. Through the window the source-line
    current agrees to 1.8 nA (0.07 %). The difference comes at the word-line edges: PSP103 pushes
    -74 / +149 uA through the source line while clk's falling edge still has the input stage half
    open. So the next rung, if any, is C (charge), not B.
  * Not yet checked on the MNIST crossbar.
  * Limits: one geometry, the typical corner, one V_ref; no charge or noise; the reference is our
    own PSP103 run, not QSPICE.
  * Test: the_fitted_access_switch_has_psp103s_r_on_and_a_jacobian_that_matches_differences
    (R_on at the on-bias; AD against central differences in all three terminals, on both
    softplus branches).
Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 961 passed (960 + the new test); `cargo xtask validate` 28/28;
fmt and clippy clean. No existing model or deck changed.

2.20.0 - 2026-10-08
--------------------
**Node collapse, opt-in (`VA_COLLAPSE=on`): PSP103's proven ideal shorts and dead branch rows
leave the system, and one SG13G2 transistor drops from 16 own unknowns to 7.** PSP103 writes
each parasitic resistor as `if (R > 0) I(a,b) <+ V(a,b)/R; else V(a,b) <+ 0;` and so carries a
branch-current row for each of the seven, needed or not. With the SG13G2 card, S-SI and D-DI are
true shorts (`rsh = 0`), each costing a node and a row to say "the same node". The other five are
resistors whose rows are only ever pinned to zero.
  * va-codegen: `lower::collapse_plan` / `CompiledModel::collapse` proves both cases, folding
    branch conditions through variables the parameter-only setup fixes (`RSE_i`; assigned in
    the setup and never by a per-load statement, task or function output). The old prover
    (`ideal_shorts`, literals and parameters only, unchanged) recognised none of PSP103's.
    `instantiate_dropping` maps the dropped rows to ground, where every stamp is dropped.
  * va-cli: `collapse_nodes` gives nodes joined by a proven short one unknown (the deck net a
    member is wired to, or one fresh unknown). It leaves a short that would join two different
    nets as it was, and drops the shorts' rows and the dead ones.
  * Measured (Xeon 2.8 GHz): `fig5_access` 208 -> 136 unknowns, same 12 837 timepoints, outputs
    equal to its 1e-6 print precision, time unchanged (its solve is small). 16-neuron crossbar,
    `VA_JIT=on --bypass`, 4 threads: 35 262 -> 16 830 unknowns, 74.9 / 73.1 s -> 62.4 / 60.9 s
    (~1.2x), peak memory 983 -> 758 MB; same 1 375 timepoints, 1 344 at identical times, V_m
    equal to 1e-10 V there.
  * **Not bit-identical, by design, and that is why it is opt-in**: the matrix is smaller, so LU
    rounds differently and step control can land a step elsewhere. A collapsed node is no longer
    reported under its own name (`V(X1.SI)` is `V(X1.S)`). A removed row is not reported, and the
    remaining `X1.b...` are renumbered. With it off, output is byte-identical to 2.19.0
    (checked on `fig5_access` and `bench_access_bias`).
  * `bench_xbar_jit_linux.sh`: a `_col` run suffix, and `jit_bp_col` among the defaults, with
    the largest V_m difference collapse makes reported next to bypass's.
  * New tests: `the_collapse_plan_folds_setup_variables_and_finds_dead_rows` (a short through a
    setup variable, a dead row, a per-load variable left unfolded) and
    `collapse_nodes_joins_shorted_nodes_and_drops_their_rows` (a chain of two shorts into one
    net; never two nets merged).
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 960 passed, 0 failed (after merging main), with
    `VA_COLLAPSE` off and on; `cargo xtask validate` 28/28 with it on; clippy clean. A
    deck-diff of every deck with it on against off was still running when this was committed;
    its result is in the next entry.


2.19.0 - 2026-10-08
--------------------
**The bypass replay runs on several threads, bit-identical to the serial one.** With `--bypass`
on a large circuit, replaying the bypassed devices' recordings was a serial loop as long as the
device evaluation it saves (profile of the 16-neuron crossbar, 35 262 unknowns, one thread:
evaluation 36 %, replay and its bookkeeping 33 %, linear solve 17 %).
  * How the bits are kept: floating-point sums depend on order, so what is kept is the order
    in which each *entry* of the system receives its additions. The sparse system is split by
    destination (`SparseSystem::parts`: part p owns a range of residual/charge rows and a range of
    Jacobian/charge-Jacobian slots). Each recording's additions are bucketed by part (`Buckets`,
    kept with a bypassed device's recording and rebuilt only when the pattern or the partition
    changes). Each thread walks every device in instance order and adds its own part's bucket.
    Preparing the buckets, finding a fresh recording's slots and storing the new bypass entries
    also run in parallel, one device each, reusing the old entry's buffers: the first version
    allocated per device and lost ~14 % of every worker to the allocator's lock.
  * When: transient with `--bypass`, more than one thread, and at least
    `PARALLEL_MIN_REPLAY_STAMPS` (50 000) recorded stamps in the assembly (`VA_PARALLEL=always`
    forces it, `never` turns it off). An assembly with a stamp only the serial sink can take (an
    entry the pattern does not hold yet, or a side effect) goes the serial way, unchanged.
  * Measured (Xeon 2.8 GHz, 4 threads, `--bypass`, old and new binaries alternated; this
    container's machine ran about 2x slower than for 2.18.0+2's figures, so compare within a
    row only): crossbar 16 neurons, 2 cycles, `VA_JIT=on`: 86.3 / 83.8 s -> 72.8 / 74.0 s
    (1.15x); 8 neurons, 3 cycles: 65.2 / 60.5 s -> 55.2 / 55.7 s (1.13x). One thread is the
    serial path, unchanged. What is left on the main thread is mostly the linear solve (BTF + LU
    ~34 % of its samples), the next limit.
  * Identity: output hashes equal to 2.18.0+3 on the 8- and 16-neuron crossbars with
    `--bypass` (1 and 4 threads), and with `VA_PARALLEL=always --bypass` on `fig5_access`,
    `ring_osc` and `rectifier`. New test `the_parallel_replay_gives_the_serial_replays_bits`
    (8 nodes, 4 parts, within and past tolerance, a grown pattern; fails if the replay drops
    its corrections, checked).
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 955 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.18.2 - 2026-10-08
--------------------
**The ten diagnostics the error corpus flagged as poor now say what is wrong, where.** The
corpus's `poor:` list is empty; each fixed file asserts the new wording and location, so none
of these can quietly regress.
  * **Tokens are named as written.** `found Some(Hash)` → `` found `#` ``,
    `expected Semicolon` → `` expected `;` ``, `found None` → `found the end of the file`
    (`Token::describe`).
  * **A missing `;` is reported where it belongs**: after the last token of its line
    (`` expected `;` after identifier `n` ``), not at the next line's first token.
  * **An unclosed `begin` or module names its opener**:
    `` expected `end` to close the `begin` opened at f.va:9:10 ``. End-of-file errors use the
    file's own line numbering, not "preprocessed line N".
  * **`/*` and string literals left open** are reported by the preprocessor at the line they
    open on. An unclosed comment used to swallow the rest of the file and show up as a missing
    `endmodule`. An unclosed string reached the lexer, which gave a byte offset into the
    preprocessed text and quoted everything after it.
  * **Lex errors are located as file:line:col** (`lexer::lex_located`, through the
    preprocessor's line map) and quote the offending character.
  * **Preprocessor errors carry file:line** (`undefined macro` included); an error inside an
    `` `include ``d file keeps that file's location.
  * **`electical p, n;`** reads as a net under an undeclared discipline: the error says so,
    points at the word, and suggests `electrical` (edit distance <= 2 against the built-in and
    declared disciplines; no suggestion when nothing is that close).
  * **`===`/`!==` and `casex`/`casez`** are parse errors saying Annex C excludes them, and what to
    write instead. `casex` used to be a *refusal* claiming it was "not yet" parsed. Every
    parser refusal was also tracked under "Analog events", including ones that have nothing
    to do with events; the tracking is now per refusal.
  * **A genvar read outside its loop** says so, instead of "unknown identifier".

**A real bug found on the way.** The macro expander copied text one byte at a time with
`byte as char`, so every non-ASCII character outside a comment was turned into Latin-1 mojibake:
`$strobe("1 µA")` printed `1 Âµ A`, and a lex error quoting `£` showed `Â`. It now copies whole
characters. This changes the output of any model that prints non-ASCII text, and that change is
the fix.

Cost and limits:
  * A string literal that runs past the end of its line is now a preprocessor error. Verilog
    strings cannot span lines, and `\`-continued macro lines still work, but a third-party
    model that relied on a tool accepting it would now stop. Nothing in the repository does:
    `check models` 66/66, `check benchmark/models` 25/25, `check external` 8/9 (unchanged).
    **The local 280-file corpus was again not run here.**
  * Elaboration errors still carry no line number; that needs source spans in the AST and is
    the largest remaining gap in where a diagnostic points.
  * The corpus still records 2 gaps (a port without a direction; `Temp` on an `electrical`
    net), unchanged.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 957 passed, 0 failed (955 + two preprocessor unit tests; the
corpus gained `lex_unexpected_character.va`, 36 files, inside its one test); `cargo xtask validate` 28
checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.18.1 - 2026-10-08
--------------------
**An error corpus, and four kinds of invalid Verilog-A that used to compile are now rejected.**
`tests/fixtures/errors/` holds 35 models, each with one deliberate mistake and a header saying
what `va-cli check --codegen` must report: the stage, the words, the line:col where it is
right, and the exit code. `crates/va-cli/tests/error_corpus.rs` runs the real binary on each, so
what is tested is what a user reads. The format is in that directory's README.

Rejected from this version, each with a message naming what the user wrote:
  * **Wrong argument count** to a built-in (`exp(x, 2.0)`, `pow(x)`, `max(a, b, c)`, `idt` with
    five arguments) or to a user `analog function`. Before, `exp(V(p,n), 2.0)` dropped the
    second argument and simulated as `exp(V(p,n))`. One table serves both the analog-block and
    the parameter-default path, so the two cannot disagree.
  * **Undeclared variables**, in the analog block and in analog functions. Before, any
    assignment target was declared on the spot, so a misspelt `gm_eff = ...` for a declared
    `gmeff` compiled as a second variable and `gmeff` kept its old value. To make this
    possible the parser now keeps an analog function's `real`/`integer` local names (they were
    discarded). Assigning to a parameter gets its own message, not "unknown variable".
  * **Duplicate declarations** at module scope: a parameter declared twice (the second
    default silently won), a variable declared twice, and a variable sharing a parameter's
    name (it replaced the parameter in every read).
  * **A parameter whose default is not finite** (`1/0`), which surfaced only as a
    non-finite residual at the first Newton iteration.

`va-cli check` now **exits 1 when any model fails**, after printing the full report. It used to
exit 0 whatever it found, so it could not gate a script or a CI job.

Cost and limits, plainly:
  * Three frontend unit tests had undeclared variables in their inline Verilog-A and now
    declare them; nothing else in the repository relied on the leniency. The committed models
    are unchanged: `check models --codegen` 66/66, `check benchmark/models --codegen` 25/25,
    `check external --codegen` 8/9 (same failure as before). **The local third-party corpus
    (last recorded at 256/280) was not run in this session**, because it lives outside the
    repository. It is the place where a real model relying on an implicit variable or a
    duplicate parameter would show up. Any drop there is a correction (those models were
    invalid, and some were running with a wrong value), but it has to be measured, not assumed.
  * Anything that scripted `va-cli check` and expected exit 0 on a corpus with failures now
    sees exit 1. Nothing in this repository does.
  * The corpus records **2 gaps** (still accepted though invalid: a port without a direction,
    and `Temp(...)` on an `electrical` net, which then fails in `sim` as "singular matrix") and
    **10 messages worth improving** (internal token names such as `Some(Hash)` shown to the
    user, a missing `;` blamed on the next line, lexer errors located by a byte offset into the
    preprocessed text, `casex` refused as "not yet" parsed when Annex C excludes it for good).
    Elaboration errors still carry no line number. None of this is fixed here.

Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 955 passed, 0 failed (954 + the corpus test);
`cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28.


2.18.0+4 - 2026-10-08
--------------------
**`bench_xbar_jit_linux.sh` on a Ryzen 9 9950X (32 logical CPUs), at 4x the default deck.**
NEURONS=32 CYCLES=5: 4 096 PSP103, 70 366 unknowns, 5 141 timepoints. Plain 619.0 s, `VA_JIT=on`
615.4 s (1.006x), `--bypass` 257.7 s (2.40x), both 255.8 s; one thread: bypass 355.7 s, both
323.4 s (1.10x). Bypass replays 95.3 % of loads. Every check OK: the JIT is bit-identical with and
without bypass, and bypass gives the same bits on 1 and 32 threads.

What it says, against 2.18.0+2's Xeon run of the 8-neuron deck (JIT 1.49x): at 70 k unknowns the
JIT is worth nothing on many threads. Bypass alone is worth 2.4x, and going from 1 to 32 threads
under bypass gains only 1.38x. Both point at the time going to the sparse solve and per-step
overhead, not to device evaluation, which is the only thing the JIT speeds up. That is an
inference from these figures; this run has no profile to confirm it.

Two things to watch. Bypass moved V_m by up to **1.8e-5 V**, against 1.0e-14 V on the small deck.
It is within the tolerances bypass is built on, but it is nine orders of magnitude larger, and a
deck this size is where it shows. And the report says `checkout: n/a`: the run was not made from
a git checkout, so the exact source revision rests on the binary's SHA-256 (d8afd439...) alone.
No simulator change; the figures are added to the script's header.

2.18.0 - 2026-10-08
--------------------
**A compiled model's static program can run as machine code (`VA_JIT=on`, prototype, Cranelift),
and contributions stop allocating: one SG13G2 PSP103 `load()` 47 -> 41 us interpreted, 19 us
as machine code.** `docs/proposals/snn-transistor-performance.md` §4.2-4.3.

  * va-codegen, `sched/jit.rs` (new): each model's static program becomes one Cranelift
    function, every partial a load from a fixed address, the rule's flops and a store;
    contiguous partial pairs packed as `f64x2`; the both-zero rule a branch-free mask;
    transcendentals from the same `libm`, rarer operators by calling the interpreter's own
    functions. The only `unsafe` in the crate, for calling the code, the shims' pointers and
    `Send`/`Sync` (owner sign-off 2026-10-08, CLAUDE.md §5; the module doc says why each is
    sound); the crate's `forbid(unsafe_code)` became `deny`. Off unless `VA_JIT=on`.
  * va-codegen, `FastSum`: on the static path a flow contribution's terms are summed from the
    registers into a stack buffer, `Dual::scale`/`Dual::add`'s arithmetic in their order:
    gradient allocations per PSP103 load 93 -> 3. Bit-identical; helps with or without the JIT.
  * xtask `jit-check` (new): machine code against interpreter over every `.va`, bit for bit,
    any two NaNs counted as equal -- the only differences seen are NaN signs on `psp104` (LLVM
    turns the interpreter's `v * -1.0` into a sign flip; IEEE leaves a NaN's sign unspecified).
    60 of 98 models compile, all agree. `sched-check` 0 differing.
  * Measured (Xeon 2.8 GHz): `bench-instance .../bench_access_bias.net` 40.9 us interpreted,
    19.1 us `VA_JIT=on`; `fig5_access` 1 thread 32.7 -> 22.6 s with the JIT, output hashes equal.
  * Dependencies: cranelift-{codegen,frontend,jit,module,native} 0.131 (pure Rust; licences
    Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception / MIT / Apache-2.0, all on deny.toml's list).
  * Limitations: ~0.9 s of compilation per distinct model card at its first evaluation
    (`fig5_tx.net`: 19 cards); ~1 MB of code per card, never freed; host ISA only, tested on
    x86-64 Linux only (Windows and Apple-silicon macOS untested); potential contributions and
    self-probed branches still build `Dual`s. `xtask deck-diff` was still running when this was
    committed; its result is in the next entry.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 954 passed with `VA_JIT` off and on;
    clippy clean.
