2.17.0 - 2026-10-07
--------------------
**A compiled model's evaluation is 1.66x cheaper on PSP103, bit-identical, and `--bypass` no
longer goes parallel for work it never measured.** For the transistor-level SNN, where one
SG13G2 PSP103 evaluation is most of every run (`docs/proposals/snn-transistor-performance.md`,
new: where the time goes, what 2.17.0 did, and what is left, ranked).

  * va-codegen, the static program (`sched.rs`, module doc "Less to run") runs less code:
    constants are folded at compile time by the expression the instruction would evaluate (a
    setup value no per-load statement reassigns counts as a constant, and is no longer reset
    every load); an assignment whose variable reaches no contribution, condition, bound or task
    compiles to nothing (kept if it calls the tree walker, which can fail); an `if` whose arms
    hold only assignments is laid out as condition, jump and arms; consecutive such statements
    run as one stretch instead of one `exec` and one walk step each. On the static path the
    variable table is no longer allocated, cleared and dropped per load. One PSP103 `load()` with
    the SG13G2 card: ~78 -> ~47 us (`xtask bench-instance .../bench_access_bias.net`).
  * va-core, bypass (`par.rs`): in `Mode::Auto` a bypassed assembly went parallel on an estimate
    it never measured (zero after the operating point, which meant parallel), so it handed a few
    cheap leftover devices to the pool every iteration; 4 threads ran 1.9x slower than 1 on
    `fig5_access`. Each instance's own evaluation time is now measured (serially: first time, then
    every 256 assemblies) and an assembly goes parallel when its fresh instances' times reach
    `PARALLEL_MIN_WORK_NS`. A stored recording takes its sparse slots from the slot cache that has
    just placed it, and the column each stamp's drift correction reads is found once at store
    time instead of searched for on every replay. A recording starts with its last length's
    capacity. Speed only: bypass was already bit-identical across thread counts.
  * Measured (Xeon 2.8 GHz, 4 vCPU, 2.16.2+6 -> this, single runs, each build's runs back to back, not interleaved; read as +-5 %):
      fig5_access, 1 thread           51.5 -> 33.2 s     with --bypass 10.2 -> 7.6 s
      fig5_access, 4 threads          37.5 -> 30.2 s     with --bypass 19.3 -> 9.8 s
      xbar_h2_8, 4 threads            81.1 -> 55.7 s     with --bypass 37.5 -> 29.1 s
      xbar_h2_8, 1 thread, --bypass   81.5 -> 57.2 s
  * Still true: on `fig5_access` with `--bypass`, 4 threads are slower than 1 (9.8 vs 7.6
    s): the hand-off on this machine costs more than the 200 us threshold assumes (tuned on an
    i7, not re-tuned here).
  * Identity: `xtask sched-check` over the 98 `.va` files in the tree, 0 differing (60 on the
    static path); `xtask deck-diff` against 2.16.2+6: not finished when this was committed; its result is recorded in the
    next entry; stdout hashes equal on
    `fig5_access` (with and without `--bypass`, 1 and 4 threads) and `xbar_h2_8` (the
    8-neuron hidden-2 crossbar, 1 024 PSP103, plain and `--bypass`). New tests
    `the_static_path_folds_constants_and_drops_dead_assignments` and
    `an_if_of_assignments_runs_as_code_and_takes_the_arm_the_bias_selects`, each failing with
    its reduction switched off (checked).
  * Limitations, stated: the program is still interpreted (compiling it to machine code needs
    `unsafe` and owner sign-off, CLAUDE.md §5; the proposal's §4.2); liveness is
    flow-insensitive, so a variable read anywhere that matters is kept everywhere it is
    assigned; `case`, loops and `if`s with a contribution or task in an arm still go through the
    statement walk; contributions are still turned into heap-allocated `Dual`s to stamp.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 954 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.16.2+6 - 2026-10-05
---------------------
**Multiphysics example 15: a silicon microring bank -- bistability, self-pulsing and chaos**
(`models/multiphysics/ringchaos/`; `ringchaos_plots.py`; `docs/multiphysics.md` and the
tutorial). Coupled-mode rings with two-photon absorption, free carriers (5 ns) and heat (100 ns),
four state equations per ring; the bus carries the complex field from ring to ring as two nodes.
  * Bistability: a power sweep up and down against the model's own steady states and Jacobian
    eigenvalues: folds 3.445 / 0.2515 mW (sweep jumps at 3.70 / 0.20 mW, late by the thermal
    lag), a supercritical Hopf at 0.373 mW (24.7 MHz) where the pulsing band closes, and a
    subcritical one at 7.47 mW (pulsing coexists with a steady state up to ~12 mW).
  * Self-pulsing against scipy (LSODA): periods 40.500 / 40.497 ns at 0.40 mW and 96.174 /
    96.191 ns at 6 mW; extremes equal to 4 digits.
  * Two rings on one bus at 7 mW: identical rings lock (periodic; 25.062 vs scipy 25.118 ns
    between large peaks, 0.2 %); ring B with a 260 ns thermal time is chaotic: a folded return
    map, 290 distinct maxima in 50 us, a 1e-9 twin decorrelated in 10 us, and a Benettin
    Lyapunov exponent of the same equations of 1.36e6 /s over 100 us (still settling: 1.86, 1.67,
    1.46e6 at 25/50/75 us; positive throughout).
  * Found on the way, and stated in the docs: at 8 mW the same bank is only transiently chaotic
    (settles after 50-125 us), caught by a Lyapunov estimate that kept falling and a 300 us run;
    identical rings and rings coupled through the substrate lock instead of going chaotic; a
    DC operating point on the unstable steady state never pulses (the decks ramp the laser on);
    a twin's largest pointwise difference is not a chaos test (periodic twins end 2.4 ns apart).
  * Limitations: one mode per ring, no Kerr effect, lumped carriers with a linear free-carrier
    index, one thermal node per ring, one-way bus.
  * Measured at this version: `ringchaos_plots.py` 9 min 58 s (51 min 50 s with --lyapunov);
    `cargo test --workspace` 952 passed, `cargo xtask validate` 28/28 (as 2.16.2; no code
    changed).

2.16.2+4 - 2026-10-04
---------------------
**Multiphysics example 14: an SRAM single-event upset** (`models/multiphysics/seu/`;
`seu_plots.py`; `docs/multiphysics.md` and the tutorial). An ion track's charge collected by
diffusion (a 50-slice `generate` ladder of a `carrier` discipline, the deposit set as the
operating point through `analysis("static")`), a 6T cell of simplified-EKV transistors held in
its state by `.nodeset`, and the critical charge found by bisection over generated decks.
  * Collection against the diffusion equation's exact series: -0.15 % at 0.1 ns (the ladder cannot
    follow the t^-1/2 spike for its first ~3 ps), within 0.002 % from 2 ns on.
  * Critical charge against an exponential pulse's tau, between two limits from the cell's own
    equations: C VDD = 1.2 fC for an instantaneous pulse (exact: a symmetric cell's separatrix is
    q = qb), reached within 0.7 % at 0.1 ps; I_crit tau with I_crit = 46.6 uA (the N-curve's
    peak), approached from above (+11 % at 1 ns, +4.9 % at 3 ns).
  * Collected by diffusion from a 2 um track, the cell needs 10.54 fC deposited, 8.8 x C VDD;
    the same with 50, 100 or 200 slices (10.72 fC with 25).
  * Limitations: one-dimensional collection (no funnelling, drift or lateral spread), uniform
    track, transistors without capacitance or body effect.
  * Measured at this version: `seu_plots.py` 3 min 28 s (two decks and 41 bisection runs);
    `cargo test --workspace` 952 passed, `cargo xtask validate` 28/28 (as 2.16.2; no code changed).


2.16.2+3 - 2026-10-04
---------------------
**Multiphysics example 13: a horn loudspeaker** (`models/multiphysics/speaker/`;
`speaker_plots.py`; `docs/multiphysics.md` and the tutorial). A moving-coil driver (stiffening
suspension, Bl falling with excursion, sealed back box, temperature-dependent coil), an
exponential horn as a `generate` ladder over an `acoustic` discipline, the mouth's radiation load,
and a coil -> magnet -> ambient thermal network. Electrical, mechanical, acoustic and thermal.
  * Small signal against the exact continuous horn (Webster transfer matrix): input impedance
    within 0.011 % (N = 256); radiated power within 0.21 % to 1 kHz with 256 sections, while 64
    sections are 2.7 % off at 501 Hz and 14 % at 1 kHz (the ladder's dispersion; its own cutoff,
    c/(pi dz), is ~2 kHz).
  * Large signal, 20 V at 30 Hz below the horn's cutoff: the cone's excursion carries 2.3 % third
    harmonic, the mouth pressure 94 % (the horn passes 90 Hz, not 30 Hz); the nonlinear coil takes
    55 x the linear one's Joule heat (the Bl drop removes the back-EMF at resonance). Energy
    balance over ten cycles: residual -0.002 % and +0.001 %.
  * Thermal compression, 40 V at 200 Hz for 12 s: coil to 110.3 C, output -2.032 dB against
    -2.028 dB from the small-signal response at the coil's resistance.
  * Cost, stated: the thermal deck was first 30 s with 64 sections and was on course for 45 min
    (40 steps a cycle for 1200 cycles per coil time constant); cut to 12 s and 32 sections.
  * Limitations: plane-wave horn without wall losses, lumped radiation impedance, constant Le,
    symmetric nonlinearities, Bl and Kms independent of temperature.
  * Measured at this version: the three decks 4 min 12 s together (`speaker_plots.py`); `cargo
    test --workspace` 952 passed, `cargo xtask validate` 28/28 (as 2.16.2; no code changed).


2.16.2+2 - 2026-10-04
---------------------
**Multiphysics example 12: cavity optomechanics** (`models/multiphysics/optomech/`;
`optomech_plots.py`; `docs/multiphysics.md` and the tutorial). A 10 MHz mechanical mode in a
1550 nm cavity: the field as two real nodes, radiation pressure as a force from the stored energy,
the detuning moved by the displacement. Optical, mechanical and (unused here) thermal domains.
  * Threshold and cooling: four instances kicked by 1 fm. Amplitude rates -1569.0, +0.3, +3138.0
    (blue, 0.5/1/2 x P_th = 28.34 nW) and -9420.3 /s (red, 2 x P_th), against the exact
    eigenvalues of the model's own linearized equations, -1570.5, +0.3, +3141.0, -9429.3 /s:
    within 0.1 %, all on the damped side, at a 1 ns step cap (a 5 ns cap reads 2.3 % low).
  * Phonon laser at 4 x P_th: growth at the exact rate (9418 /s), saturation at 4.7559 pm; an
    independent energy balance (field ODE integrated under imposed harmonic motion, work per cycle
    = mechanical loss) gives 4.7577 pm.
  * Correction found while building it: the first decks were centred on a hand-derived threshold
    of 27.25 nW, against which the simulator looked 7.7 % slow. The exact eigenvalues agreed with
    the simulator; the weak-coupling formula, evaluated correctly, gives 28.34 nW as well. The
    script now computes both references itself.
  * Limitations: one optical and one mechanical mode, classical, linear absorption; the
    photothermal path (`gt`) is in the model but in no deck.
  * Measured at this version: both decks 2 min 6 s together (`optomech_plots.py`, 2.3 million
    steps); `cargo test --workspace` 952 passed, `cargo xtask validate` 28/28 (as 2.16.2; no code
    changed).


2.16.2+1 - 2026-10-04
---------------------
**Multiphysics example 11: a circuit-breaker arc** (`models/multiphysics/breaker/`;
`breaker_plots.py`; `docs/multiphysics.md` and the tutorial). A Mayr arc between moving contacts,
cooled by the gas a puffer blows across it; the puffer is compressed by the contacts' travel and
heated by the arc. Electrical, gas dynamics and mechanical domains in one module.
  * Static characteristic: under a slow current ramp, with no blast, V*I = 20004 W at 10 A and
    20000.0 W at 1 kA against the closed form's 20 kW (the 0.02 % is the arc's lag, tau d(ln I)/dt).
  * Interruption of an offset 1 kA, 50 Hz fault: with blast the arc chops 4.404 A at 19.144 ms and
    the recovery voltage peaks at -16,969 V (energy-conservation closed form -17,203 V); without,
    3.652 A at 19.225 ms and -14,828 V (-15,060 V). The closed form is 1.5 % high in both: it holds
    the source still over the half-ring.
  * Written so the solver survives it: the state is ln g (twenty decades become a range of 46);
    `exp` is continued linearly past ln g = 18.4, because a `min()` clamp zeroed the row's
    derivative and the matrix went singular; the contacts start closed; the gas density is
    floored for Newton's first iterate. The run needed the 2.16.2 junction-guess fix.
  * Not shown, stated: re-ignition. With tau from 1 to 20 us, with and without blast, the arc
    always cleared at this circuit's recovery-voltage rates (up to ~2 kV/us).
  * Limitations: Mayr only (no Cassie regime), constant tau, incompressible nozzle flow, travel
    prescribed, the netlist `SIN` has no phase (hence the fully offset fault).
  * Measured at this version: both decks 2.8 s together (`breaker_plots.py`); `cargo test
    --workspace` 952 passed, `cargo xtask validate` 28/28 (as 2.16.2; no code changed).


2.16.2 - 2026-10-04
-------------------
**The structural junction guess counts an `exp` only when its argument may read an electrical
quantity.** Since 2.16.1 the guess (a module with `exp` and no `$limit` has its electrical nodes
step-limited like a pn junction's voltage) was limited to electrical nodes, but any `exp` still
triggered it.
  * Found by the circuit-breaker example: its arc is held as ln g, and `exp(Lg(lg))` made both
    breaker terminals "junctions". Newton then cycled on the limiter's 0.33 V clamp at the
    terminal, every step longer than 0.3 ns failed, and a 25 ms run had not finished after ten
    minutes. With the fix it takes under a second.
  * Rule: an `exp` counts if its argument may read an electrical quantity: a probe of a branch
    with a non-ground electrical node, directly or through any operator, or, conservatively,
    any variable or user function (their values are not traced). Constants, parameters and
    `$param_given`-style queries do not count. A diode's `exp(V(a,c)/vt)` still counts.
  * Deck-diff against 2.16.1+3: 127 identical, 2 different, 13 skipped. The two are the new,
    not-yet-committed breaker decks: `breaker_static` takes 10,079 points where it took 61,876;
    `breaker_interrupt` was killed on the old binary after 8 minutes of 0.3 ns steps. Every
    committed deck, ReRAM included, is bit-identical (its `exp` arguments reach a voltage).
  * Test: `an_exp_of_a_non_electrical_state_is_not_a_junction` (an Arrhenius `exp` of a thermal
    state is not a junction; a diode `exp` is).
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 952 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored (1 new);
    `cargo xtask validate` 28/28.


2.16.1+3 - 2026-10-04
---------------------
**Multiphysics example 10: a piezoelectric harvester** (`models/multiphysics/piezo/`;
`piezo_plots.py`; `docs/multiphysics.md` and the tutorial). A Duffing cantilever with a piezo
layer, base excitation as a node voltage (a swept `chirp`, or a jittered `multisine` standing in
for random vibration, since `$rdist_*` folds to its mean in a transient), and Schottky bridges
into storage.

  * Linear power into a load: 6.3194 V against the exact two-degree-of-freedom steady state's
    6.3193 V, at a 20 us cap. The step matters: Q ~ 50 magnifies the trapezoidal rule's
    frequency error; 0.1 ms reads +0.08 %, converging at second order.
  * Duffing sweep: the jump, against harmonic balance (folds at 51.762 / 52.809 Hz, peak 0.8572 mm
    at 52.776 Hz). The sweep peaks at 0.859 mm, and the jumps lag the folds by 0.3 / 0.2 Hz at
    0.2 Hz/s and 0.19 / 0.14 Hz at half that.
  * Broadband storage as an ensemble of four realizations: the hardening beam wins one of four,
    9 % less energy on average. Stated, because the example gave two wrong answers on the way:
    +37 % from a deck that had lost a load resistor, -4 % from evenly spaced (periodic, bursty)
    tones.
  * The Schottky model carries leakage and junction capacitance: without them an all-reverse-
    biased bridge left the piezo floating, and the step collapsed.
  * Release record for 2.16.1: the combined deck-diff, 2.16.0 against 2.16.1 (rerun after the
    two partial runs were stopped by memory pressure), gives 113 identical, 11 different,
    13 skipped. Each difference is explained: the grid cascade (event state), the HH and ReRAM
    decks (junction guess; ReRAM event tolerances), and the Li-ion and ring-bank decks, which
    2.16.0 could not run.
  * No crate changed; figures are from runs at this version.

2.16.1+2 - 2026-10-04
---------------------
**Multiphysics example 9: Gray-Scott reaction-diffusion** (`models/multiphysics/grayscott/`;
`gray_scott.py`; `docs/multiphysics.md`, and its tutorial section). A PDE on an N x N torus as a
circuit: 2-D vector nets of a user discipline, built by nested `generate` loops, with the
reaction as a nonlinear current and the Laplacian as conductances. The model writes the whole
grid to a file through `$fopen`/`$fwrite`, from an event `or` list (2.16.0).

  * `gray_scott_wellmixed.net`: the reaction alone ends exactly on its closed-form states,
    (0.200000, 0.400000) and (1.000000, 3.6e-71), from either side of the saddle: bistable.
  * `gray_scott_spots.net`: Pearson's mitosis regime on 32 x 32 (2049 unknowns, sparse) divides
    one spot into 2 then 4 by t = 3000, which tile the torus; a 20 s step cap gives the same
    pattern as 5 s, in 97 s instead of 225 s.
  * Not a deck, stated: the labyrinth regime needs a larger torus (48 x 48, ~16 min), and grew
    the seed's four-fold symmetry rather than a labyrinth.
  * No crate changed; figures are from runs at this version.

2.16.1+1 - 2026-10-04
---------------------
**Multiphysics examples 5-8, and a tutorial for all of them.** Each example has decks with
closed-form checks in their headers, a plot script that prints the checks beside the
simulation, and a section in `docs/multiphysics.md`. The tutorial, `docs/multiphysics_tutorial.qmd`
(Quarto), walks through every example: each figure's command, and every flag, card and
parameter it depends on, with the reason it is there.

  * Power-grid swing (`models/multiphysics/powergrid/`; `grid_plots.py`). Classical swing
    equations on bus-angle nodes; the DC operating point is the nonlinear load flow; lines carry
    their own relays. Equal-area criterion: the simulator's critical clearing time, bisected, is
    within 0.02 % of the closed form's 0.2624 s, and the stable machine peaks at 130.39 deg
    (equal areas: 130.40). Cascade: one tie lost, two tripped, islands at +-1.4323 Hz (closed
    form +-1.4324).
  * Thermo-optic ring bank (`ringbank/`; `ring_plots.py`). Coupled-mode optics (16 ps) on 1 ms
    thermal nodes. Self-heating bistability: the jumps land within 0.009 nm of the fold points.
    Four PI-locked rings against crosstalk: the heater powers match the thermal network's closed
    form to 1e-4 mW in all three phases. The model's photon lifetime was first off by 2
    (tau = Q/w0 for 2Q/w0), caught by the linewidth check.
  * Li-ion cell (`liion/`; `li_plots.py`). Single-particle model: diffusion-shell ladders,
    inverted Butler-Volmer (the forward form gave Newton a 1e50 conductance), SEI growth,
    Hatchard-type abuse reactions.
      - Discharge: C/20 within 0.03 % of the open-circuit capacity (1.9609 Ah).
      - Calendar aging: SEI thickness within 2e-5 of the parabolic law over two years.
      - Oven test: runaway threshold bisected at 134.07 C for these parameters; runaway time
        step-converged to 0.2 s.
  * Electromagnetic relay (`relay/`; `relay_plots.py`). A Jiles-Atherton core, made continuous
    for an implicit integrator (lagged-H direction, smoothed switches), with a gap and armature
    and a heated coil.
      - B-H loop energy closes to 3e-5.
      - Lift-off at B = 0.1588 T (force balance: 0.15853 T); hot-pickup voltage ratio 1.2547
        against 1.2555.
      - A 20 um residual gap leaves the armature held by remanence.
      - Two model bugs, stated: a negative contact damping, hidden by backward Euler's numerical
        damping, and a smoothing scale that made the loop rate-dependent.
  * Limitations, per example, are in each model's header and in docs/multiphysics.md.
  * No crate changed. Figures are from runs at this version. `cargo test --workspace` (951
    passed) and `cargo xtask validate` (28/28) are 2.16.1's, the same code.

2.16.1 - 2026-10-04
-------------------
**Three simulator fixes found by the multiphysics examples.** Each was found by an example that
ran and printed numbers, wrong or refused, and each has its own deck-diff.

  * **An event's expression reads the instance's committed state.** `ModelInstance::events` had
    no state to read. A generated model therefore evaluated its `cross`/`above` sites with every
    state-channel variable at its seed and every `transition` without history, so a site that
    reached either through a variable saw the world before its own event fired.
      - Found by the power-grid cascade: a relay that had opened its line through
        `open = 1; g = 1 - transition(open, ...)` kept seeing it closed. It "tripped" again at
        every swing, 98 trip messages for 2 trips. The answer was right only because opening an
        open line changes nothing.
      - Fix: Interface beta gains `events_with_state(x, ctx, state, sink)`, a default method
        calling `events` (additive; `docs/interfaces.md`). va-transient passes each instance's
        committed slice; `Multiplied` forwards it.
      - Deck-diff against 2.16.0: 114 identical, 1 different (the cascade deck), 13 skipped.
      - Test: `a_monitored_expression_sees_the_state_its_event_set`. It counts 1 trip in five
        cycles; without the fix it counts 10.
  * **The linear solvers' residual test is measured row by row, on each row's own scale.** It
    compared the raw ‖Ax − b‖ with `1e-6 (1 + ‖b‖)`.
      - Found by the thermo-optic ring bank: a microring's field row (entries ~1e11, in 1/s)
        leaves ~1e-4 of round-off after a correct factorisation, and near Newton convergence b
        is tiny. The dense path called a well-posed 72-unknown system singular; the sparse path,
        rounding differently, solved it.
      - Fix: a solve passes if the raw test passes **or** the same test passes with each row
        divided by its largest |a_ij| (dense and sparse alike). Not the scaled test alone: that
        one ignores the size of x, and rejected a 150-stage gain chain's first Newton step
        (stage voltages ~1e11, 3e-8 of round-off in a row whose largest coefficient is 1.2e-3),
        which the raw test passes. The union keeps every solve that passed before, with the same
        bits, and adds the ones only units made it reject.
      - Deck-diff against the previous fix: only the ring-bank deck differs; it failed before.
        (That run was stopped by the machine's memory pressure before its last decks; the union
        form cannot change a solve the raw test accepted, which is every deck that ran before.)
      - Tests: `a_badly_scaled_well_posed_system_is_solved` fails with the old test and passes
        with the new; `proportional_rows_at_different_scales_are_still_singular`.
  * **The structural junction guess limits electrical nodes only.** A module with an `exp` and
    no `$limit` used to have every node step-limited like a pn junction's voltage.
      - Found by the Li-ion cell: an Arrhenius factor was enough, the cell's temperature moved
        0.24 K per Newton iteration from its 0 K start, and the operating point failed.
      - Fix: the guess now claims only `Discipline::Electrical` nodes. A module that declares
        its junctions with `$limit` is unaffected.
      - Cost, stated: decks whose models mix `exp` with non-electrical nodes take different
        Newton paths and step sequences:
          · ReRAM: the limiter had been forcing small steps. The sweep deck now takes 10,181
            points in 0.5 s, where it took 23,327 in 21 s; the 5 MHz deck 40,001 where it took
            49,748.
          · That exposed a reporting artefact in the ReRAM model, fixed here too: its SET/RESET
            strobes printed whichever accepted point first passed mid-gap, and RESET's voltage
            moved with the step size (-0.830 V at a 1 us cap, -0.852 V at 0.2 us). The events
            now carry a 1 ns time tolerance (`cross(expr, +1, 1e-9)`), and the reported values
            agree to 0.1 mV between the two caps. They still round to the header's figures.
          · HH axon: the thin fibre's ninth velocity report moves 0.568 -> 0.570 m/s; every figure
            its headers quote (18.971 / 12.218 m/s; 0.577 / 0.531 m/s; 29.62 / 18.82 mV peaks)
            re-measured unchanged.
        Its deck-diff, too, was stopped by memory pressure before the last decks.
      - Test: `a_declared_junction_overrides_the_structural_guess` gains a thermal node, which
        is no longer claimed.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 951 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored (3 new);
    `cargo xtask validate` 28/28.

2.16.0 - 2026-10-04
-------------------
**Event `or` lists** (LRM §5.10): `@(cross(...) or timer(...) or final_step)` and any mix of
`cross`, `above`, `timer`, `initial_step` and `final_step` now composes into
`if (e1 || e2 || ...)`, each scheduled event registering its own site. The body runs once at a
point where any of them fires, and once when several fire together. Before, only
`initial_step or final_step` composed; any list with a scheduled event was refused.

  * Why now: the Gray-Scott example needs `@(initial_step or timer(T, T) or final_step)` to
    write its grid at the start, periodically and at the end (a timer never fires on the run's
    initial point, and `final_step` is what catches a run whose end is not a timer multiple).
  * Still refused: a list naming `absdelta` (unimplemented) or a step event with an analysis
    filter (`final_step("tran")`). Those are the cases where dropping a term would run the body at
    the wrong points.
  * Nothing that parsed before changes: bare events and step-event lists now take the same path
    and build the same AST. `check --codegen` over models/, benchmark/models and external/ gives
    the same verdict on all 313 module files as 2.15.0, apart from the new example itself.
  * Tests: the three tests that pinned the old refusals now assert the composition
    (`an_or_list_with_scheduled_events_composes`, `an_or_list_with_an_unscheduled_event_is_refused`).
    `an_or_list_of_events_fires_on_either_and_once_on_both` (va-cli) runs a cross+timer list
    (8 firings in 1 us) and a list of two identical timers (4, not 8).
  * `Cargo.lock` now carries the version again: the 2.15.0 and 2.15.0+1 commits left it at
    2.14.0+3, because `cargo metadata --no-deps` does not rewrite it.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 948 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored (3 tests
    removed, 3 added); `cargo xtask validate` 28/28.

2.15.0+1 - 2026-10-04
---------------------
**Multiphysics example 4: a Hodgkin-Huxley axon with electrodiffusion**
(`models/multiphysics/axon/`; `docs/multiphysics.md`). `N` compartments built by `generate` loops
over vector nets of three disciplines: electrical, a user `gate` (channel open probabilities) and
a user `chemical` (intracellular Na+/K+). Each compartment's ENa and EK follow its own
concentrations through Nernst, and the concentrations follow the currents, diffusion and a
first-order pump. `@(cross)` at two compartments prints the conduction velocity via `$strobe`.

  * `hh_giant_axon.net`: the squid axon with HH's parameters. 18.97 m/s at 18.5 C, against HH's
    computed 18.8 (+0.9 %). Converged: 18.98 m/s in 300 compartments, 19.01 m/s with a 2 us step
    cap. A twin at 6.3 C conducts at 12.22 m/s (Q10 = 3 makes the gates 3.73x slower, the
    velocity only 1.55x). That figure is good to ~1 % only: alone in a deck it is 12.35 / 12.29 m/s
    at 10 / 2 us caps.
  * `hh_thin_fibre.net` + `hh_thin_fibre.py`: a 0.25 um fibre at 100 Hz for 1 s. In the middle
    compartment [Na]i goes 55.3 -> 86.6 mM and ENa 52.1 -> 40.9 mV, and the spike peak follows it,
    29.6 -> 18.8 mV. Velocity 0.577 -> 0.531 m/s; the first matches the sqrt(radius) scaling of
    the giant axon's (0.61) to 5 %. K+ lost (30.3 mM) balances Na+ gained (31.6 mM).
  * What it cost: the spatial-convergence run found the override bug fixed in 2.15.0. The
    concentrations are pinned at the DC point with `analysis("static")` because, in a thin fibre
    with a slow pump, Newton's first steps drove a concentration through zero and Nernst's log to
    NaN. My first deck header compared the 6.3 C velocity with HH's 18.8 m/s; theirs was computed
    at 18.5 C.
  * Limitations: ideal extracellular ground, phenomenological pump, an ion-less leak, squid
    kinetics in the thin fibre.
  * No crate changed. Figures are from the runs above at this version; `cargo test --workspace`
    (948 passed) and `cargo xtask validate` (28/28) are 2.15.0's, the same code.

2.15.0 - 2026-10-04
-------------------
**A deck override reaches what elaboration folded.** A device line's `name=value` used to be
applied to the one elaboration of a module made at its defaults, by editing the parameter's value
in the IR. That reaches every analog-block read and nothing the frontend had already folded: a
vector net's width, a `generate` bound, a constant index, **another parameter's default**. All of
it ran and printed numbers. The frontend now records which parameters it folded
(`va_ir::Module::elaboration_params`, an additive §6 change, `docs/interfaces.md`), and va-cli
elaborates a module again, through the new `elaborate_unit_with_overrides`, once per distinct set
of folded values a deck uses. stderr says so (`elaborated `m` again for N=300`). A device whose
values match no elaboration is an error, not a fallback.

  * Found by the HH axon example (next entry): `N=300` on a cable sized `[0:N-1]` kept 150
    compartments, divided its length by 300, and reported 37.6 m/s for a 19 m/s fibre.
  * Wrong answers it corrects in committed decks, measured by `xtask deck-diff` against 2.14.0+3
    (105 identical, 8 different, 13 skipped). Three moved, and all three are the same bug: a
    compact model whose defaults read `TYPE` gave every PMOS the NMOS defaults.
      - `nand/bsimsoi.net`: PMOS `VTHO` was +0.7 V instead of -0.7 (also `U0`, `EU`). V(out) at
        VA = 0.6 V goes 1.1759 -> 1.1295 V; the endpoints barely move.
      - `nand/bsimimg.net`: `WELLTYPE = -TYPE`, so every PMOS had an NMOS well. V(out) at
        VA = 1.2 V goes 0.3707 -> 0.3001 V.
      - `nand/hisim2.net`: **failed on every platform** (NaN at Newton's first iteration, listed in
        `docs/roadmap.md` as an open model problem). Its PMOS took NMOS mobility defaults
        (`MUEPH1`, `BB`). It solves now.
    `bsim6`, `bsimbulk107` and `fiber_mzi_noise` re-elaborate but print identical results: their
    folded values feed nothing these decks exercise, or the fold was attempted and not kept.
    The record over-approximates on purpose, since a needless re-elaboration costs milliseconds
    and a missed one costs a wrong answer.
  * Cost: one extra elaboration per distinct folded set (two for a CMOS deck: TYPE=1 and -1),
    plus one stderr line each. Every deck whose stdout did not change is listed above.
  * Tests: `folded_parameters_are_recorded_and_an_override_rebuilds_them` (frontend) and
    `a_device_override_of_a_folded_parameter_is_elaborated_with_its_value` (va-cli: three devices,
    two re-elaborations, currents checked against n*2a). The golden-IR snapshots gained one
    `elaboration_params: []` line each.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 948 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored;
    `cargo xtask validate` 28/28.

2.14.0+3 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**Multiphysics example 3: a filamentary ReRAM cell** — ionic filament gap, Joule heating and
Arrhenius-activated drift (`models/multiphysics/reram/`; `docs/multiphysics.md`). The gap is a state
node on a user discipline, pinned to its initial value at the DC point with `analysis("static")`;
SET/RESET are reported by `@(above)`/`@(cross)`; a fast gap asks for steps through `$bound_step`.

  * `reram_sweep.net` (10 ms triangle, two ambients): SET at 0.641 V (300 K) / 0.507 V (400 K),
    self-limited by the 2 kOhm compliance (gap closes over 0.58 ms); RESET at -0.830 / -0.696 V with
    the filament at ~610 / 553 K, abrupt (gap reopens in 10.5 us); the pinched loop drawn by
    `docs/examples/multiphysics/reram_iv.py`. Converged in step size (switching voltages agree to
    0.5 mV between 1 us and 0.2 us maximum steps).
  * `reram_freq_{5k,500k,5meg}.net` + `reram_loops.py`: the memristor fingerprint, lobe areas
    6.4e-4 -> 4.4e-4 -> 1.2e-4 V*A and gap swing 1.46 -> 0.93 -> 0.36 nm as the drive outruns the drift.
  * What it cost to get there, stated: my first header had SET and RESET's character backwards
    (SET is the self-limiting one with a series resistor); a field factor twice this one switched
    below a picosecond and could not be integrated; a single deck with three frequencies took over
    20 min (the fastest cell's switching sets every step), so the frequencies are separate decks of
    two periods, ~10 s each; and a signed loop integral cancels a pinched loop's two lobes, so each
    lobe is integrated alone.
  * Docs and decks only; no code change. Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 946
    passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask validate` 28/28.


2.14.0+2 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**Multiphysics example 2: an electrostatic MEMS actuator** — pull-in, squeeze-film and
thermoelastic damping, contact bounce and stiction (`models/multiphysics/mems/`, composable parts
on a position-as-potential mechanical node; `docs/multiphysics.md`).

  * `mems_pullin.net` (DC sweep): deflection 0.045741 / 0.220332 / 0.456840 um at 2 / 4 / 5 V, the
    closed form to the printed digits; the jump to the stop between 5.20 and 5.25 V brackets
    V_PI = 5.2259 V.
  * `mems_resonance.net` (AC, 3 V bias, 1 Pa and 100 Pa): resonance 9412.55 Hz for both, Q = 1605.2
    (thermoelastic-limited) and 78.2 (squeeze-film-limited), all as the models' equations predict.
    Two errors this check caught, both mine and not the simulator's: the thermoelastic heat term's
    sign (an active coupling: Q came out 1973, exactly the negative-damping prediction), and my own
    expected frequency, which had dropped the thermoelastic stiffening (2.5 Hz).
  * `mems_hysteresis.net` (transient triangle): landing at 5.38 V, 48 bounces on the Hertzian
    contact, release at 0.7038 V against the closed form's 0.7023 V.
  * Found on the way, kept in the models' comments: `limexp` folds to plain `exp` in this engine
    (docs/token-reference.md), so a logistic with a large argument overflowed to inf and its
    derivative to NaN; and `pow(max(d, 0), 1.5)` differentiates to 0*inf at d <= 0. Both models
    are written around them (a two-branch logistic, an explicit `if`).
  * Docs and decks only; no code change. Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 946
    passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask validate` 28/28.


2.14.0+1 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**First multiphysics example: a Peltier cooler under PID control** (`docs/multiphysics.md`, a
new page that will collect the examples of `docs/examples/examples_to_implement.txt` with a
Verilog-A coverage table). `models/multiphysics/tec/`: a thermoelectric module with Seebeck,
Peltier, Joule and Thomson effects on a user `thermal_abs` discipline (absolute kelvin), thermal
parts, and a PID with a `laplace_nd` derivative and back-calculation anti-windup.

  * `circuits/multiphysics/tec_dtmax.net` (DC sweep): minimum cold-face temperature 232.4555 K
    at 15.00 V / 5.8114 A against the closed form's 232.455 K at 15.00 V / 5.811 A.
  * `tec_energy.net` (`.op`): heat out of the hot face minus heat pumped minus electrical power
    = -2.5e-6 W of 20 W (print precision), with the Thomson heat (1.44 W) in the balance.
  * `tec_pid.net` (transient): from the uncooled 299.79 K the plate reaches the 0.1 K band of
    278.15 K at 30.86 s without undershoot, and rides a 1 -> 3 W load step at 60 s (+0.18 K).
  * Two things the example needed, stated: a `laplace_*` filter must be a whole contribution, so
    the derivative sits on its own node; and an instantaneous loop closure underflowed the
    timestep, so the enable ramps in over 0.2 s with `transition`.
  * Docs and decks only; no code change. `cargo test --workspace` 946 passed, 0 failed; `cargo
    xtask validate` 28/28.


2.14.0 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**An event on a variable fires.** `e = Temp(s) - tset; @(cross(e - band, -1)) ...` — the
ordinary way to write an event on a computed quantity — never fired: a generated model evaluates
its event sites (`GeneratedModel::events`) outside the statement walk, where no variable was
bound, so `e` read as 0.0, the site registered a constant, and nothing said so (the stated
limitation claimed validation would reject such a module; it does not, since the variable is
assigned before the event in the block). Now the block's variables are bound first by one
statement walk at that point, with nothing stamped, printed or written. Found by the new TEC
example, whose settling report never appeared.

  * Still stated: that walk has no committed state and no fired events, so a variable set only
    inside an event body, or only by an operator's history, reads its persistent seed (0) at
    registration.
  * Gated by `an_event_on_a_variable_fires` (`r = V(p) - 0.5; @(cross(r, +1))` fires once, at
    the first point past tau*ln 2).
  * Output changes: `xtask deck-diff` against the 2.13.0 binary: 90 identical, 1 different, 25
    skipped — the different one is the new `circuits/multiphysics/tec_pid.net`, whose
    `@(cross)` report now prints; no existing deck had an event on a variable.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 946 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.13.0+1 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**CI made cheap enough to fit the account's GitHub Actions minutes**, which ran out on
2026-10-03 after six pushes in a day: every push ran eight jobs, the gate and the cross-platform
repro check each on Linux, Windows and macOS, with macOS billed at 10x and Windows at 2x. Now:

  * push to `main` or a pull request: the gate on **Linux only**, plus `cargo deny`;
  * documentation-only changes (`docs/`, `*.md`, `paper_writing/`, `perf-logs/`, `references/`,
    `LICENSE`): nothing;
  * a `v*` tag (a release): **macOS and Windows** — the gate in `release.yml` (unchanged, already
    on all three platforms) and the repro check in `ci.yml`, which no longer repeats the gate;
  * "Run workflow" on `ci` with `full`: everything, on all three platforms, on demand;
  * a newer push cancels the run still going for the same ref; every job has a timeout, so a
    hang cannot burn the quota; the gate's separate `cargo build` step is gone (the test step
    builds the same code).
  * What it costs in checking: Windows and macOS are no longer seen between releases. Windows is
    covered by the development machine, where every release entry's figures are measured;
    macOS, and cross-platform bit-identity, by the release tag or a manual run.
  * No code change. Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 945 passed, 0 failed;
    `cargo xtask validate` 28/28. The workflows were checked to parse; they have not run on
    GitHub yet, since the quota is exhausted.


2.13.0 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**A model's statement walk that stops at run time is reported, not swallowed.** Validation at
build time is meant to make it impossible, but when a walk fails while a model is evaluated — a
`while` past its iteration cap, a read of a variable no path assigned — every statement after the
failure is skipped at that point, and until now `GeneratedModel::load` discarded the error (`let _
= self.run(...)`), as did a setup prefix that stopped. That is how 2.12.0's `$fopen` persistence
bug hid: the run "succeeded" with a model that had silently stopped half-way. Now the instance
reports a `$warning`-kind task, `<instance>: evaluation stopped early: <why>; the statements after
it did not run at this point` (`va_codegen::WALK_STOPPED`), acted on like any warning — from
accepted evaluations — and `va-cli` prints each distinct one once, with a closing count of the
repeats it held back. The skipping itself is unchanged: a loop that runs away still must not stamp
values from a half-finished environment.

  * Static analyses now run their evaluation at the converged solution for every circuit, not
    only those with tasks, so a stopped walk shows up in `.op`/`.dc`/`.ac`/`.noise` too. Cost
    measured as within noise: a 101-point BSIM4 NAND sweep 0.38 s vs 0.36-0.39 s; ISCAS c432
    `.op` 6.3-7.2 s vs 7.2 s.
  * Gated by the codegen loop-cap test (now asserting the warning) and
    `a_stopped_statement_walk_is_reported_at_every_accepted_point`.
  * Output changes: none on any deck — `xtask deck-diff` against the 2.12.0 binary: 89
    identical, 0 different, 25 skipped, i.e. no committed deck had a walk stopping in silence.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 945 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean. **Not pushed**: the account's GitHub Actions minutes ran out
    today (every push runs eight CI jobs, macOS counted 10x), so CI has not checked this.


2.12.0 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**`$monitor` and file output work.** `var = $fopen(name[, type])` returns the LRM's descriptors
(multichannel without a type, `mcd | 1` reaching the screen too; file descriptors with one) from a
per-instance file table; `$fdisplay`, `$fwrite`, `$fstrobe`, `$fmonitor`, `$fdebug`, `$fclose`
and `$fflush` write, flush and close through them. `$monitor`/`$fmonitor` print at an accepted
step only when an argument (other than `$abstime`) changed since the last accepted one, kept on
the instance's state channel, which commits only at accepted steps. File operations act only
from accepted evaluations, `$fdebug`'s on every iteration (LRM §9.5.9). `va-cli` opens paths
relative to the working directory, creates or truncates a path the first time it is opened in
the run and appends on every later open, and flushes and closes everything when it ends. Until
now `$monitor` and the writes were dropped (with a warning since 2.8.0) and `$fopen` was an
elaboration error.

  * Interface change (§6, both, additive, at the project owner's request): `Stmt::Task` gained
    `target` and nine task kinds; `StampSink` gained `file` and `va_abi::FileOp`
    (`docs/interfaces.md`, 2026-10-03c). Recorded and replayed on the plain, bypass and
    slot-cache paths.
  * Two existing faults found on the way, both fixed here: a `$fopen` target in
    `@(initial_step)` was not kept as a persistent variable, and reading it later failed
    silently — the model's statement walk discards its errors, so every statement after the
    read stopped running, tasks included (`persistent_vars` now counts task targets; the
    discarded walk error itself is unchanged, and is raised with the project owner); and a
    `@(final_step)` body that only prints or closes a file never ran, because the final-step
    probe compared stamps only (it now compares tasks and file operations too).
  * Refused, with reasons: `$fopen` for reading (`"r"`, `"+"`) — no read is implemented, and the
    LRM's rewind-on-rejection would have to be; `$fopen` nested in an expression. Stated
    limitations: a file opened in `@(initial_step)` is never opened in `.op`/`.dc` (that event
    fires only on the transient seed here); `$monitor` in a static analysis or a function body
    prints at every point; 30 multichannel files per instance.
  * New example: `circuits/tasks/rc_logger.net` with `models/tasks/rc_logger.va` — 47 lines to
    `target/rc_logger_points.txt` (one per accepted point), four level changes to
    `target/rc_logger_levels.txt` and the screen; `docs/examples.md` shows the output.
  * Output changes (`xtask deck-diff` against the 2.11.0 binary: 86 identical, 3 different, 25
    skipped): the three decks in `circuits/tasks/`, because the old binary cannot compile
    `models/tasks/rc_logger.va` and so fails the whole library; with each deck given only its own
    model file, `rc_watch` and `current_limit` are identical between the two. Corpus 256/280,
    unchanged file by file.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 944 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.11.0 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**Fresh evaluations add into the sparse matrix by position too, with or without `--bypass`.**
`va_core::par::SlotCache` keeps each instance's stamp layout — `(row, col, slot)` per Jacobian and
charge-Jacobian stamp, in the order its last fresh evaluation made them — and the next recording
of that instance is added by comparing each stamp with the layout (two integers) instead of
hashing `(row, col)`. A stamp that differs (a branch taken differently) is looked up and the
layout rewritten from there, so a wrong slot is impossible; a new sparse pattern clears it. Used
by the transient's sparse path for every parallel evaluation (`load_all_sparse`) and every bypass
miss (`load_all_bypass_sparse`). The serial path for cheap circuits, the dense path and DC are
unchanged. Same additions in the same order: **bit-identical**.

  * Measured, interleaved against 2.10.0 on the hidden-2 crossbar, i7-1185G7 on mains: without
    bypass, 8 neurons 201.1 -> 179.7 s (1.12x, two pairs); with `--bypass`, 8 neurons 41.6 ->
    37.8 s (1.10x, three pairs), 16 neurons 86.3 -> 79.9 s (1.08x, two pairs). Modest, as it
    should be: a fresh evaluation costs far more than its stamping, and under bypass only ~10 %
    of loads are fresh. Not measured at 64 neurons.
  * Identity: stdout + stderr equal to 2.10.0 with and without `--bypass` on `fig5_access`,
    Fig. 4, Fig. 5, the c17 transient and the 8-neuron crossbar;
    `slot_cache_replays_a_recording_bit_for_bit_whatever_its_layout_does` covers a layout that
    changes mid-recording and a new pattern. `xtask deck-diff` against 2.10.0: 88 identical, 0 different, 25 skipped.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 941 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.10.0 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**Bypass replays add into the sparse matrix through cached slots.** Each bypassed device's
recording keeps the matrix slot of every Jacobian and charge-Jacobian stamp, resolved once
against the sparsity pattern (`Pattern::id`) and recomputed only when the pattern changes, so a
replay no longer hashes `(row, col)` per stamp (`va_core::par::load_all_bypass_sparse`,
`SparseSystem::add_jacobian_at`/`add_dcharge_at`). The transient's sparse path uses it; the dense
path and runs without `--bypass` are untouched. Same additions in the same order: **bit-identical**.

  * Measured, `--bypass`, hidden-2 crossbar (`xbar_h2_<N>.net`), the two builds interleaved
    (2.9.1 vs this), i7-1185G7 on mains: 8 neurons 59.4 -> 41.7 s (1.42x, three pairs, spread
    <= 2 s); 16 neurons 131.0 -> 88.3 s (1.48x, two pairs); 64 neurons 1315 -> 1035 s (1.27x, one
    pair; an earlier non-interleaved pair gave the same ratio, 1188 -> 947 s). The gain falls at
    64 because device evaluation, which this does not touch, is the larger part there (683 s of
    a profiled 1153 s); why each evaluation costs ~8x more at 64 than at 8 neurons is still open.
  * Methodology note, because it cost a wrong first table: a sequential run that always timed
    the old build first showed 1.78x at 8 neurons; the old build's own repeat was 59 s, not
    115 s. Only the interleaved figures above are kept.
  * Identity: stdout + stderr hashes equal to the 2.9.1 build on the 8/16/32/64-neuron crossbars,
    and to 2.7.0 on `fig5_access`, Fig. 4, Fig. 5, `ring_osc`, `rectifier`, c17 transient;
    `slot_cached_replay_gives_the_sinks_bits_and_survives_a_grown_pattern` pins it, a pattern
    change (stale slots, overflow stamps) included. `xtask deck-diff` against 2.9.0 without the
    flag: 88 identical, 0 different, 25 skipped.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 940 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.9.1 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**Fix: 2.8.0 took device bypass away from every PSP103.** 2.8.0 made any module containing a
system task impure for bypass, judged from the source. PSP103 compiles in its safe-operating-area
`$strobe`s (`` `define SOAcheck ``) but runs them only `if (SWSOA > 0)`, and SWSOA defaults to 0,
so no PSP103 was ever bypassed again: `fig5_access.net` with `--bypass` went from 35 to 27
eligible instances and from 12.4 s to ~25 s. Found while measuring the slot cache. A task now
counts only if it can run under the instance's parameters (`va_codegen::lower::task_reachable`:
a condition that folds from parameters to the other arm hides it; a task in an analog function
body always counts). The guard test is renamed
`bypass_purity_rests_on_two_folds_and_on_no_reachable_task` and pins both halves (SWSOA = 0 pure,
SWSOA = 1 impure); `docs/interfaces.md` carries the correction under the 2.8.0 revision.

  * Output with `--bypass` is bit-identical to 2.7.0 again, on `fig5_access`, Fig. 4, Fig. 5,
    `ring_osc`, `rectifier` and the c17 transient (stdout and stderr hashed, eligibility and
    replay counts equal). Without `--bypass` nothing changes: purity is read only by bypass.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 939 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.9.0 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**`va-cli sim --tasks-once`: print each distinct system-task line once.** A `$strobe`/`$display`/
`$warning`/... line identical to one already printed is counted instead, and a closing line on
stdout and stderr says how many were held back. Off by default, because it is not the LRM's
behaviour (a task prints every time it runs at an accepted point); it exists for vendor models
whose parameter checks are plain analog code and repeat at every point, which 2.8.0 made visible:
`benchmark/nand/bsim4.net` prints its `RECALCULATION for no K1 or K2` 404 times, once with the
flag ("403 repeated system-task line(s) not printed").

  * Lines are told apart by their text alone: two instances stay distinct only if the model
    prints `%m` (BSIM4's checks do not, so its four instances share one line). A line whose
    values change still prints every time; every `$error` still counts toward the exit status.
  * Output changes without the flag: none. `xtask deck-diff` against the 2.8.0 binary: 88
    identical, 0 different, 25 skipped.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 939 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.8.0 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**System tasks have an effect.** `$strobe`, `$display`, `$write`, `$debug`, `$finish`, `$stop`,
`$fatal`, `$error`, `$warning` and `$info` now run, where until now the frontend dropped every
one of them. Their arguments are evaluated and formatted (LRM §9.4.3: `%d %h %o %b %c %s %e %f %g
%r %m %l`, widths, precisions, empty arguments), and they act when the LRM says: in transient,
`$debug` from every iteration, `$fatal` at once (the run fails), everything else only from an
accepted timepoint, and `$finish`/`$stop` end the run after it. In `.op`/`.dc`/`.ac`/`.noise`,
from one evaluation at the converged solution (a `$finish` stops a `.dc` sweep after its
point). `va-cli sim` prints display tasks on stdout, severity tasks on stdout and stderr with
the time, a note when `$finish` ended the analysis, and exits 1 after its results if any
`$error` ran. `%m` is the deck's instance name.

  * Interface change (§6, both, additive, at the project owner's request): `va_ir::Stmt::Task`
    and `va_abi::StampSink::task`, recorded in `docs/interfaces.md`. A module with a task is no
    longer `is_pure_in_x` (bypass would skip its output) — the 2.7.0 guard test failed on this,
    as written to, and now pins the two folds left: `$limit` and `$rdist_*`.
  * `$limit` was re-checked against LRM §9.17.3 as asked: folding to the access value is what
    the LRM allows (the simulator decides whether and how to limit; at convergence the value is
    the access value), and the declared junction is limited on its Newton step. The algorithm
    name and its `vte`/`vcrit` are still not honoured (`docs/token-reference.md`).
  * New examples: `circuits/tasks/rc_watch.net` (transient: one `$strobe` at the 0.9 V crossing,
    `$finish` past 0.95 V ends the run at 3.011 ms of 10 ms) and `circuits/tasks/current_limit.net`
    (`.dc`: a `$strobe` per point, `$info` + `$finish` at 3 mA, 7 points of 11), with
    `models/tasks/`; `docs/examples.md` shows the output.
  * Not implemented, and no longer silent: `$monitor` and the file family (`$fopen`,
    `$fdisplay`, `$fstrobe`, ...) are reported once each as a compile warning. Stated limitations:
    `$debug` in a static analysis prints once, not per Newton iteration; `$stop` acts as
    `$finish`; `$finish` does not trigger `@(final_step)`; a task in `@(cross(...))` runs at the
    accepted point after the crossing.
  * Output changes, and why (`xtask deck-diff` against the 2.7.0 binary: 84 identical, 4
    different, 25 skipped): the two new decks, and `benchmark/nand/bsim4.net` and `bsimimg.net`,
    whose vendor models now print their parameter-check messages — 404 lines each (4 instances x
    101 sweep points), because those checks are plain analog code, not `@(initial_step)`, so by
    the LRM they run at every accepted point. Correct and noisy; numbers unchanged.
  * Corpus: 256/280 files through frontend + codegen, unchanged and identical file by file
    against 2.6.0 — no model stopped compiling because its task arguments are now elaborated.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 938 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.7.0 - 2026-10-03
--------------------
**Device bypass is on main, opt-in: `va-cli sim --bypass`.** A device whose stamps are a pure
function of its own unknowns (`ModelInstance::is_pure_in_x`, Interface β, **§6 change ratified
today**) reuses its last evaluation, corrected to first order, while those unknowns move less
than `--bypass-reltol` (default 1e-6) plus 1e-3 x their nature's `abstol` — 1e-9 V on a voltage
node, 1e-15 A on a current (the prototype's flat 1e-9 was 1 nA on a current). Transient only.
**Off by default**, because a bypassed run is not bit-identical to a plain one, cheap circuits
get slower (`ring_osc` 0.1 -> 0.4 s, `fig5_neuron` 3.0 -> 5.6 s on the prototype) and no
committed golden covers a bypassed transistor. Every bypassed run says so on stdout and stderr
alike, before and after: lines starting `***** BYPASS [ACTIVE] *****` (the warning, then what
it replayed). `VA_BYPASS` is gone; `bench_transistor_linux.sh`'s `_bp` runs pass `--bypass`.

  * Why: the transistor SNN. `fig5_access.net` (8 PSP103): 24.0 s -> 12.4 s, 86.6 % of eligible
    loads replayed, every one of 12 837 timepoints shared with the plain run, worst difference
    1e-6 V (print precision). The prototype's c17 transient: 84.6 -> 37.5 s.
  * Its gain shrinks with size, now located (`docs/proposals/device-bypass.md` §6): on the
    64-neuron crossbar (140 574 unknowns) the LU solve is 5 % of a bypassed run; serial stamping
    (a SipHash lookup per stamp) grows 12x for 8x the devices, and a fresh evaluation 7.8 -> 60 µs,
    the larger part and unexplained. Both are next, before bypass could ever default on.
  * `is_pure_in_x` rests on three folds — `$limit`, `$rdist_*`, and system tasks lowered to
    nothing — stated in `docs/interfaces.md` and gated by
    `bypass_purity_rests_on_three_folds_and_this_test_catches_a_change`, which fails the day one
    of them gains an effect.
  * Also fixed: va-cli's `crossing(s) fired without meeting the time_tol/expr_tol` warning
    printed a run of spaces mid-sentence (a lost line continuation, since 2.5.0 or earlier).
  * Output changes without the flag: none. `xtask deck-diff` against the 2.6.0 binary: 86
    identical, 0 different, 25 skipped (the slow ISCAS set, and the transistor-neuron, crossbar
    and MNIST transistor decks).
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 930 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28 (and 28/28 with bypass on, on the prototype — only one instance in the
    suite is eligible, so that proves little); clippy clean.


2.6.0 - 2026-10-02
--------------------
**Transient tolerances settable from the command line, and what loosening them and scaling the
crossbar cost on the transistor SNN.** `va-cli sim --reltol <x>` (step control's relative
truncation-error budget, default 1e-3), `--abstol <x>` (its absolute floor, default 1e-6) and
`--newton-reltol <x>` (each step's Newton tolerance, default 1e-9, until now a constant in
va-transient: `TranConfig::newton_reltol`, `NEWTON_RELTOL_DEFAULT`). Without the flags every
run is unchanged. A loosened run is not re-validated against golden; the help text says so.

  * Tolerance study (`circuits/snn/transistor/tolerance_study.py`, figure `tolerance_study.png`):
    on `fig5_tx.net`, `--reltol` from 1e-3 to 1 moves V_m at the 15 latch samples by at most
    0.003 % and never changes a spike, and **no looser run is faster** (445.6 s reference;
    454-587 s, with 5-38 % fewer timepoints). The 10 % limit the study searched for is never
    reached. `--newton-reltol 1e-7`: identical, 508 s. The rest of the Newton sweep and the
    `--abstol` sweep were not run (stopped by choice).
  * Crossbar timing (`gen_mnist_tx.py`: the MNIST decks with SG13G2 access devices, neurons as
    transistors or behavioural, any subset of layers and neurons). The hidden-2 crossbar at
    8/16/32/64 neurons, 2 cycles: plain 255/445/883/1909 s, linear at ~160 us per access
    transistor per timepoint, 140 574 unknowns and 3.4 GB at 64. With the `proto/static-bypass`
    prototype (not on main): 70/157/407/1289 s, the gain falling 3.6x -> 1.5x with 90 % of loads
    replayed throughout, so something besides device evaluation grows superlinearly (not yet
    profiled). The whole network with transistor neurons is 437 480 unknowns; 5 ns had not
    finished in 10 min. Extrapolated, and labelled so in the README: ~16-52 h per image for the
    crossbars alone with behavioural neurons.
  * Output changes: none. `xtask deck-diff` against the 2.5.0 binary, flags not given: 85
    identical, 0 different (the new transistor and crossbar decks and the slow ISCAS set skipped).
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 926 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.5.0 - 2026-10-02
--------------------
**The Fig. 5 neuron runs with every block as transistors, and three transient fixes it needed.**
`circuits/snn/transistor/gen_neuron.py` writes the Fig. 5 deck with any of the neuron's four
blocks (switches, comparator, flip-flop + reset logic, input stage) as SG13G2 PSP103 devices;
`fig5_tx.net` has all four: 68 devices, 1169 unknowns, 23 786 timepoints, 443.9 s (i7-1185G7,
8 threads), output spikes at steps 6, 10, 14 as in the paper. Every design and size is ours (the
paper gives none), and the input stage is not Fig. 2's push-pull conveyor (dead zone at
I_in = 0): a biased conveyor, 1/K mirrors, a trimmed sink, and output steering onto V_m or a
follower of V_m. The rung-by-rung ladder and the input stage's measured accuracy are in
`circuits/snn/transistor/README.md`.

  * Cost, stated plainly: V_m departs from the paper's ideal-switch equations by up to 0.173 V,
    of which 0.131 V is the access transistors' R_on (2.3.0) and ~43 mV the neuron's blocks; the
    deck's 0.05 V check reports FAIL, as it has since 2.3.0, for that reason. The wall time is
    18x the access-only deck's for 8.5x the devices.
  * va-transient, **breakpoint slivers**: a breakpoint less than `tstep_min` after the current
    time is now the instant already landed on. A model registered one a single ulp after a
    source corner the run had just landed on, and every attempt was clamped to a 2.1e-22 s step
    (companion coefficient 4.7e21, residual stuck at 2^-20 A) until the run underflowed. Test
    `a_breakpoint_registered_one_ulp_after_a_landing_is_not_stepped_to` (discriminating: without
    the fix the shortest step is a 2e-25 s sliver).
  * va-transient, **stall rule**: at the minimum step only, a Newton solve whose last two
    iterations moved by less than 1e-6 relative / 1e6 x the per-unknown abstol with the residual
    under 1e-6 is accepted and counted (`Waveform::stalled_steps`; va-cli prints a note). Found on
    a pass gate turning off at V_DS = 0. No deck in the ladder above invokes it at this version.
  * va-transient: an LTE reference solve that does not converge now halves the step, as the
    primary solve's failure does, instead of ending the run.
  * va-codegen: `counters_count_evaluations_only_while_enabled` had failed since 2.4.0: its
    probe-allocation bound assumed the tree-walking evaluator, and the static schedule reads
    probes from slots. The bound now applies with `VA_SCHED=off` only. A test fix. It fails
    deterministically on a clean 2.4.0+1 checkout, so 2.4.0's "924 passed" either missed it or
    was measured before the final schedule change; which one is not known.
  * Output changes: `xtask deck-diff` against the 2.4.0+1 binary, 82 identical, 3 different, all
    from the sliver fix: `fig4_neuron`, `fig5_neuron`, `fig5_access` lose 2-4 timepoints each
    (sub-floor duplicates of a corner; fig4_neuron and fig5_access then take a different step
    sequence), and every V_m sample `check_snn.py` reads is identical at the printed precision
    (Figs. 4 and 5 PASS with the same numbers). The six new transistor-neuron decks and the slow ISCAS'85 set
    were not diffed.
  * Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 926 passed, 0 failed; `cargo xtask
    validate` 28/28; clippy clean.


2.4.0+1 - 2026-10-02

**2.4.0's static schedule verified on every deck, the slow ISCAS'85 ones included: no output
changes.** An overnight run of the 2.4.0 binary (`perf-logs/2026-10-01-static/`, local), one deck
at a time with `VA_SCHED=off` and then on, each run writing straight to a file. No simulator
change.

  * The 85 regular decks: `deck-diff` 85 identical, 0 different.
  * All 13 slow ISCAS'85 decks byte-identical, every run exiting 0. Old evaluator → static
    schedule:
    c432 14 → 8 s, c499 20 → 11 s, c880 22 → 12 s, c1355 30 → 15 s, c1908 44 → 24 s,
    c2670 88 → 49 s, c17_tran 90 → 46 s, c3540 202 → 115 s, c5315 273 → 170 s,
    c6288 164 → 92 s, c432_tran 995 → 552 s, c7552 (268k unknowns) 403 → 251 s,
    c432_seq 3050 → 2157 s.
  * This replaces 2.4.0's "9 of 13 so far". Those nine had run on a build from before the last
    change, in which conditions read a register's value directly; all thirteen were rerun here
    on the final binary.


2.4.0 - 2026-10-01
--------------------
**A statically scheduled evaluator for compiled models: PSP103 with IHP's SG13G2 card goes from
~245 µs to 76 µs per `load()`, bit-identical.** `va-codegen/src/sched.rs`. Which unknowns an
expression can depend on is fixed by the model's structure, not the bias. So each model is
compiled once into instructions over fixed registers: every variable, intermediate and probe gets
one value and one partial per slot it can depend on. Variable reads and constants then cost
nothing, and nothing is pushed, copied or allocated per node. Contributions are handed to the
unchanged stamping code as `Dual`s at the end. A model the compiler cannot take whole stays on the
tree walk (`docs/proposals/gradient-storage.md` §0.2 lists what is refused).
`VA_SCHED=off` turns the path off.

  * Why this and not sparse gradients (§0.1, branch `sparse-gradients`, not merged): those were
    bit-identical and removed 97 % of the allocations, but were no faster on one thread. The
    cost was the interpreter's per-node overhead (~6 100 instructions per load, 57 % of them
    pushes of a variable or a constant), not the storage.
  * Correctness. `cargo xtask sched-check` (new) compares every stamp and state proposal of the
    two paths at 42 bias points, in DC and transient, for every model. Over 167 model files:
    0 differing finite evaluations, 66 models on the static path. Some random bias points are
    NaN on both paths; there the tree walk spreads the NaN into partials the value cannot
    depend on, the one designed difference. `deck-diff` with `VA_SCHED=off` against on:
    85 identical, 0 different. The slow ISCAS'85 decks, one at a time: 9 of 13 identical so far
    (c17_tran, c432, c499, c880, c1355, c1908, c2670, c3540, c432_tran), every run exiting 0.
    c432_seq, c5315, c6288 and c7552 are still running. `cargo test --workspace` 924 passed.
  * Speed, i7-1185G7, `fig5_access`: 8 threads 60.8–61.5 → 31.9–33.2 s; 1 thread 115.9 →
    41.0 s. ISCAS'85 decks roughly halve: c432_tran 1116 → 549 s, c3540 218 → 124 s. With
    device bypass (branch `proto/device-bypass`, merged with this on `proto/static-bypass` for
    measurement): 14.9 s on 8 threads and **9.3 s on 1**. That is 12.5× against today on one
    thread, and bit-identical to bypass alone.
  * Also: `deck-diff`/`deck-run` read every `--model` of a deck's documented command line, and
    accept `va-cli sim` lines as well as `cargo run -- sim`. Before, the transistor decks ran
    with only their first library and failed identically on both sides, which counted as
    "identical". `bench-instance` prints the hoistable count. `bench_transistor_linux.sh`
    names its runs by setting (`acc1_old_bp` = one thread, tree walk, bypass).
  * Limitations: each instance keeps a register file behind an uncontended `Mutex`. A
    tree-walked call inside a compiled expression gets the full support. The
    definite-assignment check is conservative. With bypass on, one thread beats eight: the
    parallel threshold does not know that most loads are replayed.


2.3.0+3 - 2026-10-01
--------------------
**The transistor-deck benchmark on Linux, with and without device bypass.** `scripts/benchmark/
bench_transistor_main.txt` (`main` at 2.3.0+1) and `bench_transistor_bypass.txt` (branch
`proto/device-bypass`), both run by `bench_transistor_linux.sh` on an AMD Ryzen 9 9950X (32
threads, Ubuntu 26.04, `performance` governor). No simulator change.

  * Same answers as Windows: every output hash matches the i7-1185G7 runs. That is `bcbc3345…`
    ideal switches, `6681705b…` access transistors, and `a0c31322…` with bypass. The bypass run
    is identical on 32 threads and on 1. Both branches give the same hash without bypass.
  * Access transistors without bypass: 18.2 s on 32 threads, 64.5 s on 1 (Windows i7: 56–61 s
    and 116 s). The ideal-switch deck takes 1.2 s.
  * **With bypass: 9.7 s on 32 threads, 9.9 s on 1 thread**, against 17.9 s and 64.4 s without.
    That is 1.8× on 32 threads and **6.5× on one**. Threads stop helping once bypass is on: each
    assembly has few devices left to evaluate, too few to be worth spreading across threads.
  * The scripts' `checkout:` line reads `n/a` on that machine (git could not report the commit);
    the branch is identified by the bypass runs and the binary hashes instead.


2.3.0+2 - 2026-10-01
--------------------
**PSP103 profiled on the SG13G2 card: allocation and zeros, not maths.** Adds `cargo xtask
bench-instance <deck> --model ... [--dc] [--reps N]`, which times each compiled instance's `load()` at
the deck's own operating point, and `circuits/snn/transistor/bench_access_bias.net` (one access
transistor on, one off). No simulator change.

  Measured (i7-1185G7): one PSP103 `load()` with the SG13G2 card costs 195–237 µs, against 106–111 µs
  with the `.va` defaults at the same bias. The analysis context and the bias make no difference, and
  the card's full JUNCAP (`swjuncap=3`) accounts for the doubling. A samply profile puts the maths
  library at ~2 %, gradient heap traffic at ~25–30 %, the tape interpreter at 26 % self, `Dual`
  arithmetic at ~20 % and the variable table at ~11 %. A load visits 6 186 nodes, and **a gradient
  holds 2.9 non-zero entries out of 21**. `docs/proposals/gradient-storage.md` §0 records this and
  moves its option D (sparse, inline gradients) first. Nothing is decided.

2.3.0 - 2026-10-01
------------------
**The SNN gets real transistors: an IHP SG13G2 technology layer, two model libraries in one run,
and a cold start that PSP103 survives.** The first step toward a transistor-level SNN, built so
that the foundry's SG13S models can replace the stand-in without touching the decks.

  * `models/sg13g2/`: IHP's open-PDK PSP 103.8.2 (unchanged, plus `discipline.h`) and
    `sg13g2_cards.py`, which evaluates the PDK's ngspice libraries (typical corner, pre-layout,
    non-RF) into flat cards, one per geometry. SG13G2 is the public sibling of the paper's
    SG13S, not the same process. **Not validated against a reference simulator** (QSPICE
    cannot run PSP103 Verilog-A; ngspice declined for now: these devices are a stand-in).
  * `circuits/snn/transistor/`: `tech.py`, the swappable technology layer every MOS device goes
    through, and `fig5_access.net`, the Fig. 5 deck with its 8 access switches as
    W/L = 10/0.45 µm HV NMOS.
  * `va-cli sim --model` can repeat. The libraries compile as one, and each file resolves its
    `` `include ``s in its own library only (the SNN blocks' `../disciplines.vams` beside
    PSP103's `discipline.h`).
  * **va-transient's seed rule.** From a cold (`UIC`) seed, a row with state pinned to a driven
    source through a compact model's terminal resistance (PSP103's internal source node on a
    1.65 V source line) has to jump at every step size. The controller shrank to the floor and
    the run died at t = 0. The floor-sized first step is the t = 0+ solution UIC means, so it
    is now accepted and followed by a restart. This is a behaviour change, but only for runs
    that used to fail: `xtask validate` output, Fig. 4, Fig. 5, the PSP103 inverter and
    c17_tran are bit-identical before and after. One existing test asserted that a model with
    charge non-zero at the origin *cannot* start from rest, and said it was kept to announce
    the day that changed. It now integrates, so the test checks that it starts correctly
    instead: within 2 mV of the closed-form RC curve.

  Measured (i7-1185G7): the access transistor's R_on in place is **377 Ω at W = 10 µm and
  3.7 kΩ at 1 µm**, against the ideal switch's 10 Ω and LRS1's 8 kΩ. So the weight codebook
  shifts. Fig. 5's output spikes stay at steps 6, 10, 14, but V_m departs from the paper's
  equations by 0.131 V (ideal switches: 0.012 V). **Cost: 4.0 s → 64.3 s** for the Fig. 5
  deck (8 threads; 115.5 s on 1). Timepoints grow 1.3×, and the cost per timepoint grows 12×,
  which puts one PSP103 evaluation at about 200 µs. That is the figure to bring down before
  the MNIST network (17 664 access transistors) is attempted at transistor level.
  `cargo test --workspace` 924 passed, 0 failed; `xtask validate` 28/28.

2.2.0 - 2026-09-29
------------------
**MNIST through the memristor-crossbar SNN of Xuan et al. (IJCNN 2026, Sec. IV-B), at circuit
level: ten held-out images, the circuit's class equal to the trained model's on all ten, 99.978 %
of spike slots agreeing, about an hour of wall time per image.** No Rust change.

  * `circuits/snn/mnist/`: `train_mnist.py` trains the paper's 64-64-64-64-10 network (PCA to 64,
    Adam at 1e-3, V_th 2.4 V, beta 0.6, V_reset 1 V, refractory off, 25 timesteps) with the
    **circuit's own neuron cycle for cycle** in plain PyTorch, not snnTorch's, and quantizes the
    three crossbar layers to the 9-value RRAM codebook. Held-out accuracy (the last 10 000 of the
    60 000 training images, never trained on): 93.98 % float, 92.87 % post-training quantized,
    **94.04 %** after 5 epochs of quantization-aware fine-tuning (a departure from the paper's
    plain post-training quantization). The paper reports 97.46 %; not chased (input encoding,
    epochs and surrogate slope are the untested suspects; the encoding is our choice, the paper
    does not give one).
  * `gen_mnist_deck.py` writes one image's circuit: hidden 1's spike trains (software, as in the
    paper) as word lines; three crossbars of 8 832 2T2R cells; 138 neurons, K per layer from lambda.
    10 973 devices, 2 626 unknowns. `check_mnist.py` compares every cycle x neuron spike slot with
    the model; `run_all.sh` runs the ten resumably with retries; `summarize.py` tabulates.
  * **Results** (one held-out image per digit): circuit class = model class **10/10**; = label 8/10,
    the model's own two errors (a 4 read as 9 by both; a 9 tied 12-12 between 4 and 9 in both).
    Spike slots agreeing: **40 011 / 40 020**; eight images agree on every slot. Every mismatch is
    on an image whose model membrane passes within ~2 mV of V_th, measured by recomputing the
    model's V_m (digit 7, closest at 0.69 mV, has 8 of the 9).
  * **Wall time (va-cli alone, i7-1185G7, 8 threads, mains): 35 145 s for the ten (9.76 h), mean
    3 514.5 s, range 3 061.8-3 820.6 s**, mean 63 846 timepoints (~2 200 per 250 ns cycle, set by
    the network's switching activity, not the matrix size); >1.2 GB of memory per run (the
    waveform is held in memory).
  * **Stated limitation: V_m is not bounded by the supply.** Output membranes swing from about
    -6.7 V to +8.7 V: the input stage and attenuator are ideal, and so is the model's linear
    equation, which is why they agree. A 3.3 V circuit would clip at the rails, so this shows the
    circuit computes the trained model's equations, not that a 3.3 V implementation of these
    weights classifies the same. Bounding V_m in circuit and training, then retraining, is the
    follow-up (`circuits/snn/mnist/LOG.md`, Step 11).
  * `models/snn/synapse_2t2r.va`: a 2T2R cell as one device (two cell + access-switch paths, no
    internal node), identical to the two-part form on the Fig. 5 deck at the printed resolution;
    it saves 2 unknowns per synapse (17 664 on this network).
  * The first overnight run was stopped by the host for low memory after 3 images; the cause was
    the checker holding each image's ~0.25 GB of output and a parsed copy in memory. It now streams
    the output from a file, validated identical to the old reader, and the run resumed.
  * `scripts/benchmark/bench_snn_linux.sh /path/to/va-cli [digits]`: runs the samples from a
    checkout with a given executable (the v2.0.0 one suffices), timing va-cli alone per image and
    hashing its output. Not yet run on Linux.
  * `circuits/snn/mnist/LOG.md` records every step, command, figure and dead end in order;
    `docs/examples/snn_mnist/` has the plots and image 0's complete netlist.

Measured at this version: no Rust change, so 2.1.0's figures stand (923 tests; `validate` 28/28);
zoo `check models --codegen` 42/42 (+ synapse_2t2r).
