1.2.5 - 2026-09-22
------------------
**A capacitor pinned by a fast-slewing source could not be integrated at all.** The step
controller rejected every step down to the floor and gave up:

    V1 in gnd PULSE(0 1.2 0p 200p 100p 1n 2n)
    C1 in gnd 1.7f
    R1 in out 10k
    .tran 10p 1n          ->  timestep underflow at t=0

That deck is now `circuits/benchmark/cap_fast_edge.net`, and it contains **no Verilog-A** —
reference capacitor, resistor and waveform source. The bug was never about compact models; it
only looked that way, because a CMOS gate input is exactly this circuit, and it is what stopped
a PSP103 inverter from switching.

  **Cause.** A transient starts from the operating point, which carries no reactive current by
  definition, so on the first step the source's branch current jumps from zero to `C·dV/dt`.
  `divided_difference_error_ratio` error-controls *every* unknown including that row, with a
  formula that assumes smoothness — and a discontinuity's divided difference does not shrink
  with `h`. Shrinking is a remedy for truncation error, not for a discontinuity. Confirmed to be
  the LTE path and not Newton by instrumenting both, and the threshold matched `lte_abstol`
  (1e-6 A) exactly: at 6e9 V/s, `C = 1e-16` passed and `C = 2e-16` failed.

  **Fix.** A row that carries no integrated state — `Branch` by
  `va_core::mna::classify_unknowns`, not dynamic by `classify_dynamic_rows`, in practice a
  voltage source's branch current — loses its veto **at the floor**, the last step size the
  controller would try. An inductor's branch row is also `Branch` and keeps its veto: it carries
  flux, so it is dynamic, and its current is continuous precisely because it is integrated.

  **Two wrong versions came first, and both are the reason the final one looks the way it
  does.** Recording them because the shape of this fix is not guessable from the bug:

  * *Excluding those rows outright* fixed the capacitor and broke other things. Rows without
    integrated state still say something useful about how fast a circuit is moving:
    `motorway_ramp_mpc` underflowed at t = 3600 s where it had run, because the step grew past
    what Newton could take across the metering step change, and `circuits/rectifier.net` lost
    23% of its timepoints (718 -> 551) and 19% of its accuracy against golden (8.269e-4 ->
    1.027e-3, past its own 1e-3 tolerance). A structural rule cannot tell "discontinuous" from
    "merely sharp", and both look identical in the estimate.
  * *Relaxing for one step only* hung. The controller accepts at the floor, grows the step,
    meets the same discontinuity, shrinks back and accepts again — about 1e8 steps for a 1 ns
    window. A hang is worse than the error it replaced. The relaxation therefore **latches**:
    once a run has proved that shrinking is futile, those rows stay out for the rest of it.

  So the only code path that changes is the one that previously returned `TimestepUnderflow`.
  Anything converging at a larger step is untouched, which is what makes the claim below
  possible.

  Measured at this version. `cargo test --workspace` 832 passed / 0 failed (3 new). `cargo xtask
  validate` 28 checked, 0 failed golden, convergence 28/28 — **every figure identical, digit for
  digit**, to the run before this change. Corpus `va-cli check external --codegen` 200/224.
  `circuits/benchmark/cap_fast_edge.net` reaches tstop in 120 timepoints with `V(out)` exactly
  half of `V(in)` on the plateau; the PSP103 inverter runs its five cycles in 1861 timepoints
  and 5.4 s.

  **New: `circuits/benchmark/`** — decks that exercise real vendor compact models, and the decks
  that reproduce what those models found. Deliberately outside `xtask validate`, since most need
  a `.va` from the gitignored `external/`. That is the point of them: every bug in this series
  survived a green 28/28 gate because nothing in the zoo looked like it. Each header says how to
  run it, what it should produce, and exactly how it differs from the external deck it came
  from — `.subckt`, `.param`/`'expr'`, `+` continuation lines and nested `.dc` are all
  unsupported, and no model cards were available, so every one runs on `.va` defaults.

  **A note on the number.** The entry below was published earlier today as `1.3.0` and is
  relabelled **1.2.4** as of 1.2.5+1: the transient operating-point start belongs in this 1.2
  line. Every reference in the source, the docs and `benchmark.md` moved with it. What cannot
  move is the commit subject of 58f1388, which still reads `1.3.0` — rewriting a pushed commit
  would mean a force-push, so the history says 1.3.0 and everything else says 1.2.4.

1.2.4 - 2026-09-22
------------------
**A transient now starts from the DC operating point, as SPICE does, unless the deck says
`UIC`.** It used to start from the zero vector plus each element's `IC=`, unconditionally, with
no way to ask for anything else — and `cargo xtask gen-golden` carried a matching workaround
that forced `UIC` onto every deck it handed QSPICE, because the two had to be made to agree
somehow.

  **This was not a stylistic difference. It made every CMC MOSFET unrunnable in transient.** At
  `x = 0` a compact model's charge is not consistent with any solution, so the first step has to
  move all of it — and halving the timestep makes the required current *larger*, not smaller, so
  the step controller can only shrink until it underflows. BSIM4, BSIM-BULK 107, BSIM-SOI,
  PSP103 and EKV2.6 all failed at `t = 0` on every release before this one, and none of them
  could ever have run. All five integrate now. Verified against v1.1.1 that this is pre-existing
  and not a regression from this week's work.

  Why nothing caught it: the zoo's transient gates are RC, RLC and diode circuits whose zero
  start *is* physically consistent (a capacitor at 0 V holds no charge), and the zoo's own
  MOSFET model carries no charge at all.

  `.tran <tstep> <tstop> [UIC]` is parsed by `va-netlist`; `Netlist::tran_uic` carries it.

  **No goldens were regenerated, and none needed to be.** Flipping the default broke six gates —
  `rc_step`, `rc_discharge`, `rlc_ring`, `rl_decay`, `ring_osc`, `laplace_step` — and those six
  are exactly the decks whose physics is a from-rest or initial-condition response, which from
  an operating point are flat lines. They meant `UIC` all along; their `.tran` cards now say so,
  and they reproduce their committed goldens **identically**, because `gen-golden` had been
  generating those goldens with QSPICE's `UIC` forced on. `rc_step_hv`, `rectifier`,
  `abstime_ramp` and `vsin_load` were unaffected either way: their operating point at `t = 0`
  coincides with the cold start. The same rule applied to four more decks used by unit tests
  (`transformer`, the three `motorway_ramp*`) and two inline test decks.

  `gen-golden`'s workaround is gone with it: `cold_start_tran_deck` now mirrors the deck's own
  `UIC` onto QSPICE instead of forcing it, so the two simulators start from the same place
  because the deck says where, not because a translator patched it.

  Measured at this version. `cargo test --workspace` 830 passed / 0 failed (3 new). `cargo xtask
  validate` 28 checked, 0 failed golden, convergence 28/28 — **every figure identical to
  v1.2.3's run**, which for a change to where every transient begins is the claim worth making.
  Corpus `va-cli check external --codegen` 200/224, 182/191 self-contained.

  **What this does not fix, found while testing it.** A PSP103 CMOS inverter from an OpenVAF
  deck still fails, and the cause is a separate, pre-existing bug in the LTE controller with
  nothing to do with compact models. Minimal reproduction, no Verilog-A at all:

      V1 in gnd PULSE(0 1.2 0p 200p 100p 1n 2n)
      C1 in gnd 1.7f
      R1 in out 10k
      .tran 10p 1n          -> timestep underflow at t=0

  A capacitor whose voltage is pinned by a source slewing fast at `t = 0` gives the source's
  branch current a step discontinuity across the first step — the operating point has no
  reactive current by construction, the first transient point has `C·dV/dt`.
  `divided_difference_error_ratio` estimates the error over **every** unknown, including that
  branch-current row, and a discontinuity's divided difference does not shrink with `h`, so
  every step is rejected down to underflow. Confirmed to be the LTE path and not Newton by
  instrumenting both. The threshold is quantitative and matches exactly: failure begins when
  `C·dV/dt` crosses `lte_abstol` (1e-6 A) — at 6e9 V/s, `C = 1e-16` passes and `C = 2e-16`
  fails. The fix belongs in `va-transient`: error-control the reactive states rather than every
  row, and do not LTE-control a step across a known discontinuity.

1.2.3 - 2026-09-22
------------------
**`@(above(...))` in a `.dc` sweep fired at every point past its threshold instead of once, on
the crossing.** LRM §5.10.2: `above(expr)` triggers when `expr` *becomes* positive. A standalone
operating point has no earlier point to have been non-positive at, so there "already positive"
is the trigger — and `va_core::dc::operating_point_with_events` has taken the previous point's
site values as a `previous` argument since the event channel was built, for exactly the sweep
case. `solve_dc_sweep` never threaded them and passed `None` at every point, so a signal that
went past its threshold at the third point re-fired at the fourth, fifth and every point after.
A latch that never latched.

  Sweeping `V1` 0 -> 1 V through `@(above(V(p,n) - 0.5)) g = 1.0;` over a `g = 1e-3` default:

      V1        before        after
      0.0-0.5   1 mS          1 mS
      0.6       1 S           1 S      <- the crossing
      0.7-1.0   1 S           1 mS     <- did not *become* positive here

  The history is not reset when a warm start fails and the point is retried cold (v1.2.2's
  fallback): which sites crossed is a property of the two operating points, not of the path
  Newton took to the second one.

  **This changes results**, which is the point of it — it is the first change in this series
  that does. Any deck with an `above` in a swept analysis gets different numbers, and they are
  the LRM's numbers. `cargo xtask validate` is 28/28 with all figures unchanged, because no zoo
  deck puts an `above` in a `.dc` sweep — which is also why nothing caught this.

  Two tests, both confirmed to fail against the un-threaded code.
  `above_fires_once_on_the_crossing_of_a_dc_sweep_not_at_every_point_after` is the bug itself;
  the fixture makes the two readings differ by 1000x so nothing turns on a tolerance.
  `above_fires_at_the_first_point_of_a_sweep_that_starts_past_the_threshold` is its companion
  and guards the opposite mistake: the first point of a sweep has no predecessor, so it *is* the
  initialization case and must still fire. Implementing this as "never fire without a previous
  point" would have passed the first test and silently lost the first point's event.

  Measured at this version. `cargo test --workspace` 827 passed / 0 failed (2 new). `cargo xtask
  validate` 28 checked, 0 failed golden, convergence 28/28, every figure identical to v1.2.2's
  run. Corpus `va-cli check external --codegen` 200/224, 182/191 self-contained.

  `benchmark.md`'s open list loses this item. What is still open there: no machine code, the
  prefix-only setup hoist, `build_instances` called three or four times per run, nested `.dc`,
  and the untested cold-start fallback.

1.2.2+1 - 2026-09-22
--------------------
**`LICENSE` names a copyright holder.** The Apache-2.0 text shipped with the placeholder line
from its own appendix, `Copyright [yyyy] [name of copyright owner]`; it now reads
`Copyright [2026] [Philippe Velha]`. Author's edit, committed as written.

  No code, no behaviour, hence a build digit rather than a patch. Nothing was re-measured for
  this entry: the figures that stand are v1.2.2's — 825 tests, `cargo xtask validate` 28/28,
  corpus 200/224.

  One thing left as the author wrote it, worth a look rather than a silent fix: the line sits
  inside the licence's **APPENDIX**, which is the boilerplate template you copy into source
  files, and which says three lines above it to replace the bracketed fields *including the
  brackets*. So the conventional form is `Copyright 2026 Philippe Velha`, and the conventional
  place is a `NOTICE` file or a per-file header, with the appendix left as the template it is.

1.2.2 - 2026-09-22
------------------
**A `.dc` sweep continues from the previous point instead of restarting Newton at the origin.**
Adjacent points of a sweep are the same circuit at a slightly different source value, so the
answer at one point is an excellent guess for the next — a couple of iterations instead of a
dozen from zero. Every point had been solved cold, which is the one thing every other SPICE
does not do.

    circuit                          v1.2.1    v1.2.2
    BSIM4 Id-Vg, 1001 points          3434 ms    626 ms    5.5x
    HICUM/L2v3 output family,         2811 ms   1050 ms    2.7x
      6 x 201 = 1206 points                                (4.1x net of six process launches)

Both best of three: this machine's run-to-run spread on these is 20-40%, and a single
measurement of either would have been worth little.

  New in `va-core`, both additive — the old entry points are one-line delegations and behave
  exactly as before: `newton::solve_with_events_from` and `dc::operating_point_continued` take
  an optional starting point. A `start` whose width is not `dim` is refused rather than padded
  or truncated, since either would silently solve a different problem.

  **On failure the point is retried cold.** Continuation is an accelerator, not a change of
  contract: a guess can in principle be worse than the origin, and a sweep that used to produce
  an answer must not stop producing one because the solver was handed a hint. With the retry the
  set of points that converge can only grow. Stated plainly: **that fallback is not exercised by
  a test**, because constructing a circuit where a warm start fails and a cold start succeeds is
  a research question rather than a fixture. What is tested is that it cannot make things worse
  by construction.

  **It moves no answers.** All 1001 BSIM4 sweep points are identical to the run taken before any
  of this week's work, digit for digit; so are all 1206 HICUM points and all 28 figures in
  `cargo xtask validate`, three of which are `.dc` sweeps. Stronger than the printed digits:
  `a_reused_instance_set_sweeps_exactly_as_per_point_rebuilds_would` compares raw solution
  vectors with `==` against a cold, per-point-rebuilt solve and passes, so on that circuit
  continuation moves not a single bit. That test now covers both sweep optimisations at once.

  `a_starting_point_changes_the_path_not_the_answer` checks a nonlinear circuit from four
  starting points including the origin — and, because that would pass just as well if `start`
  were quietly ignored, also checks that with a **one-iteration budget** a solve starting at the
  answer converges while one starting at the origin fails. That second half is what makes it
  discriminating.

  Measured at this version. `cargo test --workspace` 825 passed / 0 failed (4 new: 2 in
  `va-core`, and the widened `va-cli` pair from v1.2.1). `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked,
  0 failed golden, convergence 28/28, all figures identical to v1.2.1's run. Corpus `va-cli
  check external --codegen` 200/224, 182/191 self-contained.

  **Not done, deliberately.** `solve_dc_sweep` still passes `None` for `AboveValues` at every
  point, so an `@(above)` in a swept deck fires on "already positive" at each point rather than
  on a crossing from the point before — `dc::operating_point_with_events` has the parameter for
  exactly this and it is threaded nowhere. That is an event-semantics change, not a speed one,
  and it deserves its own decision rather than being folded into a performance release. Nested
  `.dc` (two sources, `dc VC 0 2 0.01 VB 0.65 0.9 0.05`) is still unsupported; `DcSweep` names
  one source, and the six inner sweeps have to be run separately.

  Where the sweep time now goes, for whoever picks this up: ~0.6 ms per BSIM4 point, of which
  the model evaluation is ~0.13 ms per Newton iteration. The remaining fixed cost per run is
  that `quantities()`, `branch_currents()` and `sizing()` each call `build_instances`
  independently.

  **New: `benchmark.md`** at the repo root — every figure this week produced, with the
  methodology that makes them checkable and the ways each comparison is *not* like-for-like:
  the per-`load()` table and what each of the three evaluator changes bought on its own, model
  preparation against the published OpenVAF/Xyce/ADS/Spectre compile times (and why that row is
  not a win to quote), the sweep timings version by version, the HICUM output-family card with
  the five ways this run differs from the ngspice deck it came from, and the thermal-node
  termination study. It also carries the open list, worst first.

1.2.1 - 2026-09-22
------------------
**A `.dc` sweep builds its instances once instead of once per point.** `solve_dc_sweep` cloned
the deck at every swept value and called `solve_dc`, which rebuilt every device in it —
including each compiled Verilog-A model. `va_codegen::build_instance` is 7.3 ms for BSIM4
against 10.7 ms for a whole sweep point, so roughly two thirds of a sweep was reconstructing
models that had not changed, and it threw away each model's cached setup (v1.2.0's
`Lowered::static_prefix`) every point, turning once-per-instance work into once-per-point work.

Now the deck is built once and only the swept source is rebuilt in place — through the same
`build_instance` call the original construction used, not a copy of it, with the global unknown
counter rewound to what it held before that device first claimed anything, so the replacement
occupies exactly the rows the original did. `BuiltInstances` gained a `slots: Vec<DeviceSlot>`
recording each phase-1 device's index and its before/after counter values; it is now a named
struct rather than a four-tuple, which is what makes that fifth field readable at its seven
call sites.

  A rebuild that claimed a different number of unknowns would push every later device's rows
  out of alignment and the solver would quietly solve a different circuit. It cannot happen for
  a source — a voltage source's row count does not depend on its value — so the code says so
  with a real error rather than a `debug_assert`, and the message names the device.

  **Measured, 1001-point BSIM4 Id-Vg sweep: 10.7 s -> 3.17 s**, and every one of the 1001 output
  lines is identical to the run taken before any of v1.2.0's work. End to end against where this
  started, the same sweep was **30.0 s** at v1.1.1: 9.5x, output unchanged throughout.

  Nothing is carried between points. The solve starts from the same initial guess it always did,
  and the only mutable state an instance holds is the cached setup, which is bias-independent by
  construction. Two new tests state that rather than trusting it:
  `a_reused_instance_set_sweeps_exactly_as_per_point_rebuilds_would` compares the whole solution
  vector with `==`, not a tolerance, against a per-point rebuild at every point of the diode
  sweep — a sweep that reused a stale operating point would still converge and still look
  plausible, so an approximate comparison is the wrong instrument. It was confirmed to
  discriminate by dropping the in-place replacement and watching it fail at the second point.
  `re_pointing_the_swept_source_does_not_claim_new_unknowns` checks the solution vector never
  widens and that the source is still actually driving its node.

  Measured at this version. `cargo test --workspace` 823 passed / 0 failed (2 new).
  `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed golden, convergence 28/28 — diffed line by line
  against v1.2.0's run, all 28 error figures identical, and that gate contains three `.dc`
  sweeps (`diode_iv`, `diode_clamp`, `diode_iv_params`), which is the part of it this change can
  reach. Corpus `va-cli check external --codegen` 200/224, 182/191 self-contained.

  **Not fixed here, same shape.** `quantities()`, `branch_currents()` and `sizing()` each call
  `build_instances` on their own, so a single `va-cli sim` run still builds the deck three or
  four times before it solves anything — a fixed cost, not a per-point one, but it is three
  wasted `build_instance` calls per compiled model and it is why a one-point `.op` on BSIM4
  still costs ~48 ms of which ~16 ms is the frontend. And `.tran`/`.ac`/`.noise` build once
  already, so they were never affected by this.

1.2.0 - 2026-09-22
------------------
**The evaluator stops paying for work it does not do.** One `ModelInstance::load()` — the
residual + Jacobian evaluation every Newton iteration of every timepoint pays for — is 3.4x to
9.3x cheaper on the CMC standard models, with every golden number bit-identical. Measured
before and after with the same new tool, `cargo xtask bench-model`:

      model            exprs  unkns   stmts   setup    before     after   speedup
      BSIM4            19109     18    1949    1556   1215 us    131 us      9.3x
      PSP103           40807     21    1220     757    987 us    161 us      6.1x
      BSIM-SOI         16301     23    1216     700   1045 us    159 us      6.6x
      BSIM-BULK107     19070     24     932     282   1067 us    212 us      5.0x
      HICUM/L2v3        7408     22     191       1    271 us     61 us      4.4x
      JUNCAP200        12932      3     215     194    200 us     49 us      4.1x
      EKV2.6            2490      4     268      47    278 us     81 us      3.4x

End to end, a 1001-point BSIM4 Id-Vg sweep went from 30.0 s to 10.7 s, output unchanged to the
last digit.

Three changes, in descending order of what they bought:

  * **`Dual`'s gradient knows how to be nothing.** It was two `Vec<f64>` of length `n_unknowns`,
    allocated for every intermediate value — including the 78-87% of them that depend on no
    unknown at all (7816 of BSIM4's 9020 per `load`, measured). A gradient channel is now
    `Grad::Zero` or `Grad::Dense`, and `Zero` holds no allocation and needs no length. Exact,
    not heuristic: a value is independent of the unknowns precisely when both its operands
    were. ~3.1x on its own.
  * **Local variables are indexed, not hashed.** `Ctx::vars` was a `HashMap<u32, Dual>`; BSIM4
    declares 1446 locals and reads them thousands of times per `load`, paying a SipHash for
    each. Now a `Vec<Option<Dual>>` indexed by `VarId`. A further ~1.6x on BSIM4.
  * **A model is split into setup and eval, and the setup runs once.** Most of a compact model
    is parameter checking, temperature scaling, geometry and binning — bias-independent
    statements that produce the same numbers on iteration 300 as on iteration 1. `lower` now
    computes how many leading statements those are (`Lowered::static_prefix`: 1556 of BSIM4's
    1949, 757 of PSP103's 1220); `load` evaluates them once per instance and starts every later
    call past them. A further ~1.6x on BSIM4, ~1.2x on PSP103, and nothing at all on
    HICUM/L2v3, whose setup is not a prefix (1 statement of 191).

  **Why it is a prefix and not "every bias-independent statement":** nothing is reordered. A
  bias-independent statement sitting after a bias-dependent one may read what that statement
  wrote, so hoisting it needs dependence analysis this does not do. That is the whole of why
  HICUM/L2v3 gains nothing here, and it is the obvious next thing to widen.

  **The classification is a whitelist with no wildcard arm**, so a new `Expr` variant or
  `Builtin` fails to compile until someone decides which side of the split it is on. Getting
  that wrong in the permissive direction would freeze a value at its first evaluation, and the
  solve would simply converge to the wrong answer with nothing to see. `$abstime`, `$simparam`
  (gmin moves under gmin stepping), the analysis-context builtins, every analog operator and
  every noise source are all outside it. `$temperature`/`$vt`/`$mfactor` are inside it because
  they read the temperature the *instance* was built at — **if `$temperature` is ever re-sourced
  from `AnalysisCtx`, the cached setup must be keyed on temperature or it will serve one sweep
  point's numbers to the next.** That caveat is written at `lower::static_prefix_len` too.

  **One behaviour did change, and a test caught it.** A setup that *fails* — a `while` whose
  condition never goes false, hitting the iteration cap — used to abort the walk and skip every
  statement after it. Split into a phase of its own, the failure was being swallowed and the
  contribution downstream of the runaway loop started stamping. The setup now records whether
  it completed and `load` skips the eval phase if it did not, restoring the old behaviour
  (`a_runaway_while_loop_is_bounded_by_the_iteration_cap_not_a_hang`).

  **Corpus 199 -> 200/224, and it is a correctness fix rather than a loosening.**
  `L_UTSOI_102_nqs.va` was being refused for "ddt of a value that already depends on a time
  derivative". It does not contain one. `carries_charge` asked "is any partial non-zero" of a
  dense vector of zeros — and multiplying zeros by an infinite coefficient makes them NaN, with
  `NaN != 0.0` true, so a value carrying no charge reported that it did. Verified on the
  pristine code: excluding NaN from that one predicate alone is enough to make the file pass.
  `Grad::Zero` removes the NaN at the source. The *structural* rule that refuses a genuine
  `ddt(ddt(x))` lives in `lower` and still fires, checked with a minimal model and now with
  `an_infinite_coefficient_does_not_fake_a_charge_channel`. This is the same `0 * inf` family as
  v1.1.1 — the third instance, and the reason the new representation is worth more than its
  speed.

  **New: `cargo xtask bench-model [<model.va>...]`.** `bench-scale` measures the solver on an RC
  ladder of reference primitives, which carry no Verilog-A at all; this measures the other half,
  and on a real compact model the other half is three orders of magnitude larger than the solve
  it stamps into. Defaults to the seven models under `external/code`, skipping any that are
  absent (`external/` is gitignored).

  Measured at this version. `cargo test --workspace` 821 passed / 0 failed (7 new: 5 for the
  setup/eval split, 1 for the charge-channel NaN, plus the discrimination check). `cargo xtask
  validate` 28 checked, 0 failed golden, convergence 28/28 — and **diffed line by line against a
  run of the pristine tree: all 28 error figures are bit-identical**, which is the evidence that
  a 9x change to how the evaluator works moved no answer. Corpus `va-cli check external
  --codegen` 200/224, 182/191 self-contained.

  **What this does not do, and what is next.** It is still a tree-walking interpreter; nothing
  is compiled to machine code. The remaining per-`load` cost is recursive `eval` dispatch,
  `Result` plumbing and arena indirection on the bias-dependent core, which a flat SSA tape
  would remove and a Cranelift JIT (pure Rust, so it clears §5's no-native-link rule, but it
  needs `unsafe` to call what it emits and so needs sign-off under §5) would remove further.
  And the larger fish: **`solve_dc_sweep` rebuilds every model instance at every sweep point**.
  `build_instance` is 7.3 ms for BSIM4 against 10.7 ms per sweep point, so most of a DC sweep is
  now rebuilding a model that did not change — and it throws away the cached setup each time.
  That is a `va-cli` change, not a codegen one, and it is the next thing worth doing.

1.1.1 - 2026-09-21
------------------
**Two AD rules returned NaN where the derivative is finite, and it cost us the big CMC
MOSFETs.** Both are `0 * inf` in `va-codegen`'s dual numbers, and both fire at *x = 0* --
which is not an edge case, it is where every Newton solve starts.

  * `Dual::powf` differentiated the base by the logarithmic rule `u^v * (v*u'/u + v'*ln u)`.
    At `u = 0` that is `0 * inf` = NaN, where `d/du u^1.5 = 1.5*sqrt(u) = 0`; at `u < 0` with
    an integer `v` it routes through `ln(u)` = NaN, where `d/du u^2 = 2u` is finite. It now
    uses the closed form `v*u^(v-1)` for the base and forms the `u^v*ln u` term only when the
    exponent actually varies. `pow(x, k)` and `x*x` now give the same derivative, which is the
    invariant that makes the two spellings interchangeable in a model.
  * `Dual::chain` scaled *every* gradient channel by the outer slope, including channels whose
    incoming gradient is exactly `0.0`. At a function's own singularity the slope is `±inf`,
    so a subexpression built only from parameters -- which cannot affect the Jacobian at all --
    came back with an all-NaN gradient that then propagated into rows it never reached. A zero
    channel now stays zero. A channel the operand *does* depend on still reports the true
    singularity: `d/dx sqrt(x)` at 0 is still `+inf`, because it is.

  Why it took this long to surface: the zoo's own models never raise a bias-dependent quantity
  to a power near zero, and `va-abi`'s reference models are hand-written. `cargo xtask
  validate` was 28/28 before the fix and is 28/28 after it -- the gate could not see this. What
  saw it was `external/code`, the CMC standard-model distributions (BSIM, PSP, HICUM, MEXTRAM,
  EKV, ...), added this release as corpus.

  Measured at this version. `cargo test --workspace` 814 passed / 0 failed (4 new AD tests).
  `cargo xtask validate` 28 checked, 0 failed golden, convergence 28/28. Corpus
  `va-cli check external --codegen` 199/224 files declaring a module, 181/191 self-contained --
  up from 113/132 because `external/code` adds 92 module-declaring files, of which 85 pass
  frontend + codegen.

  What the fix bought, on the seven models the OpenVAF paper benchmarks (each a bare `.op`, no
  model card, so the numbers are the defaults' operating point and not a validated I-V):
  BSIM4, PSP103 and BSIM-BULK 107 went from "NaN Jacobian on iteration 1" to a converged
  operating point. EKV2.6, JUNCAP200 and BSIM-SOI already solved. HICUM/L2 v3.0 solves to
  Vb = 0.7 V and fails above it (Newton, 100 iterations, residual 3.6e11) -- that one is the
  unwired convergence aids (junction limiting), not the compiler, and stays open.

  Limitations this does not touch. `pow` with `v < 1` at `u = 0` still returns `±inf`, as it
  must. Model *preparation* is 5-50 ms for these models against OpenVAF's 0.23-6.7 s, but that
  is not a like-for-like win: OpenVAF emits optimised machine code and we build a tree-walking
  AD evaluator, and the bill arrives at evaluation time -- a 1001-point BSIM4 Id-Vg sweep takes
  30 s here, about 30 ms per DC point. Closing that is a separate piece of work.


1.1.0 - 2026-09-17
------------------
**Every `sim` says what it is about to solve, and roughly what that costs**, before solving
(`va_cli::estimate`, `va_cli::sizing`; the user's request, and `docs/validation.md` now carries
the designer-side reading of the dense-LU limit it is calibrated from):

```
[va-cli] circuit: 11 device(s) (8 compiled), 28 unknown(s) (17 net(s) + 11 auxiliary row(s)), ~12601 points (adaptive, 12601 is the card's floor)
[va-cli] estimate: 0.8-39.1 s of solve, 18.8 kB of matrix -- rough, dense LU scaled from bench-scale on an i7-1185G7
```

  The first line is exact: devices (and how many are compiled Verilog-A instances), the matrix
  dimension split into the deck's nets and the auxiliary rows nothing in the deck names -- a
  branch current per voltage source and inductor, a compiled model's internal nodes *per
  instance*, an `idt` accumulator, one row per denominator degree of a `laplace_*` -- and the
  point count: the exact frequency grid for `.ac`/`.noise`, the swept values for `.dc`, one for
  `.op`, and for `.tran` the card's own `tstop/tstep`, labelled as the floor it is (the step
  controller only adds; 1.0-1.5x on this project's decks).

  The second is a **bracket, and the width is the honest part**. Inside the measured range its
  ends are the neighbouring rows of `bench-scale` -- a statement of fact, not a fit -- and beyond
  the last row they are the exponent-2 and exponent-3 scalings dense LU sits between, which is
  what the user asked for. On top of the solve sits a per-instance model-evaluation term
  (0.006-0.2 ms per compiled instance, 0.002-0.04 ms per non-linear reference primitive), because
  the calibration ladder is linear primitives and cannot speak for a compiled model's cost: at
  3 unknowns the first draft of this estimate was **200x low** on `rectifier.net` for exactly
  that reason. The bracket reaches 30x wide on a small circuit, where Newton iteration counts and
  expression size dominate and no matrix dimension predicts them, and it is gated against the
  five measured decks (`the_bracket_contains_every_measured_deck`) so it cannot silently stop
  covering reality. It covers the solve, not the ~77 ms of start-up and model compilation.

  **`xtask bench-scale` now times `.ac` and `.noise` too**, so the frequency-sweep half of the
  estimate is measured rather than assumed to be some ratio of the transient column. One deck
  serves all four analyses (`AC 1` on the source costs the other three nothing). Measured: an AC
  point costs 1.2-1.5x a transient point despite running no Newton loop, because its arithmetic
  is complex; a noise point is that plus the adjoint.

**The dense-LU limit is 2.8x further out than it was on 2026-09-11, on the same machine.** Two
runs agree, so it is not noise: `tran_ms/pt` at 802 unknowns fell 147.3 -> 52.3, at 402
24.9 -> 14.2, while the 12- and 22-unknown rows are unchanged. A speed-up that grows with `dim`
and vanishes at small `dim` is what **fewer Newton iterations per timepoint** looks like (a big
circuit pays a whole O(dim^3) factorization per iteration; a small one is dominated by fixed
overhead), and the plausible cause is 0.9.22's per-nature `abstol` in the transient update test.
**Not bisected -- stated as likely, not verified.** The stated limit moves with it: a
10 000-point transient is interactive to ~400 unknowns, 8.7 minutes at 800, impractical beyond
~1 600 (was ~200 / half an hour / ~1 000). README and `docs/future_development.md` §1 updated
to match; the old table is kept beside the new one in `docs/validation.md` rather than
overwritten.

**`docs/validation.md` gained the designer-side reading** the estimate is the tool for: what a
row in the matrix actually comes from (and what costs none), how many timepoints fit in a
minute / ten minutes / an hour at each size, where this repository's own decks sit (all two
orders of magnitude below the limit -- `motorway_ramp` is the slowest at 5.3 s because of 12 646
timepoints, not because of 28 unknowns), seven design rules, and what sparse would buy (99.5% of
the factorization at 800 unknowns multiplies zeros).

  Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 810 passed / 0 failed
  (+13, all in `va_cli::estimate`); fmt and `clippy -D warnings` clean; `cargo xtask validate`
  28/28, convergence 28/28; zoo `check models --codegen` 35/35; corpus 112/132 (94/99). The new
  stderr lines change no gate: `xtask validate` and `va-harness` read the library entry point and
  stdout, not the banner.

1.0.1 - 2026-09-17
------------------
README rewritten around the executable. It described only the `cargo` path, which after 1.0 is
the *developer's* path: a user's first command should be `./va-cli`, and the download it comes
from should be the first section. Now: what the project is and what 1.0 means, the per-platform
archive table with unpack-and-run for Linux/Windows/macOS (and the macOS quarantine note), the
rectifier as the install check with the numbers to expect, a real `sim` transcript, every
analysis one line each in binary form, the flags (`--report`, `--plot`, `--integration`,
`--model <dir>`), `check`, and what a `refused:` block means and how it differs from a parse
error or a convergence failure.

  Added, because none of it was in the README before: what ships (35 models, 5 discipline
  headers, 49 decks, 29 golden files), the five physical domains with examples, the validation
  section (28/28, the four tolerance rows, the three tiers of oracle, the bring-up ladder, the
  finite-difference gate on AD), a **Limitations, stated** section (dense-LU size limit, every
  refused construct, the `I(<port>)` narrowings, cross-domain plot units, the 112/132 corpus
  figure with the note that the corpus is not shipped, and what Annex C puts out of scope), the
  crate table, a documentation map and the licence. Every command in it was run before it was
  written; the transcript is real output, not reconstructed.

  No code changed. Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 797 passed / 0 failed;
  fmt and `clippy -D warnings` clean; `cargo xtask validate` 28/28, convergence 28/28. The
  1.0.0 archives on GitHub carry the old README, since they were cut from that tag.

1.0.0 - 2026-09-17
------------------
**1.0.** The deliverable is the compiled `va-cli` executable, and the last thing between
`v1.0.0-rc1` and this number was running it on machines that are not CI runners. Done, on
three: Windows, Linux and macOS, each from its own published rc1 archive.

  **The cross-platform record.** `circuits/rectifier.net` with `models/diode.va` under
  `--tran`, from the unpacked archive with nothing from a checkout: **718 points and
  `V(out) = 4.304467 V` at `t = 2.536873e-4 s` on all three platforms** -- digit for digit
  what the source build gives on the development machine, which is the point of the
  criterion (the adaptive timestep sequence is deterministic, so an equal point count means
  the same floating-point environment, not merely a plausible-looking waveform). Machines:
  Windows on the development machine (i7-1185G7, Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26200, the record first
  taken at 0.9.20+1), plus a Linux x86-64 machine and an Apple-silicon macOS machine -- the
  latter two named by their archive's target, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` and
  `aarch64-apple-darwin` -- whose CPU and OS versions were not captured. docs/workflow.md
  asks for the full spec; next time it should be kept.

  **What the record does not cover, said rather than rounded up:** docs/workflow.md's test is
  three decks and only the rectifier was run on Linux and macOS, so `microring_thermal.net`
  and `laplace_step.net` remain verified on Windows only. The rectifier is the load-bearing
  one -- lexer, parser, elaboration, AD lowering, MNA assembly, Newton, the transient
  integrator with LTE control, and the diode model, end to end from a deck -- but two of the
  three numbers in the workflow document have a one-platform record.

  **Found while re-checking those numbers at 1.0.0, and it is about the criterion itself:**
  `microring_thermal.net` gives **2013** points here, not the 2015 docs/workflow.md recorded
  at rc1. Not a regression and not a version drift -- measured, not assumed: the same deck at
  rc1's own commit (`d80414e`, a worktree build) gives 2013 too, in both debug and release
  profiles, and the `Popt(drop)` peak is unmoved at 7.871e-4 W. What differs is the binary:
  the 2015 was read off the **published Windows archive**, which CI builds for
  `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, while every build on this machine targets
  `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` (an MSYS `link` shadows MSVC's linker here, which is also why the
  MSVC side cannot be rebuilt locally to close the loop). Two accepted steps out of ~2000
  land differently -- an LTE accept/reject decision sitting on a threshold, which different
  code generation flips.

  So the point-count criterion is sharp on `rectifier.net` -- 718 from four separately
  compiled binaries (the three CI archives, MSVC/Linux-GNU/Darwin, plus the local GNU source
  build), which is real evidence -- and brittle on `microring_thermal.net`,
  whose step sequence sits near a threshold. docs/workflow.md now says so and puts the
  physical quantity -- the four 787 uW peaks -- first for that deck. A criterion that fails
  for a reason unrelated to correctness teaches whoever runs it to ignore failures.

  **What 1.0 is.** DC operating point and `.dc` sweep, transient with adaptive timestep and
  LTE control, small-signal AC, and noise (output, input-referred and per-device
  attribution), each driven through the real frontend -> codegen -> core pipeline from a
  SPICE-style deck and each gated against committed QSPICE golden: 28/28, convergence 28/28.
  All six bring-up-ladder rungs pass. The full Verilog-A grammar is the target and most of it
  is in -- hierarchy and instantiation, the discipline/nature system including user-defined
  disciplines and derived natures, contributions, `ddt`/`idt`, the analog control-flow set,
  analog functions, `genvar`/`generate`, vector nets and ports, array variables, `ddx`, the
  Laplace and Z filter families, the three noise builtins, analog events (`initial_step`,
  `final_step`, `cross`, `above`, `timer`) and all 20 compiler directives;
  `docs/token-reference.md` is the token-by-token record and `docs/validation.md` the
  registry of every model and deck that reproduces a reference paper or result. Five
  disciplines carry real physics, not just electrical: thermal, mechanical, optical
  (photonic models validated against three papers) and traffic (Bellemans 2002).

  **Known at 1.0, and shipped that way on purpose.** `absdelta`, `last_crossing` and compound
  triggers mixing a step event with a scheduled one are **refused**, with what/why/instead --
  no corpus model needs them. `absdelay` in transient is refused for the same reason (a pure
  delay is not an ODE; it needs a history buffer). `$rdist_*` is refused -- this engine has no
  RNG. `I(<port>)` inside case/loops, and vector ports in `I(<port>)`, are narrower than the
  LRM. The linear solve is dense LU: ~200 unknowns is interactive for a 10 000-point
  transient, ~800 is half an hour, beyond ~1 000 it is impractical (docs/validation.md has
  the table). A cross-domain plot puts W and A on the zero line -- `--report` one unit at a
  time is the answer. There is no `.gitattributes`, so line endings are whatever each
  checkout's `autocrlf` makes them. What comes after 1.0 is `docs/future_development.md`,
  sparse solve first.

  Measured at this version, on the development machine: `cargo test --workspace` 797 passed /
  0 failed / 0 ignored; `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D
  warnings` clean; `cargo xtask validate` 28/28 golden, convergence 28/28; zoo `check models
  --codegen` 35/35; corpus `check external --codegen` 112/132 module-declaring files, 94/99
  self-contained; 21 Quarto tutorials; 40 zoo models, 49 decks, 29 committed golden files.

0.9.22 - 2026-09-15
-------------------
**Traffic as a fourth conservative domain** (Bellemans, De Schutter, De Moor, "Models for
traffic control", 2002, in `references/`; `docs/traffic.md`). `models/traffic.vams`: density
is a potential, vehicle flow a flow, a section's storage a capacitor, Payne's speed an ODE
state on a bookkeeping node, queues unit stores. Models `section.va` (eqs. 4-9: conservation,
convection/relaxation/anticipation, merging; bare eq. 6 coupling by default, the Godunov
supply as an option), `origin.va` (METANET origin queue with a metering rate on a control
voltage), `boundary.va`, `tts_monitor.va`, `fundamental_diagram.va`, `alinea.va` (feedback
ramp metering, cubic anti-windup). Decks: `fundamental_diagram.net` (exact against eq. 5;
peak at C_cr = 33.5, 4038 veh/h), `motorway_ramp.net` (the paper's Section 5 example: its
Fig. 12's shape -- plateau 48 vs 47 veh/km/lane, ~37 km/h, ramp queue empty, mainline queue
drains at 3.1 vs 2.5 h; TTS 917.6 vs 923.0 for the same equations at the paper's own 10 s
step, 0.6 %; the paper's 1267 depends on constants it does not print), `motorway_ramp_alinea`
(-1 %), `motorway_ramp_mpc` (generated by `docs/examples/traffic_mpc.py`: the optimiser on the
paper's discrete model, the schedule replayed into the plant: -10.1 % vs the model's own
-10.0 %). Three figures in docs/examples (`traffic_figures.py`).

  **MPC evaluated carefully:** with the road at capacity the total time spent is invariant to
  any control (every schedule within 0.1 % under the Godunov coupling -- the departure curve
  is fixed); the only lever is the capacity drop at breakdown; under the paper's 100-vehicle
  ramp limit no schedule pays in this parameterisation; the paper's 8-minute receding horizon
  never closes the meter (the payback is an hour away; a 30-minute one neither); and the gain
  ranges from 0 to -85 % across plausible values of tau and nu, which the paper does not
  print. Stated in the models and the document; the deck ships the pair that reproduces the
  figure's shape.

  **`va-transient` (T4), both proven no-ops on every golden gate by diffing the error
  figures:** the transient Newton's update test honours per-nature `abstol` (it was a flat
  1e-12, a voltage-shaped number, and stalled at a 1e-9 residual on flows of thousands of
  veh/h -- `circuits/motorway_ramp.net` measured it); a step Newton cannot converge on is
  halved and retried like an LTE rejection instead of ending the run (the ALINEA deck failed
  at the instant the meter closed). **`va-netlist` (T6):** `PWL(t1 v1 t2 v2 ...)` sources
  (`Waveform` is no longer `Copy`). Built and removed, and said so: a `.tran ... op` start
  and a gmin/damping fallback for the DC solve -- the empty road's Jacobian is structurally
  singular at the zero vector and the fallback still diverged, so nothing shipped that no deck
  could show working. Also found in the model: an empty section next to a dense boundary is a
  near-discontinuity at t = 0 (timestep underflow); the boundary ramps in over 600 s.

  Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 797 passed / 0 failed (+4); clippy/fmt clean; `cargo
  xtask validate` 28/28, every gate's error identical to 0.9.21+2's (diffed); zoo `check
  models --codegen` 35/35 (+6); corpus 112/132 (94/99) unchanged; docs/examples.md 17 plots
  (+3); `validation.md` gained its Traffic section.


0.9.21+2 - 2026-09-14
---------------------
Docs only: the paper's Fig. 3 reproduced. `docs/examples/fiber_mzi_wavelengths.py` runs
`fiber_mzi_noise.net` through va-cli at 633, 1310 and 1550 nm (dn/dT from Bartolo's eq. A1,
w0 from Table II; 633 nm flagged as multimode in this fiber, LP01 Marcuse estimate used) and
plots the input-referred phase noise with the closed forms overlaid (within 1 % everywhere)
and the wavelength differences against the 1/lambda^2 scaling: 1310 - 1550 nm = +1.6 dB at
low frequency (paper: 1.4 + ~0.2 dB), growing above 10 kHz as the smaller mode radius moves
the k_max corner. Figure in docs/examples.md (#12b), docs/photonic-noise.md, validation.md.


0.9.21+1 - 2026-09-14
---------------------
Docs only: `validation.md` at the repository root is the **reference validation registry** —
every contribution that reproduces a reference paper, textbook circuit or QSPICE result, by
domain (photonics, optics, thermal, mechanical, electrical, and the multi-domain couplings),
each row naming the reference, the equation, the model/deck, and what it was measured against
(QSPICE golden / closed form / paper figure) with the residual. Seeded with the three-paper
photonic table from 0.9.21 and the existing zoo; `docs/validation.md` keeps the mechanics
(metrics, tolerances, the golden set) and links to it, as does the README. No code touched.


0.9.21 - 2026-09-14
-------------------
**Photonic noise from three papers** (`references/`: Glenn 1989, Bartolo/Tveten/Dandridge
2012, Scheuer 2016; `docs/photonic-noise.md` maps every equation to its model line). Four new
models and two changed ones: `waveguide.va` carries Wanser's thermorefractive phase-noise PSD
as a 73-point `noise_table_log` (8/decade, 1 Hz-1 GHz, each point the closed form from the
instance's own parameters, log-log interpolation within 0.7 % of the formula) plus Duan's
thermomechanical `1/f` as a `flicker_noise`, both on a new `optical_phase` signal-flow net in
`photonic.vams` (nature `Optical_phase`, access `Phi`, rad); `mzi.va` recombines two arms
(its Jacobian is Bartolo's eq. B2); `splitter.va`; `ring_gyro.va` is an all-pass ring with
Scheuer's Sagnac phase (eq. 13) driven by a rotation-rate control node; `cw_laser.va` gains a
`rin` parameter (linear 1/Hz, default 0 = unchanged) and an optional trailing phase port;
`photodiode.va` gains shot noise `2q(|I_j| + resp*P)`. Two decks, both `.noise` with the paper's
figure of merit as the **input-referred** column: `fiber_mzi_noise.net` (Bartolo's 40 m + 40 m
MZI at 1319 nm: -100.6 dB re rad/rtHz at 1 Hz (Duan), -124.9 at 1 kHz against the paper's
-125.5 flat region, -137.4 at 100 kHz; shot floor -154.5; the laser's RIN **exactly zero** at
quadrature, cos^2(3 deg) of it 3 deg off) and `ring_gyro_noise.net` (Scheuer's RWOG: eq. 15's
three terms per device, 69 % shot / 15 % Johnson / 17 % RIN, `Omega_min = sqrt(S_in) = 0.0137
rad/s/rtHz` for a 1 mm ring). No crate learned anything photonic.

  **Two silent zeros the models exposed, both fixed (docs/interfaces.md, revision 2026-09-14;
  no signature change).** (1) A noise function in a *potential* contribution was emitted as a
  current across `(p, n)` -- into a node the contribution pins, adjoint zero -- so it was
  listed as a contributor and delivered `0.0` (measured: a 1e-12 V^2/Hz series source into a
  1k/1k divider reported `X1 0.0 (0.0%)` against a true 2.5e-13). It now sits on the branch's
  constraint row; the divider reads `2.500083e-13 = 0.25e-12 + 4kT*500` exactly. Every
  signal-flow net only ever receives potential contributions, so this was every photonic noise
  source. (2) `noise_table` powers were const-folded at elaboration, so a deck's `L=40` -- or
  `R2 ... 3000` on the tabulated resistor -- silently kept the default's PSD. Powers are now
  lowered as constant *expressions* (parameters, literals, builtins; a probe or `$temperature`
  is still refused) and evaluated per instance; frequencies stay folded. `va-codegen`'s
  `validate` re-checks a power's sign with the overrides applied. `resistor_noise_table.va`
  note 2 and its deck's header no longer carry the "both resistors must be 1k" warning (the
  deck keeps 1k/1k so its golden is unchanged; 1k/3k is a va-cli test).

  **Also found, in the model:** a Sagnac phase of 1e-10 rad added to a 2180 rad round-trip
  phase is below double precision -- a `.dc` sweep of the rotation rate was exactly flat --
  so `ring_gyro.va` reduces the static phase modulo 2*pi first. `.noise` (linearized) was
  never affected. And the deck's ring length is written to 12 digits: an 8-digit rounding
  moved the resonance by 0.08 pm against a 0.53 pm detuning and the noise by 1.5 %.

  **Limitations, stated in the models:** Glenn's acoustic (GHz) density process is not
  modelled (no Bessel builtin; three decades above the band); no laser phase noise, because
  the phase net has no transport delay and it would cancel exactly in any interferometer
  (a silent zero, so it is absent and says so); the PSD's 1/lambda^2 uses `lambda0`, not the
  live wavelength net (a table power must be constant); Duan's term with Table II's printed
  values gives -120.6 dB at 100 Hz where the paper quotes -124.4 (not recovered, not hidden).

  **Not gated against QSPICE** (no phase net, interferometer or ring to translate). Both decks
  are pinned to closed forms in va-cli's tests -- the transcription to the paper's quoted
  -125.5 dB; the MZI's fiber share to 2*(Wanser + Duan) within 1 % at all 51 frequencies,
  each detector's shot noise to 1e-6 (after the test's own "expected" learned about the arm's
  0.008 dB of loss), the RIN to < 1e-40; the gyro's `S_V` to the three-term budget from the
  solved operating point to 1e-6, `S_in` to `S_V/H^2` with `H` from two extra DC solves.
  Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 793 passed / 0 failed (+7 over 0.9.20+1's 786); clippy/fmt
  clean; `cargo xtask validate` 28/28; zoo `check models --codegen` 29/29 (+4); corpus 112/132
  (94/99) unchanged; docs/examples.md 13 plots (+2).

0.9.20 - 2026-09-12
-------------------
`zi_nd`/`zi_np`/`zi_zd`/`zi_zp` **implemented** (Road to 1.0 B.4 closed; LRM 4.5.12,
docs/proposals/z-domain-filters.md). Interface alpha: four additive `Builtin`s, layout
`[value, T, tt, t0, Const(num_len), num..., den...]` (docs/interfaces.md revision 2026-09-12b);
Interface beta untouched. Three analyses, none a fold: DC and noise evaluate `H(1)`; AC
evaluates `H(e^{jωT})` per point on the same complex-gain stamp `laplace_*` uses (no
zero-order-hold factor, stated); transient runs the difference equation on the state channel
-- a `StatefulKind::Zi` block per call site holding the sample clock, the held/previous
outputs and the input/output histories -- sampling the input only at `t0 + k*T`, which the
instance asks the integrator to land on through `EventSink::breakpoint` (va-transient now
polls every instance for breakpoints, not only those with event sites). The output ramps from
the previous sample to the new one over the transition time: omitted means the module's
`default_transition`, zero means the negligible `tstep/1000` that `transition` uses. At the
sampling evaluation the ramp has not started, so the output never depends on the current
Newton iterate and its Jacobian contribution is exactly zero -- not an approximation.
Roots follow the LRM (`(1 - r z^-1)`, origin = the factor `z`, hence a pole at the origin is
one sample of delay and an uncancelled zero at the origin is refused as an advance); refused
at build: a lone complex root, `d_0 = 0`, `T <= 0`, `tt < 0`.

  **Gates, closed-form (QSPICE has no LRM Z-filter element):** a unity filter on a 1 V/us
  ramp is the staircase `floor(t/T)` exactly, breakpoints landing on every instant; the
  one-pole IIR `y_k = a y_{k-1} + (1-a) x_k` on a unit step holds `1 - a^(k+1)` after the
  k-th sample to 1e-9; a conjugate pole pair (`zi_np`) against the recurrence
  `y_k = x_k + y_{k-1} - 0.5 y_{k-2}` iterated in the test (overshoot to 2.5 where the old
  fold gave a flat 2.0); AC against `(1-a)/(1 - a e^{-jωT})` across a decade and a half to
  1e-9 in both parts; DC `H(1)`; the realization's root conventions and refusals. Frontend:
  the lowered layout, `default_transition` substitution, argument-count and pair errors.

  **Stated:** the run's t=0 point is the cold-start seed, where a DC source reads 0, so a
  filter with `t0 = 0` samples that; set `t0` inside the first period to sample after the
  sources are up (the test does). The `,,` null zero-argument is not parsed; write the
  no-zeros case in `zi_nd`/`zi_np` form.

  Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 786 passed / 0 failed (+3); clippy/fmt clean;
  `cargo xtask validate` 28/28; corpus 112/132 (94/99) unchanged.


0.9.19 - 2026-09-12
-------------------
`line`, and every diagnostic with it (Road to 1.0 B.5 closed). The preprocessor now keeps a
**line map**: for each line of expanded text, the file it was read from and its 1-based line
there, through `include` nesting (each open file counts its own lines; the includer resumes
where it left off) and `line number "file" level` overrides (IEEE 1364-2005 section 19.7: the
*next* line is `number` of `file`; `level` accepted and unused). `parse_unit` takes the map and
a parse error reads `at bad.va:5:30 (...)` -- the original file and line -- instead of
"at preprocessed line 27", which counted lines of text the user never saw. `va-cli` passes each
model's own path (`preprocess_named`/`compile_named`), so the file in the message is the one on
disk. Without a map the old wording is kept, and says which numbering it is.

  Gates: the map through an include and a `line` override (line-by-line, blank lines from a
  file's trailing newline included since the map must stay parallel to the text); a parse error
  naming `m.va:4` with three include lines ahead of it in the expanded text, and "preprocessed
  line 7" for the same error without the map. The directives proposal's "no Interface beta
  change" line corrected: `AnalysisCtx::tstep` (0.9.18) is one.

  Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 783 passed / 0 failed (+2); clippy/fmt clean;
  `cargo xtask validate` 28/28.


0.9.18 - 2026-09-12
-------------------
Compiler directives do what the LRM says, or say why they cannot (Road to 1.0 B.5, all but
`line`). `va-frontend`: `preprocess_full` returns the expanded text plus `DirectiveEvent`s at
the offsets they apply from and one warning per no-effect directive; each `ModuleAst` carries
the `Settings` in force at its `module` keyword (LRM scoping: none of these directives may
appear inside a module). Implemented: `default_discipline` (an undeclared port takes it; bare
form turns it off; a digital qualifier is an error naming Annex C), `default_transition`
(omitted or zero rise/fall -> the directive's value, lowered as `rise > 0 ? rise : default`),
`begin_keywords`/`end_keywords` (the four LRM specifiers; the lexer un-reserves by region,
dedicated token variants included; nesting; unmatched/unterminated are errors),
`resetall`. Recognised and reported once per file: `timescale`, `celldefine`/`endcelldefine`,
`default_nettype`, `unconnected_drive`/`nounconnected_drive` (each warning says why it has no
Verilog-A meaning). `pragma` ignored per 1364 section 19.9 except `pragma protect`, a
Refusal. `default_nodetype` (non-LRM) is an error naming `default_discipline`. The five
directives that failed as "undefined macro" no longer do. `line` is still consumed with no
effect, pending the line map (proposal section 2.1 item 5) -- stated in token-reference.md.

  **Found on the way, and fixed (behaviour change, hence the patch bump):** LRM 4.5.8 says a
  `transition()` with no rise/fall time and no directive ramps over "a negligible, but
  non-zero, transition time", precisely because "forcing a zero-duration transition ... could
  cause convergence problems". This engine forced one (rise `0.0` -> `rate = inf`), and a
  `transition` of a threshold comparison underflowed the timestep at the crossing -- found by
  the directive test's own control case. Now: `AnalysisCtx::tstep` (Interface beta, additive,
  docs/interfaces.md revision) carries the deck's `.tran` step, and `va-codegen` ramps a zero
  span over `tstep/1000`, which `bound_step` resolves at any time scale. A model that writes
  `transition(x, 0, 0)` gets the same negligible ramp, as the LRM says it must.

  **Learned:** under `"1364-2005"` the whole AMS vocabulary is unreserved -- `analog` and
  `electrical` too -- so a keyword region can hold only a structural module; the LRM's own
  `sin`-as-a-port example is exactly that. The test wraps an analog module with one, using
  `default_discipline` for what `electrical` cannot say there. Also removed a literal
  `''' + M + '''` that an earlier scripted edit had left in a `va-frontend` comment.

  Gates: 12 new tests -- preprocessor events/settings replay/nesting/resetall, literal
  parsing and its errors, the qualifier error, warnings once each, the protect refusal and
  the three errors; lexer regions un-reserving `sin`/`analog`/`electrical`; end to end,
  `default_discipline` making a port-undeclared module elaborate (and fail without it),
  `default_transition` filling an omitted and a zero rise time, the structural wrapper with
  `sin` as a port, warnings surfacing in `CompiledDesign`; and in `va-cli` the transient ramp
  itself: 100 ns under the directive, negligible without it, neither underflowing.

  Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 781 passed / 0 failed (+12); clippy/fmt clean;
  `cargo xtask validate` 28/28; corpus 112/132 (94/99) and zoo 25/25 unchanged.


0.9.17+1 - 2026-09-12
---------------------
Two new 1.0 blockers, planned in writing (process only, no code). The supervisor's review of
0.9.17's audit: refusing `zi_*` is not the answer before 1.0, and a preprocessor that
"consumes with no effect" is not acceptable. Both plans were written against the LRM text
(`pdftotext` on references/VAMS-LRM-2-4.pdf), not from memory.

  * docs/proposals/directives.md. Clause 10 lists 20 directives and Annex C.12 makes all of
    them Verilog-A's. Verdict per directive: `default_discipline` (10.2) and
    `default_transition` (10.3) change what an analog block computes and are implemented
    (`transition()`'s omitted rise/fall is `0.0` today; the LRM says it is the directive's
    value); `begin_keywords`/`end_keywords` (10.6, "must also be supported") select the
    reserved-word set; `line` fixes the "preprocessed line N" wart in every diagnostic;
    `resetall` resets them. `timescale`, `celldefine`, `default_nettype`,
    `unconnected_drive` have no analog meaning by the LRM's own definitions and are
    recognised and reported, never dropped; `pragma protect` envelopes become a Refusal
    (encrypted IP); `default_nodetype` is not an LRM directive (Annex F change table:
    obsolete AMS-1.x) and becomes an error naming `default_discipline`. **Found while
    checking: five LRM directives are not recognised at all** and fail as "undefined macro"
    (`celldefine`/`endcelldefine`, `default_nettype`, `unconnected_drive`/`nounconnected_
    drive`) -- 0.9.17's audit compared the document against the preprocessor's own list, so
    it could not see what neither had. Zero corpus uses of any of them; planned against the
    standard.
  * docs/proposals/z-domain-filters.md. LRM 4.5.12 semantics stated; DC `H(1)`, AC
    `H(e^{jωT})` on the existing complex-gain stamp, transient as a sampled difference
    equation on the state channel (the `transition`/`slew` mechanism, not the `laplace_*`
    state-space one: a sampled system is not an ODE) with breakpoints at every sample
    instant, the LRM transition ramp, and a Jacobian that is exact because the sampled input
    is committed state everywhere but the sampling evaluation itself. Four additive
    `va_ir::Builtin`s -- an Interface α change, and this document is CLAUDE.md section 6's
    step 1. Seven gates named, closed-form first. Depends on `default_transition`.

  Road to 1.0 gains B.4 and B.5 with both; token-reference.md's `Directive` entry now calls
  the "consumed" group a stated gap and names the unrecognised five.

0.9.17 - 2026-09-12
-------------------
Pre-1.0 audit of docs/token-reference.md against the code, both directions, by script: the
lexer's 186 reserved words vs the master table (186/186, no gaps either way), the 64 `Token`
variants vs Part 1 (all present), the 48 `va_ir::Builtin`s (all present), every `$` system
function the elaborator matches (all have entries), and the preprocessor's directive `match`
vs the `Directive` entry -- **9 of 14 handled directives were unlisted** (`ifndef`, `elsif`,
`undef`, `resetall`, `timescale`, `begin_keywords`/`end_keywords`, `line`, `pragma`); the
entry now enumerates all of them in three groups (expanded / evaluated / consumed with no
effect). Then every limitation claim in the document against what the code does.

  **One real gap, fixed (the behaviour change that makes this a patch, not a +N):** the
  four Z-domain filters `zi_nd`/`zi_np`/`zi_zd`/`zi_zp` were folded to their z=1 gain at
  elaboration -- exact at DC, a plausible wrong waveform in AC and transient -- and sat in no
  refusal list, while `va-cli`'s transient-approximation table said "elaboration rejects it
  outright". They are now **refused at elaboration** as a `Refusal` (what: the call; why: no
  sampling clock, and the fold was the silent-approximation shape CLAUDE.md section 5 names;
  instead: a `laplace_*` form; tracking: future_development section 8). Zero corpus uses, so
  the corpus is unchanged; five frontend tests that pinned the fold's arithmetic are replaced
  by one that pins the refusal, and the dead `z_root_product_at_one` helper is gone.

  **Stale claims corrected in the document:** section 1.5 `Transition`/`Slew` and `Absdelay`
  opened with "v0 is DC-only ... folds transparently to its value" (the state-channel
  implementation and the AC-exact/transient-refused status were appended at the end, and the
  `Absdelay` entry ended mid-sentence) -- both rewritten with current behaviour first and the
  history last; `$mfactor`'s entry said "implemented v0.9.11" and "folds to 1.0 since v0 has no
  netlist-driven instance parameters" in the same sentence; `$strobe` "under v0's DC-only
  model"; the variable-initializer note called `@(initial_step)` a "DC-only approximation" (it
  is a real first-step event); the `units` nature attribute was "not yet consulted by
  anything" (va-cli prints every quantity in its discipline's units); and the `zi_*` rows had
  inherited the `laplace_*` rows' "an ODE on state unknowns" evaluation text from 0.9.16's
  edit. Everything else checked holds: `limexp` folds to `exp` (row and code agree), the
  `noise_table` file form is refused with its own message, genvar instance arrays are stated
  as unsupported and are.

  Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 769 passed / 0 failed (773 - 5 fold tests + 1 refusal
  test); clippy/fmt clean; corpus 112/132 (94/99) unchanged; `cargo xtask validate` 28/28.


0.9.16+1 - 2026-09-11
---------------------
The dense-LU circuit-size limit, measured and stated (Road to 1.0, B.2). New
`cargo xtask bench-scale [--max-nodes N]`: a whole `.op` and a whole `.tran` through `va_cli`
on an RC ladder of n sections (dim = n + 1), reference primitives only, release profile, on the
development machine (i7-1185G7 @ 3.0 GHz, single-threaded):

    n_nodes    dim      op_ms     tran_ms  points   tran_ms/pt
         10     12       0.02         1.4     120        0.011
         20     22       0.06         2.1     120        0.017
         50     52       2.61       115.9     120        0.966
        100    102       1.20       252.6     120        2.105
        200    202       3.05       745.8     120        6.215
        400    402      17.48      2989.5     120       24.912
        800    802      97.46     17675.5     120      147.296

  Points are the same at every size, so `tran_ms/pt` is the solve's growth with dim: 4.0x from
  200 to 400 nodes, 5.9x from 400 to 800 -- dense LU's O(dim^3). **Stated limit, for a
  10 000-point transient: ~200 unknowns is interactive (1 min), ~400 is 4 min, ~800 is 25 min,
  beyond ~1 000 unknowns dense LU is impractical.** A `.op` stays under 0.1 s to 800 nodes; the
  wall is transient, where the factorization is paid per point. Every auxiliary row (branch
  currents, `idt` accumulators, `laplace_*` states) counts as an unknown. Written up with the
  table in docs/validation.md ("The dense-LU circuit-size limit"); the debug-profile numbers
  are 10-60x worse and are not the ones to plan with. This closes the "state the limit" half of
  the sparse decision; building sparse is docs/future_development.md section 1.

  No simulator code touched: xtask gained a subcommand, and the usage banner lists it.


0.9.16 - 2026-09-11
-------------------
`laplace_nd`/`laplace_np`/`laplace_zd`/`laplace_zp` **run in transient.** Since 0.9.0 a `.tran`
of any model calling one was refused ("folds to its DC gain"). A rational filter of denominator
degree `m` is an `m`-th order linear ODE, and the integrator already integrates ODEs through the
charge channel, so each filter now claims `m` auxiliary state unknowns (controllable canonical
form, scaled by `ω0 = (|d_0|/|d_m|)^(1/m)` so a chain of derivative states stays O(1)), stamps
one `ddt`-shaped row per state after the statement walk -- the `idt` accumulator's own
mechanism -- and its output is a linear combination of the states plus a feedthrough of the
input when `deg N = deg D`. DC uses the same rows and gets `H(0)` by construction; AC keeps the
exact `H(jω)` path unchanged; noise keeps its stated `H(0)` limitation. `va-codegen` only, no
Interface change (`lower::LaplaceStates`). Refused at build with what to write instead: a pole
at the origin (`idt`), an improper numerator (`ddt`), roots not in conjugate pairs.

  **Gate:** new `circuits/laplace_step.net` + `golden/laplace_step.golden` -- the
  `laplace_lowpass` model (a `1/(1+sτ)` transfer *and* an `sC/(1+sτ)` admittance, the second
  being the feedthrough case) stepped from cold against the R-C network it emulates in QSPICE,
  cold-started: **5.143e-6 RMS** (tol 1e-3). Closed forms: first-order step 6e-7; a
  `laplace_np` conjugate pair vs the underdamped second-order step 1.25e-4 across 3015 points,
  the 1.73 overshoot included (the fold gave 1.0). Whole-system finite-difference Jacobian
  gates for coefficient form, pole-array form and a pinned trailing state, in transient and DC.

  **Found on the way:** xtask's behavioural-translation table cold-started no entry (right for
  a `B` source reading `time`, wrong for an R-C); it now says per entry, and the first golden
  generated without it showed the capacitor already charged at t=0 -- caught by looking at the
  numbers, not the exit code. `{}` as an empty zero list does not parse (future_development
  §8). Regenerating all goldens: byte-identical modulo CRLF.

  **Also:** `docs/future_development.md`, the post-1.0 list (sparse solve, per-unit plots,
  absdelay stage 2, optimization/fitting, Monte Carlo, multi-core and the GPU constraint, missing
  analyses, language long tail, netlist usability, distribution), each entry with what it gives,
  what it costs, what it depends on and what to decide first. Road to 1.0 B.1 is closed for
  `laplace_*`; `absdelay` stage 2 moves to the future list as a post-1.0 item.

  Measured: `cargo test --workspace` 773 passed / 0 failed (+5); `cargo xtask validate` 28
  checked, 0 failed, convergence 28/28 (+1 gate); zoo `check models --codegen` 25/25; corpus
  112/132 unchanged (the `[approx]` tally no longer counts `laplace_*`, which affects the
  soundness note, not the pass count).

0.9.15+3 - 2026-09-11

Second CI run (0.9.15+2, run 34625634614): clippy and build green on all three platforms;

`xtask::tests::find_qspice_finds_the_real_install_on_this_machine`, which asserted
`find_qspice().is_some()`. That is a statement about the machine, not the code: true on the
one dev box with QSPICE installed, false on every runner and every other computer, which is
exactly the class of assumption the platform gate exists to expose. `find_qspice` now
delegates to `find_qspice_with(env, path, standard)`, and the test checks the search order
(QSPICE_PATH if it names a file, else `QSPICE64.exe` on PATH, else the standard location,
else `None`) against files it creates itself; whether this machine has QSPICE is printed, not
asserted. The rule: a test may check what the code does with what it is given, never what is
installed. CI's test step gained `--no-fail-fast` so one run reports every crate's verdict.

0.9.15+2 - 2026-09-11

The first CI run (0.9.15+1, run 34624556434) was red on all three platforms at the clippy
step, and the cause is the first cross-platform finding of the road to 1.0: the `va-cli` test
target did `include_str!("../../../external/verilogaLib-master/ohmmeter.va")`, and `external/`
is gitignored. On a fresh checkout the suite does not fail -- it does not *compile*. It had
only ever passed on the one machine that has the corpus on disk. The test now carries the
idiom it exists for (a bare `I(iprobe)` of a node whose only branch is an explicit
`V(dutm, iprobe) <+ 0`) inline, with includes resolved from the tracked `models/`. The rule it
establishes: nothing under `external/` may be a compile-time or run-time dependency of the
suite. (The CI job logs need repository-admin auth to download, so the failure was reproduced
by cloning the repository into a scratch directory without `external/` and running the gate
there -- clippy clean, 768 tests, validate 27/27, on the GNU toolchain.)

0.9.15 - 2026-09-11
-------------------
A four-discipline example, built to find what breaks, and the three simulator fixes it forced.
`circuits/microring_thermal.net`: a 1 mW CW laser at 1550.5 nm into a thermally tuned add-drop
microring (L = 188.72 um, resonant at 1550.0 nm cold, n_eff 2.85 / n_g 4.1, FSR 3.105 nm),
heated by a 2 kOhm resistor with a 12 us thermal plant driven 0->5->0 V in 200 us, read out by
a reverse-biased photodiode into 200 kOhm. Four new models (`cw_laser`, `heater`, `microring`,
`photodiode`) and `models/photonic.vams`, which adds `thermal` (conservative) plus two
**potential-only signal-flow** disciplines, `optical` (power, W) and `wavelength` (m) -- the
first signal-flow disciplines in the zoo, and they elaborated and solved unchanged. Every
analytic figure is reproduced: the resonance crosses the laser at a 7.11 K rise, the drop port
peaks at 787 uW, the photodiode clips at 27.6 uA (5 V through 200 kOhm cannot source 0.71 mA
-- the deck as specified, and stated as such), and the heater's lag puts the up-crossing at
4.32 V and the down-crossing at 3.20 V. `microring_thermal_fast.net` (10 us ramps) never
reaches resonance: max 5.48 K. Plots in docs/examples.md 8-11; findings in the roadmap.

  **Fix 1 (behaviour): the transient refusal is scoped to the models a deck places.**
  `--model models/` compiles the whole zoo, and the absdelay/laplace refusal scanned every file
  in it, so this circuit was refused for `delay_line.va` and `laplace_lowpass.va` sitting in the
  same directory. `files_reached_by` now walks the deck's device lines through each file's
  declared and instantiated modules (transitively) and the check covers those files only.

  **Fix 2 (behaviour): `CoreError::NonFinite`.** The ring divides by the wavelength probe, which
  reads 0 on Newton's first iteration; the NaN went through LU and came out as "singular matrix"
  -- the diagnostic for a floating node, the wrong hunt. `va-core::check_finite` now runs before
  either factorization and reports the row (and column, for a Jacobian entry) with the likely
  cause and the model-side guard; `va-cli` names the unknown (`X3.b0`). The model got the guard.
  Found on the way: `newton.rs`'s gmin test claimed a non-finite Jacobian entry; it is finite
  and the factorization overflows, so it stays `Singular` and the comment now says so.

  **Fix 3 (behaviour): report format.** A potential below 1e-3 in magnitude prints in
  scientific notation (`Wl(wl) = 1.550500e-6 m`, not `0.000002 m`), the rule flows already had.
  Volt-scale lines are byte-identical.

  **Open:** a cross-domain plot on one axis puts the W and A series on the zero line;
  `--report` subsets are the documented answer and the example uses them. A per-unit-axis plot
  is wanted, not built, not a 1.0 blocker.

  Measured at this version: `cargo test --workspace` 768 passed / 0 failed (+4); `cargo xtask
  validate` 27 checked, 0 failed, convergence 27/27; zoo `check models --codegen` 25/25;
  corpus 112/132 (94/99 self-contained), unchanged. The slow deck runs in 5.7 s (2013 points).
